# Independent hostile textual audit of the affine-height universality candidate

Status: **NO GAP FOUND AT THE DECLARED STRUCTURAL SCOPE — INDEPENDENT UNVERIFIED AUDIT.**

This is not Danus verifier acceptance, not a fact submission, and not a proof of the original Zhang–Jiang profile-minimax target. That target remains OPEN. One nonblocking factor-of-two wording issue is recorded below. The source was not edited.

## 1. Object, allocation, and exact scope

- Project: `gd-exact-profile-20260926`.
- Native audit allocation: `/root/audit_affine_height_universality`, one invocation, one complete candidate, no subdelegation.
- Allocation-specified model/effort: `gpt-6-astra` / `ultra`. The dispatch record, rather than this mathematical report, is the authority for backend execution metadata.
- Frozen source: `DRAFT_UNVERIFIED.md`, 11,382 bytes.
- Source SHA256: `0cdf678b000d165b1a352fc16d0dd726763e5553944f79d9f9da62fdcb22eebd`.
- Task SHA256 checked: `cb5e18ae08909e2f61bc91ce88543e98b046f9a6dee7c01f26cd18d2528f792e`.
- Read in full: `TASK_AUDIT.md`, `ALLOCATION_AUDIT.json`, the frozen source, and the project's `PROBLEM.md`. Initialization and `OPERATOR.md` were also checked.
- Mathematical programs, numerical or arithmetic replays, trajectory programs, solvers, formal calls, fact submissions, consults, installations, and subagents used: **zero**. Mathematical checking below is direct textual reasoning. Tools were used for public text access/writes and integrity hashes only.
- No worker, service, configuration, truth-store, publication, finalization, revocation, or shutdown action was taken.

The audited assertion is equality, for each fixed finite word, between the original supremum and its restriction to the displayed affine-height polytope Moreau envelopes, with at most n+2 vertices, unique minimum at zero, and initial orthogonality. It is not a quantitative estimate of that supremum. The public candidate identifier `235e485e10077887` was supplied as provenance, not used as an accepted mathematical premise.

## 2. Finding and the one wording correction

No counterexample, missing hypothesis, incorrect normal orientation, or unjustified supremum/limit passage was found at the declared scope. In particular, strong convexity and strict smoothness have different necessary roles: the former removes equal-gradient ambiguity, while the latter supplies strict slack on every distinct-gradient row. The proof uses both roles correctly.

**Nonblocking editorial issue E1, Section 2, the sentence immediately before (2.3):** the text says that “squared distance” has gradient `I-P_K`. Literally, the gradient of `dist(.,K)^2` is `2(I-P_K)`. The intended and sufficient statement is

    grad [dist(U,K)^2 / 2] = U - P_K(U).

Equation (2.1) already contains the correct factor one-half, and the displayed gradient (2.3) is correct. Thus this is a wording error, not a gap in the proposed theorem or a required modification of the construction. A future clean copy should say “half the squared distance.” For completeness, the differentiability argument with that normalization is supplied in Section 3 below.

No other repair is proposed, and the frozen source remains unchanged.

## 3. Target class: global properties and degenerate polytopes

For a nonempty compact convex K, define

    d(U) = min_(Y in K) ||U-Y||^2 / 2,
    P(U) = P_K(U).

Projection exists because K is compact, and is unique because the squared norm is strictly convex in Y. The normal condition is

    <U-P(U), Z-P(U)> <= 0  for all Z in K.

Writing this condition once for U with Z=P(V), and once for V with Z=P(U), then adding, gives

    <P(U)-P(V), U-V> >= ||P(U)-P(V)||^2.

Expanding the square of the residual difference proves

    ||(U-P(U))-(V-P(V))|| <= ||U-V||.

The trial point P(U) gives

    d(U+v)-d(U) <= <U-P(U),v> + ||v||^2/2.

The trial point P(U+v), used to bound d(U), gives

    d(U+v)-d(U) >= <U+v-P(U+v),v> - ||v||^2/2.

The Lipschitz residual makes the difference between either linear term and `<U-P(U),v>` of order at most `||v||^2`. Therefore d is differentiable with gradient U-P(U). This closes the minor normalization wording issue without invoking an external interpolation theorem.

Completing the square in the actual objective yields

    Phi(X) = <lambda,X> - ||lambda||^2/2 + d(X-lambda),
    grad Phi(X) = X-P_K(X-lambda).

Its gradient difference is a residual difference at inputs separated by the same vector, so its Lipschitz constant is at most one globally. Convexity follows from joint convexity of `<lambda,Y>+||X-Y||^2/2` and the trial point formed from convex combinations of minimizers. This argument has the correct inequality direction for partial minimization.

The exposing assumption gives `<lambda,Y> >= 0` for every Y in K, with equality only at Y=0. The norm term is also nonnegative. Since the minimum over K is attained, Phi(X)=0 forces its minimizing Y to be zero and X=Y. Conversely Phi(0)=0 by the trial point Y=0. Hence the complete minimizer set is exactly `{0}`.

None of these arguments needs K to have interior, full affine dimension, distinct listed generators, or simplex geometry. If K={0}, then Phi(X)=||X||^2/2; the exposing assumption is vacuous and the minimum remains unique. Lambda may be zero in this degenerate class member. The admissible subclass is nonempty, for example by taking K={0}, lambda=0, and any nonzero initial point in a positive finite dimension.

Every restricted instance is consequently an admissible original instance, with original minimizer zero and exactly the denominator `||X_0||^2`. Initial orthogonality is only an additional restriction. This proves the direction `W_aff <= W` without any interpolation or limiting argument.

## 4. Strict source: ordered interpolation, signs, and degeneracies

Fix the strict source used in Section 3 of the draft: mu-strong convexity with mu>0, smoothness bound ell with 0<ell<1, unique minimum at zero, and f(0)=0. For fixed i, the function

    q(x) = f(x)-<g_i,x>

is convex and ell-smooth, and its gradient vanishes at x_i. Thus x_i is a global minimizer of q. Applying the smooth descent inequality at x_j, with step 1/ell, gives

    f_j-f_i-<g_i,x_j-x_i> >= ||g_j-g_i||^2/(2 ell).

After rearrangement this is exactly the draft's orientation

    Q_ij <= -(1/ell-1)||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

The expression using y and delta is also correct: expanding

    delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>

leaves `f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2`. Thus the slack corresponds to the outgoing normal at row i, not its opposite orientation.

The optimum label is a legitimate sample of the same source. At the row `i=*`, `j` arbitrary, one obtains `Q_(*,j)=-delta_j`, hence

    delta_j >= gamma ||g_j||^2,  gamma=(1/ell-1)/2>0.

Strong convexity implies

    <g_i-g_j,x_i-x_j> >= mu ||x_i-x_j||^2.

Consequently equal source gradients force equal source points. Equal points then force equal values, y-vectors, and delta-heights. In particular a zero source gradient forces x_i=0, f_i=0, y_i=0, delta_i=0. Conversely delta_i=0 forces g_i=0 through the strict slack estimate. These implications justify every zero-denominator exclusion in M.

Repeated gradients do **not** imply equal lifted iterates: the scalars z_i may differ on a later revisit. The draft does not need that false implication. It only needs equality of Y_i and Y_j, which does follow from equality of the source gradients. Then the corresponding normal direction and its correction vanish exactly regardless of z_i or e_i. This is the correct treatment of repetitions and zero steps.

## 5. Static lift: every normal and a simultaneous finite B

For the fixed finite source trajectory, the recurrence for z consists of finitely many scalar affine operations on finite quantities. All z_i and e_i are finite and independent of B. No bounded-step or stability assumption is required for this statement.

In the lifted construction,

    <lambda_B,Y_i> = delta_i.

Every nonzero listed Y_i has positive delta_i. A convex combination of listed generators with zero height can assign positive weight only to zero generators, and is therefore zero. This establishes strict exposure on the entire polytope, not only on its generating list. The polytope is compact and has at most n+2 vertices; repetitions and redundant generators only decrease the vertex count.

Independently expanding the projection normal gives

    <G_i-lambda_B,Y_j-Y_i>
      = <g_i,y_j-y_i> + (e_i/B-B)(delta_j-delta_i)/B
      = Q_ij + e_i(delta_j-delta_i)/B^2.

The sign of both the `-lambda_B` term and the height difference matches the required projection condition for `X_i-lambda_B` at Y_i.

If g_i differs from g_j, the denominator `gamma||g_i-g_j||^2` is strictly positive. There are only finitely many such ordered pairs, including j=*. Every ratio in M is finite; its maximum is finite, even if some ratios are very large. If there are no distinct-gradient pairs, the stipulated value M=0 is harmless. For `B^2 >= max(1,2M)`, each positive correction is bounded by half of the corresponding strict interpolation margin. Negative corrections only help. Therefore every distinct-gradient normal is negative, and every equal-gradient normal is zero.

The index set covers i=0,...,n and j=*,0,...,n. It includes terminal row n, terminal comparison point n, every reversed ordered sample pair, and every sample-to-optimum row. Since the tested points generate K_B, linearity in the comparison point extends the inequality to every point of K_B.

The missing-looking optimum row is not actually missing: at the actual lifted optimum X=0, Y=0, the normal against any generator is

    <-lambda_B,Y_j> = -delta_j <= 0.

Hence `P_K(-lambda_B)=0` independently of the dynamic z recurrence. It is neither necessary nor appropriate to assign a dynamic z-value to the optimum label.

The most delicate edge case is a zero source gradient while the lifted vertical coordinate is nonzero. Here x_i=0 and Y_i=0, but X_i=(0,z_i/B) and G_i=(0,z_i/B). Against a nonzero generator the exact normal is

    -delta_j + z_i delta_j/B^2.

This is precisely a distinct-gradient pair already controlled by M. Against a zero generator the normal is zero. Thus source arrival at its minimum does not incorrectly force the lifted run to arrive at its own minimum, and its subsequent vertical motion remains legal. Later large steps can magnify z, but all finitely many resulting rows, including the terminal row, were included before B was selected.

There is no circular dependence: source samples and z/e are fixed first; M is then a finite number; B is chosen; K_B and lambda_B define one static function. That function is not changed during the lifted run. Including the source terminal in its design is valid because the adversary may depend on the entire predetermined word.

## 6. Exact lifted recursion, objective, and radius

Once the simultaneous normal tests hold, the unique projection at each lifted sample is Y_i. Therefore the gradient is G_i, and evaluating the actual minimization objective at its actual minimizer gives

    Phi_B(X_i) = delta_i + ||G_i||^2/2
               = f_i + e_i^2/(2B^2).

There is no unspecified affine constant, no omitted vertical norm, and no replacement of the terminal by a more convenient point. The formula holds for the actual terminal i=n because that row was included in the same normal argument.

The horizontal update is the original source update; the vertical update is exactly

    z_i - h_i e_i = (1-h_i)z_i+h_i delta_i = z_(i+1).

Induction from X_0 then shows that these are the actual iterates of the one static Phi_B under the same original GD word. For h_i=0 both horizontal and vertical coordinates are unchanged. Later repeated horizontal points with different vertical coordinates are already handled by the repeated-gradient normal analysis.

Since z_0=0,

    X_0=(x_0,0),  <lambda_B,X_0>=0,  ||X_0||^2=||x_0||^2>0.

The target function has unique minimizer zero. Thus this norm is the true closest-minimum distance, not merely a distance to a selected convenient minimizer. The size of lambda_B contributes neither to that radius nor to the point X_0.

The terminal payoff is consequently

    2 Phi_B(X_n)/||X_0||^2
       = 2 f(x_n)/||x_0||^2 + e_n^2/(B^2||x_0||^2).

The additional term is nonnegative. Its coefficient is fixed when the strict source and word are fixed, so the stated O(B^-2) description is valid at precisely that scope. Domination itself uses one finite B and does not need a limit in B.

## 7. General sources, selected minimizers, and supremum quantifiers

The original problem permits any selected minimizer x_star in the denominator. Translating that selected minimizer to zero and subtracting f_star leaves f>=0 and grad f(0)=0, even if other minima exist. For 0<epsilon<1/2,

    f_epsilon = (1-2epsilon)f + (epsilon/2)||x||^2

is epsilon-strongly convex. Its gradient Lipschitz bound is

    (1-2epsilon) + epsilon = 1-epsilon < 1.

The coefficients have the required signs. Zero is its unique minimizer with value zero. It therefore satisfies all strict-source hypotheses, including injectivity needed for duplicate-gradient rows.

For each fixed finite word and fixed source, the finite induction in the draft is sufficient. The induction hypothesis `x_i^epsilon -> x_i` gives boundedness of the points near epsilon=0 and, by continuity of grad f,

    (1-2epsilon) grad f(x_i^epsilon) + epsilon x_i^epsilon
        -> grad f(x_i).

Multiplication by the fixed finite h_i preserves convergence. After finitely many such updates, continuity of f and boundedness of the terminal points give convergence of the terminal regularized objective to the original objective. No continuous dependence over an unbounded number of updates is being asserted.

The denominator remains exactly `||x_0-x_star||^2` after translation. For a nonunique-minimum source it need not have been the source's closest-minimum distance, and the proof does not require that: the original definition itself allows every selected minimizer. The constructed target's denominator is nevertheless its true closest-minimum distance because its minimum is unique. In particular, source starts at another minimizer are not excluded; their original payoff is zero and the same approximation argument remains valid.

The quantifier order is

    fixed n,h -> arbitrary original source and selected minimizer
      -> each epsilon>0 -> its actual finite source run
      -> a finite B depending on that run -> a restricted instance.

For each epsilon the restricted supremum is at least the corresponding regularized source payoff. Since the left side is the same supremum for all epsilon, taking the scalar limit yields the original source payoff as a lower bound for it. Taking the supremum over original sources then proves `W_aff >= W` at the candidate's scope. There is no exchange of a horizon limit with a supremum and no requirement that the constructed instances, B-values, or polytopes converge.

Both original and restricted suprema are meaningful. For example, the elementary bounds `||grad f(x)||<=||x||`, `f(x)<=||x||^2/2`, and `||x-h grad f(x)||<=(1+h)||x||` show a finite word has a finite original supremum. A finiteness estimate is not needed to rescue the limit argument, but confirms that no ambiguity such as subtracting infinite quantities is hidden here.

For advertised smoothness L>0, the replacement F=f/L has smoothness at most one; its gradient update with h is exactly the original update with h/L on f. Its normalized payoff is `2(F(x_n)-F_star)/||x_0-x_star||^2`, equal to the original W normalization. Conversely multiplying a normalized constructed target by L gives an admissible L-smooth original instance. The relation W=2R is retained throughout.

Every source dimension d stays finite, and the construction uses d+1 dimensions. It claims neither a fixed universal dimension nor a simplex. Empty words n=0 still have samples {*,0}, at most two generators, and no update to check. Zero steps, revisits, hitting the source optimum, and arbitrarily large but finite nonnegative steps introduce no missing case.

## 8. Coverage and remaining boundary

| Requested attack | Audit outcome |
| --- | --- |
| Global convexity, differentiability, smoothness | No gap found; half-squared-distance wording E1 recorded |
| Unique complete minimizer set, including K={0} | No gap found |
| All ordered strict interpolation margins | Sign and coefficient checked textually |
| Equal gradients and repeated samples | Injectivity closes the zero-denominator cases; z need not repeat |
| Every original and lifted normal | Star, terminal, reversed rows, and convex-hull extension covered |
| Zero source gradients and zero steps | Nonzero vertical residual explicitly retained and controlled |
| One simultaneous finite B | Finite maximum with positive denominators suffices |
| One static function and actual GD terminal | Exact recurrence and all terminal normals checked |
| Objective formula and nonnegative error | Exact evaluation at the projection; no missing norm term |
| True target radius and initial orthogonality | Unique target minimum and z_0=0 give exact identities |
| Nonunique source minima and regularization | Selected-minimizer denominator preserved |
| Supremum and finite-horizon limit quantifiers | Correct order; no uniform B or limit interchange needed |
| Vertex count, dimension, n=0, finite large steps | All within the stated class and quantifiers |
| W=2R and arbitrary L | Normalization maintained |
| Uniform approximation, attainment, simplex, selector claims | None follows or is asserted |
| Original Zhang–Jiang profile-minimax conclusion | Still OPEN; no quantitative comparison supplied |

The structural equality, if subsequently accepted by the verifier, would transfer any genuine uniform bound on the entire restricted class to the original supremum. This audit supplies no such bound, no every-word lower comparison with U_n^ZJ, and no asymptotic separation. The main unresolved quantitative problem is not reduced to a proved estimate by this construction.

This allocation ends with the two public audit artifacts. It grants no automatic next research or verification call.

# Affine-height polytope Moreau envelopes represent the full finite-word supremum

MAIN-AUTHORED JOINT UNVERIFIED candidate. Original exact-profile minimax
target OPEN. This is a structural equality of suprema, not a quantitative
Zhang--Jiang comparison. No mathematical program or formal call was used.

## 1. Precise proposed theorem

Fix any finite n>=0 and any predetermined finite nonnegative word
h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)). Normalize advertised smoothness to one. Let W_n(h)
be the original supremum of2(f(x_n)-f_star)/||x_0-x_star||^2 over all
finite-dimensional convex differentiable1-smooth functions with an attained
minimum and x_0!=x_star, under x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R.

Consider the following subclass in arbitrary finite Euclidean dimensions D.
Choose a compact polytope K with at most n+2 vertices, containing0, and a
vector lambda such that

    <lambda,Y> >0 for every nonzero Y in K.

Thus0 is the exposed unique minimizer of the linear functional on K
(K={0} is allowed). Define the ONE globally specified function

    Phi_(K,lambda)(X)
       = min_(Y in K) [<lambda,Y>+||X-Y||^2/2].       (1.1)

Restrict the nonzero initial point further by <lambda,X_0>=0. Run the
same original word h on this function in the usual Euclidean metric.
Let W_n^aff(h) be the supremum of2Phi(X_n)/||X_0||^2 over these choices.

The candidate theorem is

    W_n^aff(h)=W_n(h) for EVERY finite n and h>=0.     (1.2)

Every member of the restricted class is globally convex differentiable
1-smooth with UNIQUE minimizer0. A strictly smooth strongly convex source
trajectory is represented with exact horizontal dynamics in just one extra
coordinate; its terminal objective increases by an explicit nonnegative
O(B^-2) term. General source functions are then covered by regularization
and finite-horizon continuity. No uniform approximation in n,h is claimed
or needed for the fixed-word equality of suprema.

The restriction in(1.1) concerns the Moreau envelope of a linear functional
plus a polytope indicator. Its gradient is X-P_K(X-lambda), NOT P_K(X).
It must not be confused with the previously studied projection-gradient
potential or its polar-cone minimizers.

## 2. Direct properties of the target class

Completing the square gives

    Y(X)=P_K(X-lambda),
    Phi(X)=<lambda,X>-||lambda||^2/2
                                  +dist(X-lambda,K)^2/2. (2.1)

Projection onto the nonempty compact convex K exists and is unique. Its
normal condition is

    <X-lambda-Y(X),Z-Y(X)> <=0 for every Z in K.      (2.2)

For two inputs U,V, adding their normal inequalities gives
<P_K(U)-P_K(V),U-V>>=||P_K(U)-P_K(V)||^2. Consequently I-P_K is
1-Lipschitz, since expanding its squared difference gives at most||U-V||^2.
The usual two trial-point comparisons for half the squared distance,
followed by this continuity, prove its differentiability with gradient I-P_K.
Thus(2.1) is differentiable and

    grad Phi(X)=X-P_K(X-lambda),                     (2.3)

with Lipschitz constant at most one. Convexity also follows directly from
(1.1): for minimizers Y_U,Y_V the convex combination lies in K, the linear
term averages exactly, and convexity of squared norm bounds Phi at the
corresponding convex combination of inputs.

Both terms in(1.1) are nonnegative. Their sum is zero iff Y=0 and X=Y,
using the strict exposing property. Hence Phi>=0 and its complete minimum
set is exactly{0}. These facts prove W_n^aff(h)<=W_n(h), including the
specified normalization and the additional initial orthogonality restriction.

## 3. Strict source and its exact interpolation slack

First let f:R^d->R be mu-strongly convex and ell-smooth, where mu>0 and
0<ell<1. Translate and subtract a constant so its unique minimizer is0
and f(0)=0. Fix any x_0!=0 and its ACTUAL finite original trajectory.
For EVERY i=0,...,n, including the actual unqueried terminal, write

    g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i),
    y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.

Also use the optimum labels x_*=g_*=y_*=0,f_*=delta_*=0. Set
gamma=(1/ell-1)/2>0. Every ordered original row satisfies

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>
        <=-gamma||g_i-g_j||^2.                     (3.1)

For completeness, the stronger coefficient1/(2ell) follows by applying
the elementary smooth descent inequality to q(x)=f(x)-<g_i,x>.
This convex ell-smooth function is minimized at x_i, so
q(x_i)<=q(x_j-(1/ell)grad q(x_j))
       <=q(x_j)-||g_j-g_i||^2/(2ell).
Rearranging proves(3.1). The smooth descent inequality follows by
integrating the Lipschitz gradient along that one segment; no external
interpolation theorem is needed beyond this argument.

In particular Q_(*,i)=-delta_i gives

    delta_i>=gamma||g_i||^2>=0.                    (3.2)

Strong convexity makes the gradient injective: its strong monotonicity
implies g_i=g_j only if x_i=x_j, and then y_i=y_j and delta_i=delta_j.
Also g_i=0 iff x_i=0, in which case y_i=delta_i=0. These degenerate
cases are essential when the word has zero steps or revisits a point.

## 4. One extra coordinate with fixed affine heights

Define scalar numbers entirely from the fixed finite source trajectory:

    z_0=0,
    z_(i+1)=(1-h_i)z_i+h_i delta_i, 0<=i<n,
    e_i=z_i-delta_i, 0<=i<=n.                       (4.1)

These are symbolic finite definitions, not an executed numerical
recurrence. They do not depend on the forthcoming lift magnitude B.

For B>0 put in R^(d+1)

    lambda_B=(0,B),
    Y_i=(y_i,delta_i/B),
    X_i=(x_i,z_i/B),
    G_i=X_i-Y_i=(g_i,e_i/B),
    K_B=conv{0,Y_0,...,Y_n}.                       (4.2)

K_B has at most n+2 vertices. Every nonzero Y_i has delta_i>0 by
(3.2) and injectivity; if delta_i=0 then Y_i=0. Therefore the linear
functional lambda_B exposes0 strictly on the WHOLE K_B: a convex
combination with zero height can use only zero vertices. This proves
the required minimum property rather than merely assuming it.

We now choose a finite B that makes every Y_i the actual projection
P_(K_B)(X_i-lambda_B). For each i in{0,...,n} and j in{*,0,...,n},
direct expansion gives

    <G_i-lambda_B,Y_j-Y_i>
       =Q_ij+e_i(delta_j-delta_i)/B^2.              (4.3)

When g_i=g_j, the two points and heights agree, so BOTH sides of the
normal test are exactly zero. Otherwise(3.1) has a strictly negative
margin. Define the finite nonnegative scalar

    M=max { [e_i(delta_j-delta_i)]_+
                       /(gamma||g_i-g_j||^2): g_i!=g_j }, (4.4)

where an empty maximum is0. Choosing B^2>=max(1,2M) makes(4.3)
at most -gamma||g_i-g_j||^2/2 for every distinct-gradient pair.
All other pairs are the exact zero cases already handled. Because K_B
is the convex hull of precisely these points, this checks EVERY normal
inequality, not just adjacent or active rows. At the optimum, projection
P_(K_B)(-lambda_B)=0 also follows directly from its exposing property.

Thus(2.2)--(2.3) prove simultaneously, for the ONE static Phi_B of(1.1),

    P_(K_B)(X_i-lambda_B)=Y_i,
    grad Phi_B(X_i)=G_i,
    Phi_B(X_i)=delta_i+||G_i||^2/2
               =f_i+e_i^2/(2B^2), 0<=i<=n.         (4.5)

Including terminal n in the construction is allowed: an adversarial
function may depend on the whole predetermined word, and is fixed once
constructed. No sample is reselected during this actual lifted run.

## 5. Actual original dynamics and exact initial normalization

For EVERY original update, (4.1)--(4.2) give exactly

    X_i-h_i G_i
       =(x_i-h_i g_i,[(1-h_i)z_i+h_i delta_i]/B)
       =X_(i+1).                                  (5.1)

Thus the lifted points are actual GD iterates of the one function Phi_B,
with the SAME steps. The horizontal coordinates agree with the source
exactly, not approximately; all vertical motion is paid and retained.

The initial point is X_0=(x_0,0), so

    <lambda_B,X_0>=0,
    dist(X_0,argmin Phi_B)^2=||x_0||^2.             (5.2)

This is the TRUE closest-minimum distance because the minimum is unique0.
At the actual terminal,

    2Phi_B(X_n)/||X_0||^2
       =2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2+e_n^2/(B^2||x_0||^2)
       >=2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2.                       (5.3)

Every term is finite. Choosing larger B reduces the additional objective,
but no limit in B is needed for the domination inequality. A large lift
vector is allowed; its norm is not part of the initial radius. The equality
of initial distances has been proved, not inferred by dropping a coordinate.

## 6. General convex smooth source by finite-horizon regularization

Now take ANY original1-smooth convex differentiable f with a selected
minimizer x_star, and any x_0!=x_star. Translate that selected minimizer
to0 and subtract f_star. Other minima are allowed at this stage. For
0<epsilon<1/2 define

    f_epsilon(x)=(1-2epsilon)f(x)+(epsilon/2)||x||^2. (6.1)

It has unique minimizer0, is epsilon-strongly convex, and is
ell_epsilon=1-epsilon smooth. The strict-source construction therefore
applies to its actual GD run from the SAME x_0 for the SAME fixed word.

For fixed n and h, these source runs converge to the original one as
epsilon decreases to0. A direct finite induction proves this: if
x_i^epsilon->x_i, continuity and Lipschitzness of grad f imply
grad f_epsilon(x_i^epsilon)->grad f(x_i), and the update with the fixed
finite number h_i then gives x_(i+1)^epsilon->x_(i+1). Function-value
continuity and the explicit small quadratic in(6.1) yield

    f_epsilon(x_n^epsilon)->f(x_n).                (6.2)

For EACH epsilon, (5.3) constructs a member of the restricted class
with at most n+2 vertices and orthogonal initial point whose payoff is
at least2f_epsilon(x_n^epsilon)/||x_0||^2. Taking the limit in these
scalar inequalities gives

    W_n^aff(h)>=2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2.

The source f, selected minimizer and initial point were arbitrary, so
W_n^aff(h)>=W_n(h). Together with Section2 this proves the proposed
equality(1.2) within this JOINT UNVERIFIED draft.

For arbitrary advertised L>0 first replace f by f/L after translating
the chosen minimizer; the normalized updates are exactly h_i grad(f/L)
and the payoff remains W=2R. Thus the reduction respects the original
normalization. Zero steps, repeated points, hitting the minimum, empty
words, arbitrary finite dimensions and arbitrarily large finite steps
were not excluded. No infinite-dimensional limit or uniform error claim
across words/horizons is used.

## 7. Consequences that are NOT claimed

This candidate would show that a uniform upper inequality for the entire
class(1.1), with its exposed minimum and orthogonal initial point, transfers
to the unrestricted W. It supplies NO such quantitative inequality.
In particular it does not prove W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D_m), any benchmark
separation, or the original every-word lower comparison.

The maximum defining M may depend on every source sample and on the full
word. The construction starts from an already specified source trajectory;
it is NOT a schedule-only adversarial selector. It does not claim attainment
of the original supremum, a uniform bound on B, or that K_B is a simplex.
It does not establish that a single observed anchor attains the complete
prefix functional. Positive affine heights remain quantitatively essential.

The entire statement and proof must receive independent attack before any
selected formal submission is considered. No accepted fact is asserted,
no truth store is edited, and the original Goal remains ACTIVE/OPEN.

applies to its actual GD run from the SAME x_0 for the SAME fixed word.

For fixed n and h, these source runs converge to the original one as
epsilon decreases to0. A direct finite induction proves this: if
x_i^epsilon->x_i, continuity and Lipschitzness of grad f imply
grad f_epsilon(x_i^epsilon)->grad f(x_i), and the update with the fixed
finite number h_i then gives x_(i+1)^epsilon->x_(i+1). Function-value
continuity and the explicit small quadratic in(6.1) yield

    f_epsilon(x_n^epsilon)->f(x_n).                (6.2)

For EACH epsilon, (5.3) constructs a member of the restricted class
with at most n+2 vertices and orthogonal initial point whose payoff is
at least2f_epsilon(x_n^epsilon)/||x_0||^2. Taking the limit in these
scalar inequalities gives

    W_n^aff(h)>=2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2.

The source f, selected minimizer and initial point were arbitrary, so
W_n^aff(h)>=W_n(h). Together with Section2 this proves the proposed
equality(1.2) within this JOINT UNVERIFIED draft.

For arbitrary advertised L>0 first replace f by f/L after translating
the chosen minimizer; the normalized updates are exactly h_i grad(f/L)
and the payoff remains W=2R. Thus the reduction respects the original
normalization. Zero steps, repeated points, hitting the minimum, empty
words, arbitrary finite dimensions and arbitrarily large finite steps
were not excluded. No infinite-dimensional limit or uniform error claim
across words/horizons is used.

## 7. Consequences that are NOT claimed

This candidate would show that a uniform upper inequality for the entire
class(1.1), with its exposed minimum and orthogonal initial point, transfers
to the unrestricted W. It supplies NO such quantitative inequality.
In particular it does not prove W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D_m), any benchmark
separation, or the original every-word lower comparison.

The maximum defining M may depend on every source sample and on the full
word. The construction starts from an already specified source trajectory;
it is NOT a schedule-only adversarial selector. It does not claim attainment
of the original supremum, a uniform bound on B, or that K_B is a simplex.
It does not establish that a single observed anchor attains the complete
prefix functional. Positive affine heights remain quantitatively essential.

The entire statement and proof must receive independent attack before any
selected formal submission is considered. No accepted fact is asserted,
no truth store is edited, and the original Goal remains ACTIVE/OPEN.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000516 -->
---
fact_id: d8b59c7d92425121
problem_id: gd-exact-profile-20260926
author: max4
predecessors: []
glossary_introduces:
  B_ah: Positive finite lift scale satisfying B_ah^2>=max(1,2M_ah); distinct from the benchmark sequence B_n.
  G_ah: Actual lifted gradients indexed as G_ah(i)=(g_i,e_ah(i)/B_ah).
  K_ah: Compact target polytope; in the strict-source lift, conv{0,Y_ah(0),...,Y_ah(n)} in R^(d+1).
  M_ah: Finite maximum of the positive correction divided by the strict distinct-gradient margin, with the complete ordered index set specified in the statement.
  P_ah: Euclidean projection onto K_ah.
  Phi_ah: Single global function min over Y in K_ah of <lambda_ah,Y>+||X-Y||^2/2.
  W_aff_n: Restricted supremum over affine-height polytope Moreau envelopes in the theorem, with at most n+2 vertices, exposed minimum zero, and orthogonal nonzero initial point.
  X_ah: Actual target trajectory indexed as X_ah(i); in the lift (x_i,z_ah(i)/B_ah).
  Y_ah: Lifted proximal generators indexed as Y_ah(i)=(y_ah(i),delta_ah(i)/B_ah), with Y_ah(star)=0.
  d_ah: Half the squared distance to K_ah: d_ah(U)=dist(U,K_ah)^2/2.
  delta_ah: Strict-source heights delta_ah(i)=v_ah(i)-||g_i||^2/2, with optimum height zero.
  e_ah: Finite vertical residual numerators e_ah(i)=z_ah(i)-delta_ah(i).
  f_eps: Regularized normalized source (1-2epsilon)f+(epsilon/2)||x||^2 for 0<epsilon<1/2.
  gamma_ah: Strict interpolation margin (1/ell-1)/2, positive when 0<ell<1.
  lambda_ah: Target linear-height vector; in the lift (0,B_ah). This is distinct from scalar silver-ratio parameters elsewhere in the project.
  q_ah: For each fixed source index i, the tilted convex function f(x)-<g_i,x> used to derive its ordered row.
  r_ah: Projection residual r_ah(U)=U-P_ah(U).
  v_ah: Strict-source sampled values v_ah(i)=f(x_i), including v_ah(star)=0.
  x_i^eps: Actual GD iterate of f_eps from the same x_0 on the fixed word.
  y_ah: Strict-source proximal anchors y_ah(i)=x_i-g_i, with optimum anchor zero.
  z_ah: Finite vertical-coordinate numerators: z_ah(0)=0 and z_ah(i+1)=(1-h_i)z_ah(i)+h_i delta_ah(i).
external_refs: []
---

## statement
Affine-height polytope Moreau envelopes represent the full finite-word gradient-descent supremum.

All spaces, norms, and inner products below are finite-dimensional and Euclidean. For every integer n>=0, every finite nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), and every L>0, define W_n(h) as the supremum of
    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)
over every convex differentiable f:R^d->R with gradient Lipschitz constant at most L and an attained minimum, every selected minimizer x_star, and every x_0!=x_star, where
    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i), 0<=i<n.
The word is fixed before the objective is selected. Empty words, zero steps, and arbitrarily large finite steps are allowed. Write R_n(h)=W_n(h)/2.

For the same n,h, define W_aff_n(h) by taking the supremum of
    2 Phi_ah(X_ah(n))/||X_ah(0)||^2
over the following choices in arbitrary positive finite dimension D. Let K_ah be a compact polytope with at most n+2 vertices, containing 0, and let lambda_ah in R^D satisfy
    <lambda_ah,Y> > 0 for every Y in K_ah with Y!=0.
The degenerate polytope K_ah={0} is allowed. Define the single global objective
    Phi_ah(X)=min_(Y in K_ah){<lambda_ah,Y>+||X-Y||^2/2}.
Choose X_ah(0)!=0 with <lambda_ah,X_ah(0)> = 0, and run the actual updates
    X_ah(i+1)=X_ah(i)-h_i grad Phi_ah(X_ah(i)), 0<=i<n.

Then every such Phi_ah is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable with gradient Lipschitz constant at most 1, and has minimum value 0 and complete minimum set {0}. In particular the denominator in its payoff is its true squared initial distance to the entire minimum set. For every finite n and nonnegative h,
    W_aff_n(h)=W_n(h).

More precisely, the following constructive assertion holds. Let f:R^d->R be mu-strongly convex and ell-smooth, with mu>0 and 0<ell<1, minimum f(0)=0, and x_0!=0. Strong convexity means
    f(v)>=f(u)+<grad f(u),v-u>+(mu/2)||v-u||^2
for all u,v; ell-smooth means the gradient is ell-Lipschitz. Run the same word with advertised smoothness 1, so x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad f(x_i). For all i=0,...,n, including the terminal sample, put
    g_i=grad f(x_i), v_ah(i)=f(x_i),
    y_ah(i)=x_i-g_i, delta_ah(i)=v_ah(i)-||g_i||^2/2.
Use an extra optimum label star with x_star=g_star=y_ah(star)=0 and v_ah(star)=delta_ah(star)=0. Define
    gamma_ah=(1/ell-1)/2,
    z_ah(0)=0,
    z_ah(i+1)=(1-h_i)z_ah(i)+h_i delta_ah(i), 0<=i<n,
    e_ah(i)=z_ah(i)-delta_ah(i), 0<=i<=n.
For a real number t let [t]_+=max(t,0). Define the finite scalar
    M_ah=max { [e_ah(i)(delta_ah(j)-delta_ah(i))]_+
                    /(gamma_ah||g_i-g_j||^2):
               0<=i<=n, j in {star,0,...,n}, g_i!=g_j },
where the maximum of an empty set is defined to be zero. For every finite B_ah>0 with B_ah^2>=max(1,2M_ah), put, in R^(d+1),
    lambda_ah=(0,B_ah),
    Y_ah(i)=(y_ah(i),delta_ah(i)/B_ah),
    X_ah(i)=(x_i,z_ah(i)/B_ah),
    G_ah(i)=(g_i,e_ah(i)/B_ah), 0<=i<=n,
    Y_ah(star)=0,
    K_ah=conv{0,Y_ah(0),...,Y_ah(n)}.
Here conv denotes convex hull and the zero in lambda_ah is the horizontal zero vector in R^d. This K_ah, lambda_ah, and X_ah(0) belong to the stated restricted class. Writing P_ah for Euclidean projection onto K_ah, all the following hold simultaneously:
    P_ah(X_ah(i)-lambda_ah)=Y_ah(i),
    grad Phi_ah(X_ah(i))=G_ah(i),
    Phi_ah(X_ah(i))=v_ah(i)+e_ah(i)^2/(2B_ah^2), 0<=i<=n,
    X_ah(i+1)=X_ah(i)-h_i G_ah(i), 0<=i<n,
    <lambda_ah,X_ah(0)> = 0,
    dist(X_ah(0),argmin Phi_ah)^2=||x_0||^2,
    2Phi_ah(X_ah(n))/||X_ah(0)||^2
       =2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2+e_ah(n)^2/(B_ah^2||x_0||^2).
Thus the horizontal trajectory is exact in one extra coordinate and the terminal objective correction is nonnegative and is of order B_ah^(-2) for this fixed source and word.

For an arbitrary original source, the equality of suprema uses translation of its selected minimizer to zero, division of the objective by L, and then the regularizations
    f_eps(x)=(1-2epsilon)f(x)+(epsilon/2)||x||^2, 0<epsilon<1/2.
For each fixed source and finite word these are epsilon-strongly convex and (1-epsilon)-smooth with unique minimum at zero; their actual terminal values converge to that of the translated, normalized original source. The lift is chosen separately for each epsilon. This covers arbitrary selected original minima, including nonunique original minimum sets.

The assertion includes repeated samples, equal source gradients, zero source gradients, and all normals involving the original terminal. It asserts neither a quantitative convergence rate or benchmark comparison, nor a uniform approximation across words or horizons, attainment of either supremum, simplex geometry, a uniform bound on B_ah, or an adversary selectable from the schedule alone without a source trajectory.

## proof
The proof is self-contained and uses no predecessor or external theorem.

1. Properties of the target class.

For a nonempty compact convex K_ah in a Euclidean space, let P_ah(U) be the unique minimizer of ||U-Y||^2 over Y in K_ah. Existence follows from compactness; uniqueness follows from strict convexity of the squared norm on the convex set. For Y in K_ah, the characterization of Y=P_ah(U) is
    <U-Y,Z-Y> <= 0 for every Z in K_ah.                         (A)
Necessity follows by differentiating the squared distance at the endpoint Y of each segment from Y to Z. Conversely, expanding
    ||U-Z||^2=||U-Y||^2-2<U-Y,Z-Y>+||Z-Y||^2
shows that (A) implies Y minimizes squared distance.

Applying (A) at U and V, using the other projection as comparison point, and adding gives
    <P_ah(U)-P_ah(V),U-V> >= ||P_ah(U)-P_ah(V)||^2.
Let r_ah(U)=U-P_ah(U). Expanding ||r_ah(U)-r_ah(V)||^2 gives
    ||r_ah(U)-r_ah(V)||^2
      =||U-V||^2-2<U-V,P_ah(U)-P_ah(V)>
                   +||P_ah(U)-P_ah(V)||^2
      <=||U-V||^2.
In particular r_ah is 1-Lipschitz.

Put d_ah(U)=dist(U,K_ah)^2/2. For any displacement v, using P_ah(U) as a trial point at U+v, and P_ah(U+v) as a trial point at U, gives respectively
    d_ah(U+v)-d_ah(U) <= <r_ah(U),v>+||v||^2/2,
    d_ah(U+v)-d_ah(U) >= <r_ah(U+v),v>-||v||^2/2.
The difference of the two linear terms has absolute value at most ||v||^2 by the Lipschitz bound. Subtracting <r_ah(U),v> therefore leaves an error between -3||v||^2/2 and ||v||^2/2. This proves differentiability and grad d_ah(U)=r_ah(U).

Completing the square in the definition of Phi_ah yields
    Phi_ah(X)=<lambda_ah,X>-||lambda_ah||^2/2+d_ah(X-lambda_ah).
Its unique minimizing Y is P_ah(X-lambda_ah), and
    grad Phi_ah(X)=lambda_ah+r_ah(X-lambda_ah)
                 =X-P_ah(X-lambda_ah).                         (B)
The Lipschitz bound for r_ah proves that this gradient has Lipschitz constant at most 1.

To check convexity directly, fix U,V and t in [0,1]. Let Y_U=P_ah(U-lambda_ah) and Y_V=P_ah(V-lambda_ah). The point tY_U+(1-t)Y_V lies in K_ah. Use it as a trial point at tU+(1-t)V. Its linear height is t<lambda_ah,Y_U>+(1-t)<lambda_ah,Y_V>, and convexity of the squared norm bounds its squared-distance term by the same convex combination of the two squared-distance terms. It follows that
    Phi_ah(tU+(1-t)V)<=t*Phi_ah(U)+(1-t)Phi_ah(V).
All values are finite because the minimized expression is continuous on the nonempty compact K_ah.

The strict exposing condition implies <lambda_ah,Y>>=0 on K_ah, with equality only when Y=0. Both terms in the defining minimum are nonnegative. At X=0 the choice Y=0 gives value zero. If Phi_ah(X)=0, its attained minimizing Y must have zero height and X=Y; hence Y=X=0. Thus argmin Phi_ah={0}. This includes K_ah={0}, for which Phi_ah(X)=||X||^2/2. With advertised smoothness 1, every restricted instance is consequently an original instance with selected minimizer zero. Therefore W_aff_n(h)<=W_n(h) at L=1.

2. Strict source and every ordered original row.

Fix a mu-strongly convex, ell-smooth source with mu>0 and 0<ell<1, minimum f(0)=0, and x_0!=0, as in the statement. All samples indexed by 0,...,n are its actual iterates, including n; add the genuine optimum sample star. Use the definitions g_i, v_ah(i), y_ah(i), delta_ah(i) and gamma_ah in the statement.

For any differentiable function with ell-Lipschitz gradient, integration along a segment from u to u+w gives the descent inequality
    f(u+w)<=f(u)+<grad f(u),w>+(ell/2)||w||^2.
Indeed, after subtracting the constant and linear terms, the integral remainder is
    integral_(t=0)^1 <grad f(u+tw)-grad f(u),w> dt,
which is at most integral_(t=0)^1 ell*t*||w||^2 dt.

For any ordered sample pair i,j in {star,0,...,n}, consider q_ah(x)=f(x)-<g_i,x>. It is convex and ell-smooth, and grad q_ah(x_i)=0, so x_i is a global minimizer. Applying the descent inequality at x_j with displacement -(1/ell)grad q_ah(x_j) yields
    q_ah(x_i)<=q_ah(x_j-(1/ell)grad q_ah(x_j))
             <=q_ah(x_j)-||g_j-g_i||^2/(2ell).
Define the ordered residual, in the project's usual orientation,
    Q_ij=v_ah(i)-v_ah(j)+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.
Rearrangement gives
    Q_ij <= -gamma_ah||g_i-g_j||^2.                            (C)
Substituting x_i=y_ah(i)+g_i and v_ah(i)=delta_ah(i)+||g_i||^2/2 also gives exactly
    Q_ij=delta_ah(i)-delta_ah(j)+<g_i,y_ah(j)-y_ah(i)>.          (D)
With i=star, Q_(star,j)=-delta_ah(j); thus
    delta_ah(j)>=gamma_ah||g_j||^2>=0.                         (E)

Adding the strong-convexity inequalities in the two directions gives
    <g_i-g_j,x_i-x_j> >= mu||x_i-x_j||^2.
Therefore equal gradients imply equal points, equal source values, equal y_ah, and equal delta_ah. A zero gradient forces x_i=0, and hence y_ah(i)=delta_ah(i)=0. Conversely delta_ah(i)=0 forces g_i=0 by (E), and then Y_ah(i)=0 in the forthcoming construction. The argument covers coincident labels, repetitions, and zero steps without deleting any index.

3. Simultaneous normal tests for one fixed lift.

Define the finite scalars z_ah(i), e_ah(i) by the statement. They depend only on the fixed finite source trajectory and word. They are finite and independent of B_ah, since their definitions use only finitely many affine operations on finite source data and finite steps. Form lambda_ah, Y_ah, X_ah, G_ah and K_ah exactly as stated.

The convex hull K_ah is compact and has at most n+2 vertices. Its listed generators have
    <lambda_ah,Y_ah(i)>=delta_ah(i)>=0.
Each nonzero listed generator has strictly positive height by (E) and the zero-gradient implication. A convex combination of these generators and zero can have zero height only if every positive weight is on a zero generator. Such a combination equals zero. Consequently lambda_ah strictly exposes zero on all of K_ah.

For every 0<=i<=n and every j in {star,0,...,n}, expand:
    <G_ah(i)-lambda_ah,Y_ah(j)-Y_ah(i)>
      =<g_i,y_ah(j)-y_ah(i)>
          +(e_ah(i)/B_ah-B_ah)(delta_ah(j)-delta_ah(i))/B_ah
      =Q_ij+e_ah(i)(delta_ah(j)-delta_ah(i))/B_ah^2.            (F)
When g_i=g_j, the source points and heights agree, so Y_ah(i)=Y_ah(j), Q_ij=0, and the correction term is zero. The lifted points X_ah(i) need not agree on a horizontal revisit, and no agreement of their vertical coordinates is assumed or needed.

For g_i!=g_j, the denominator gamma_ah||g_i-g_j||^2 in M_ah is strictly positive. There are finitely many such pairs, so M_ah is finite. If B_ah^2>=max(1,2M_ah), the positive part of the correction in (F) is at most gamma_ah||g_i-g_j||^2/2. This follows directly from the defining maximum when M_ah>0. If M_ah=0 every such positive part is zero. Combining with (C), the normal in (F) is at most
    -gamma_ah||g_i-g_j||^2/2
on every distinct-gradient pair, and zero on every equal-gradient pair.

Since these comparison points generate K_ah, linearity in the comparison point proves
    <X_ah(i)-lambda_ah-Y_ah(i),Z-Y_ah(i)> <=0
for every Z in K_ah and every i=0,...,n. This includes the terminal as the projecting sample and as a comparison generator, all ordered remote pairs, and every sample-to-optimum normal. At the actual lifted optimum X=0, the projection normal at Y=0 is <-lambda_ah,Z><=0 for every Z in K_ah, so P_ah(-lambda_ah)=0 also holds.

The normal test (A) and gradient formula (B) now give
    P_ah(X_ah(i)-lambda_ah)=Y_ah(i),
    grad Phi_ah(X_ah(i))=X_ah(i)-Y_ah(i)=G_ah(i)
for every i=0,...,n. Evaluation at this actual minimizing Y gives
    Phi_ah(X_ah(i))
      =delta_ah(i)+||G_ah(i)||^2/2
      =v_ah(i)+e_ah(i)^2/(2B_ah^2).                           (G)
If a source gradient is zero while z_ah(i) is nonzero, Y_ah(i)=0 but the lifted vertical residual is retained. Its normals against nonzero generators are distinct-gradient pairs in (F), so the same simultaneous bound controls them.

4. Actual dynamics, radius, and terminal domination.

For each original update 0<=i<n,
    X_ah(i)-h_i G_ah(i)
      =(x_i-h_i g_i,(z_ah(i)-h_i e_ah(i))/B_ah)
      =(x_(i+1),((1-h_i)z_ah(i)+h_i delta_ah(i))/B_ah)
      =X_ah(i+1).
The equality of G_ah(i) with the gradient of the single static function Phi_ah proves that all these points are its actual GD trajectory. The entire source trajectory and finite scalars were fixed before B_ah and the polytope were chosen. The function is fixed before the lifted run, including its terminal, and is never reselected during that run.

Since z_ah(0)=0, the initial point is X_ah(0)=(x_0,0), is nonzero, and has inner product zero with lambda_ah. The complete minimizer set was proved to be {0}, so
    dist(X_ah(0),argmin Phi_ah)^2=||X_ah(0)||^2=||x_0||^2.
Using (G) at the actual terminal gives
    2Phi_ah(X_ah(n))/||X_ah(0)||^2
      =2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2+e_ah(n)^2/(B_ah^2||x_0||^2).
This proves every assertion of the constructive statement. The extra term is nonnegative. For fixed source and word its coefficient is fixed and finite, so it is of order B_ah^(-2). Terminal domination already holds at each allowed finite B_ah.

5. Arbitrary sources, selected minima, and equality of suprema.

First work at advertised smoothness 1. Choose an arbitrary original source, any one of its minimizers x_star, and x_0!=x_star. Translate coordinates by x_star and subtract f(x_star). In these coordinates f(0)=0=min f, grad f(0)=0, and the denominator is the positive number ||x_0||^2. No uniqueness or closest-point assumption has been made for the source's selected minimizer.

For each 0<epsilon<1/2, put
    f_eps(x)=(1-2epsilon)f(x)+(epsilon/2)||x||^2.
Convexity of f and positivity of 1-2epsilon give epsilon-strong convexity. Its gradient has Lipschitz constant at most
    (1-2epsilon)+epsilon=1-epsilon<1.
Both terms are nonnegative; equality at zero and the positive quadratic show that its unique minimum is zero at zero.

Let x_i^eps be the actual trajectory of f_eps from this same x_0 with the same h. For each fixed finite word these trajectories converge to the original trajectory as epsilon decreases to zero. The initial points agree. If x_i^eps tends to x_i, then these points are bounded near epsilon=0 and continuity of grad f gives
    grad f_eps(x_i^eps)
      =(1-2epsilon)grad f(x_i^eps)+epsilon x_i^eps
      ->grad f(x_i).
The update with the fixed finite h_i then gives x_(i+1)^eps->x_(i+1). Finite induction proves convergence at n. Continuity of f and boundedness of x_n^eps imply
    f_eps(x_n^eps)
      =(1-2epsilon)f(x_n^eps)+(epsilon/2)||x_n^eps||^2
      ->f(x_n).

For EACH epsilon the constructive assertion applies to this strict source with mu=epsilon and ell=1-epsilon. It gives some restricted instance, in one extra dimension and with at most n+2 vertices, whose payoff is at least 2f_eps(x_n^eps)/||x_0||^2. Consequently
    W_aff_n(h)>=2f_eps(x_n^eps)/||x_0||^2
for every epsilon. The left side is the same fixed-word supremum throughout. Taking the limit in these scalar inequalities yields
    W_aff_n(h)>=2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2.
Since the original source, selected minimizer, dimension, and initial point were arbitrary, W_aff_n(h)>=W_n(h). Together with the reverse inclusion inequality proved in paragraph 1, this establishes equality at advertised smoothness 1.

For any L>0, translate the selected minimizer and replace the original objective by (f-f(x_star))/L. The resulting source is 1-smooth. Its updates with h are exactly the original updates with h/L on f, and its normalized payoff is exactly 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2). Conversely, multiplication of any restricted Phi_ah by L supplies an admissible L-smooth original objective with the same trajectory and payoff. Thus the same equality holds for every advertised L and respects W_n=2R_n.

The order of choices is fixed n,h; arbitrary original source and selected minimizer; each epsilon; its actual finite run; a finite B_ah for that run; then one restricted instance. No exchange of a horizon limit with a supremum, no uniform choice of B_ah, and no convergence of the lifted polytopes is used. If n=0 the sample set is {star,0}, the recurrence has no updates, and every displayed construction and inequality still applies. Zero steps, repeated samples, equal gradients, source arrivals at zero, arbitrary finite dimensions, and large finite nonnegative steps were covered without deleting any sample. This proves only the stated representation equality and constructive identities.

## intuition
Strict smoothness supplies simultaneous strict ordered interpolation margins, while strong convexity resolves equal-gradient degeneracies. A vertical coordinate realizes all sampled proximal heights as one linear functional on a finite polytope. The exact lifted trajectory retains the initial radius and gains a nonnegative terminal objective correction; regularization recovers arbitrary original sources at each fixed finite word.

## DOC-000517 — archive_full_20261010/project/research/direct_affine_height_universality_20261003/GATE_PROOF.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\handoffs\gd-exact-profile-20261010-full-evidence.zip!/project/research/direct_affine_height_universality_20261003/GATE_PROOF.md

原始字节数：12383；原始 SHA-256：d5292ffdc5fc26704551730f083fbebd543c8016e7f52fcd21161750c1406f7a

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000517 -->
The proof is self-contained and uses no predecessor or external theorem.

1. Properties of the target class.

For a nonempty compact convex K_ah in a Euclidean space, let P_ah(U) be the unique minimizer of ||U-Y||^2 over Y in K_ah. Existence follows from compactness; uniqueness follows from strict convexity of the squared norm on the convex set. For Y in K_ah, the characterization of Y=P_ah(U) is
    <U-Y,Z-Y> <= 0 for every Z in K_ah.                         (A)
Necessity follows by differentiating the squared distance at the endpoint Y of each segment from Y to Z. Conversely, expanding
    ||U-Z||^2=||U-Y||^2-2<U-Y,Z-Y>+||Z-Y||^2
shows that (A) implies Y minimizes squared distance.

Applying (A) at U and V, using the other projection as comparison point, and adding gives
    <P_ah(U)-P_ah(V),U-V> >= ||P_ah(U)-P_ah(V)||^2.
Let r_ah(U)=U-P_ah(U). Expanding ||r_ah(U)-r_ah(V)||^2 gives
    ||r_ah(U)-r_ah(V)||^2
      =||U-V||^2-2<U-V,P_ah(U)-P_ah(V)>
                   +||P_ah(U)-P_ah(V)||^2
      <=||U-V||^2.
In particular r_ah is 1-Lipschitz.

Put d_ah(U)=dist(U,K_ah)^2/2. For any displacement v, using P_ah(U) as a trial point at U+v, and P_ah(U+v) as a trial point at U, gives respectively
    d_ah(U+v)-d_ah(U) <= <r_ah(U),v>+||v||^2/2,
    d_ah(U+v)-d_ah(U) >= <r_ah(U+v),v>-||v||^2/2.
The difference of the two linear terms has absolute value at most ||v||^2 by the Lipschitz bound. Subtracting <r_ah(U),v> therefore leaves an error between -3||v||^2/2 and ||v||^2/2. This proves differentiability and grad d_ah(U)=r_ah(U).

Completing the square in the definition of Phi_ah yields
    Phi_ah(X)=<lambda_ah,X>-||lambda_ah||^2/2+d_ah(X-lambda_ah).
Its unique minimizing Y is P_ah(X-lambda_ah), and
    grad Phi_ah(X)=lambda_ah+r_ah(X-lambda_ah)
                 =X-P_ah(X-lambda_ah).                         (B)
The Lipschitz bound for r_ah proves that this gradient has Lipschitz constant at most 1.

To check convexity directly, fix U,V and t in [0,1]. Let Y_U=P_ah(U-lambda_ah) and Y_V=P_ah(V-lambda_ah). The point tY_U+(1-t)Y_V lies in K_ah. Use it as a trial point at tU+(1-t)V. Its linear height is t<lambda_ah,Y_U>+(1-t)<lambda_ah,Y_V>, and convexity of the squared norm bounds its squared-distance term by the same convex combination of the two squared-distance terms. It follows that
    Phi_ah(tU+(1-t)V)<=t*Phi_ah(U)+(1-t)Phi_ah(V).
All values are finite because the minimized expression is continuous on the nonempty compact K_ah.

The strict exposing condition implies <lambda_ah,Y>>=0 on K_ah, with equality only when Y=0. Both terms in the defining minimum are nonnegative. At X=0 the choice Y=0 gives value zero. If Phi_ah(X)=0, its attained minimizing Y must have zero height and X=Y; hence Y=X=0. Thus argmin Phi_ah={0}. This includes K_ah={0}, for which Phi_ah(X)=||X||^2/2. With advertised smoothness 1, every restricted instance is consequently an original instance with selected minimizer zero. Therefore W_aff_n(h)<=W_n(h) at L=1.

2. Strict source and every ordered original row.

Fix a mu-strongly convex, ell-smooth source with mu>0 and 0<ell<1, minimum f(0)=0, and x_0!=0, as in the statement. All samples indexed by 0,...,n are its actual iterates, including n; add the genuine optimum sample star. Use the definitions g_i, v_ah(i), y_ah(i), delta_ah(i) and gamma_ah in the statement.

For any differentiable function with ell-Lipschitz gradient, integration along a segment from u to u+w gives the descent inequality
    f(u+w)<=f(u)+<grad f(u),w>+(ell/2)||w||^2.
Indeed, after subtracting the constant and linear terms, the integral remainder is
    integral_(t=0)^1 <grad f(u+tw)-grad f(u),w> dt,
which is at most integral_(t=0)^1 ell*t*||w||^2 dt.

For any ordered sample pair i,j in {star,0,...,n}, consider q_ah(x)=f(x)-<g_i,x>. It is convex and ell-smooth, and grad q_ah(x_i)=0, so x_i is a global minimizer. Applying the descent inequality at x_j with displacement -(1/ell)grad q_ah(x_j) yields
    q_ah(x_i)<=q_ah(x_j-(1/ell)grad q_ah(x_j))
             <=q_ah(x_j)-||g_j-g_i||^2/(2ell).
Define the ordered residual, in the project's usual orientation,
    Q_ij=v_ah(i)-v_ah(j)+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.
Rearrangement gives
    Q_ij <= -gamma_ah||g_i-g_j||^2.                            (C)
Substituting x_i=y_ah(i)+g_i and v_ah(i)=delta_ah(i)+||g_i||^2/2 also gives exactly
    Q_ij=delta_ah(i)-delta_ah(j)+<g_i,y_ah(j)-y_ah(i)>.          (D)
With i=star, Q_(star,j)=-delta_ah(j); thus
    delta_ah(j)>=gamma_ah||g_j||^2>=0.                         (E)

Adding the strong-convexity inequalities in the two directions gives
    <g_i-g_j,x_i-x_j> >= mu||x_i-x_j||^2.
Therefore equal gradients imply equal points, equal source values, equal y_ah, and equal delta_ah. A zero gradient forces x_i=0, and hence y_ah(i)=delta_ah(i)=0. Conversely delta_ah(i)=0 forces g_i=0 by (E), and then Y_ah(i)=0 in the forthcoming construction. The argument covers coincident labels, repetitions, and zero steps without deleting any index.

3. Simultaneous normal tests for one fixed lift.

Define the finite scalars z_ah(i), e_ah(i) by the statement. They depend only on the fixed finite source trajectory and word. They are finite and independent of B_ah, since their definitions use only finitely many affine operations on finite source data and finite steps. Form lambda_ah, Y_ah, X_ah, G_ah and K_ah exactly as stated.

The convex hull K_ah is compact and has at most n+2 vertices. Its listed generators have
    <lambda_ah,Y_ah(i)>=delta_ah(i)>=0.
Each nonzero listed generator has strictly positive height by (E) and the zero-gradient implication. A convex combination of these generators and zero can have zero height only if every positive weight is on a zero generator. Such a combination equals zero. Consequently lambda_ah strictly exposes zero on all of K_ah.

For every 0<=i<=n and every j in {star,0,...,n}, expand:
    <G_ah(i)-lambda_ah,Y_ah(j)-Y_ah(i)>
      =<g_i,y_ah(j)-y_ah(i)>
          +(e_ah(i)/B_ah-B_ah)(delta_ah(j)-delta_ah(i))/B_ah
      =Q_ij+e_ah(i)(delta_ah(j)-delta_ah(i))/B_ah^2.            (F)
When g_i=g_j, the source points and heights agree, so Y_ah(i)=Y_ah(j), Q_ij=0, and the correction term is zero. The lifted points X_ah(i) need not agree on a horizontal revisit, and no agreement of their vertical coordinates is assumed or needed.

For g_i!=g_j, the denominator gamma_ah||g_i-g_j||^2 in M_ah is strictly positive. There are finitely many such pairs, so M_ah is finite. If B_ah^2>=max(1,2M_ah), the positive part of the correction in (F) is at most gamma_ah||g_i-g_j||^2/2. This follows directly from the defining maximum when M_ah>0. If M_ah=0 every such positive part is zero. Combining with (C), the normal in (F) is at most
    -gamma_ah||g_i-g_j||^2/2
on every distinct-gradient pair, and zero on every equal-gradient pair.

Since these comparison points generate K_ah, linearity in the comparison point proves
    <X_ah(i)-lambda_ah-Y_ah(i),Z-Y_ah(i)> <=0
for every Z in K_ah and every i=0,...,n. This includes the terminal as the projecting sample and as a comparison generator, all ordered remote pairs, and every sample-to-optimum normal. At the actual lifted optimum X=0, the projection normal at Y=0 is <-lambda_ah,Z><=0 for every Z in K_ah, so P_ah(-lambda_ah)=0 also holds.

The normal test (A) and gradient formula (B) now give
    P_ah(X_ah(i)-lambda_ah)=Y_ah(i),
    grad Phi_ah(X_ah(i))=X_ah(i)-Y_ah(i)=G_ah(i)
for every i=0,...,n. Evaluation at this actual minimizing Y gives
    Phi_ah(X_ah(i))
      =delta_ah(i)+||G_ah(i)||^2/2
      =v_ah(i)+e_ah(i)^2/(2B_ah^2).                           (G)
If a source gradient is zero while z_ah(i) is nonzero, Y_ah(i)=0 but the lifted vertical residual is retained. Its normals against nonzero generators are distinct-gradient pairs in (F), so the same simultaneous bound controls them.

4. Actual dynamics, radius, and terminal domination.

For each original update 0<=i<n,
    X_ah(i)-h_i G_ah(i)
      =(x_i-h_i g_i,(z_ah(i)-h_i e_ah(i))/B_ah)
      =(x_(i+1),((1-h_i)z_ah(i)+h_i delta_ah(i))/B_ah)
      =X_ah(i+1).
The equality of G_ah(i) with the gradient of the single static function Phi_ah proves that all these points are its actual GD trajectory. The entire source trajectory and finite scalars were fixed before B_ah and the polytope were chosen. The function is fixed before the lifted run, including its terminal, and is never reselected during that run.

Since z_ah(0)=0, the initial point is X_ah(0)=(x_0,0), is nonzero, and has inner product zero with lambda_ah. The complete minimizer set was proved to be {0}, so
    dist(X_ah(0),argmin Phi_ah)^2=||X_ah(0)||^2=||x_0||^2.
Using (G) at the actual terminal gives
    2Phi_ah(X_ah(n))/||X_ah(0)||^2
      =2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2+e_ah(n)^2/(B_ah^2||x_0||^2).
This proves every assertion of the constructive statement. The extra term is nonnegative. For fixed source and word its coefficient is fixed and finite, so it is of order B_ah^(-2). Terminal domination already holds at each allowed finite B_ah.

5. Arbitrary sources, selected minima, and equality of suprema.

First work at advertised smoothness 1. Choose an arbitrary original source, any one of its minimizers x_star, and x_0!=x_star. Translate coordinates by x_star and subtract f(x_star). In these coordinates f(0)=0=min f, grad f(0)=0, and the denominator is the positive number ||x_0||^2. No uniqueness or closest-point assumption has been made for the source's selected minimizer.

For each 0<epsilon<1/2, put
    f_eps(x)=(1-2epsilon)f(x)+(epsilon/2)||x||^2.
Convexity of f and positivity of 1-2epsilon give epsilon-strong convexity. Its gradient has Lipschitz constant at most
    (1-2epsilon)+epsilon=1-epsilon<1.
Both terms are nonnegative; equality at zero and the positive quadratic show that its unique minimum is zero at zero.

Let x_i^eps be the actual trajectory of f_eps from this same x_0 with the same h. For each fixed finite word these trajectories converge to the original trajectory as epsilon decreases to zero. The initial points agree. If x_i^eps tends to x_i, then these points are bounded near epsilon=0 and continuity of grad f gives
    grad f_eps(x_i^eps)
      =(1-2epsilon)grad f(x_i^eps)+epsilon x_i^eps
      ->grad f(x_i).
The update with the fixed finite h_i then gives x_(i+1)^eps->x_(i+1). Finite induction proves convergence at n. Continuity of f and boundedness of x_n^eps imply
    f_eps(x_n^eps)
      =(1-2epsilon)f(x_n^eps)+(epsilon/2)||x_n^eps||^2
      ->f(x_n).

For EACH epsilon the constructive assertion applies to this strict source with mu=epsilon and ell=1-epsilon. It gives some restricted instance, in one extra dimension and with at most n+2 vertices, whose payoff is at least 2f_eps(x_n^eps)/||x_0||^2. Consequently
    W_aff_n(h)>=2f_eps(x_n^eps)/||x_0||^2
for every epsilon. The left side is the same fixed-word supremum throughout. Taking the limit in these scalar inequalities yields
    W_aff_n(h)>=2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2.
Since the original source, selected minimizer, dimension, and initial point were arbitrary, W_aff_n(h)>=W_n(h). Together with the reverse inclusion inequality proved in paragraph 1, this establishes equality at advertised smoothness 1.

For any L>0, translate the selected minimizer and replace the original objective by (f-f(x_star))/L. The resulting source is 1-smooth. Its updates with h are exactly the original updates with h/L on f, and its normalized payoff is exactly 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2). Conversely, multiplication of any restricted Phi_ah by L supplies an admissible L-smooth original objective with the same trajectory and payoff. Thus the same equality holds for every advertised L and respects W_n=2R_n.

The order of choices is fixed n,h; arbitrary original source and selected minimizer; each epsilon; its actual finite run; a finite B_ah for that run; then one restricted instance. No exchange of a horizon limit with a supremum, no uniform choice of B_ah, and no convergence of the lifted polytopes is used. If n=0 the sample set is {star,0}, the recurrence has no updates, and every displayed construction and inequality still applies. Zero steps, repeated samples, equal gradients, source arrivals at zero, arbitrary finite dimensions, and large finite nonnegative steps were covered without deleting any sample. This proves only the stated representation equality and constructive identities.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000518 -->
Affine-height polytope Moreau envelopes represent the full finite-word gradient-descent supremum.

All spaces, norms, and inner products below are finite-dimensional and Euclidean. For every integer n>=0, every finite nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), and every L>0, define W_n(h) as the supremum of
    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)
over every convex differentiable f:R^d->R with gradient Lipschitz constant at most L and an attained minimum, every selected minimizer x_star, and every x_0!=x_star, where
    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i), 0<=i<n.
The word is fixed before the objective is selected. Empty words, zero steps, and arbitrarily large finite steps are allowed. Write R_n(h)=W_n(h)/2.

For the same n,h, define W_aff_n(h) by taking the supremum of
    2 Phi_ah(X_ah(n))/||X_ah(0)||^2
over the following choices in arbitrary positive finite dimension D. Let K_ah be a compact polytope with at most n+2 vertices, containing 0, and let lambda_ah in R^D satisfy
    <lambda_ah,Y> > 0 for every Y in K_ah with Y!=0.
The degenerate polytope K_ah={0} is allowed. Define the single global objective
    Phi_ah(X)=min_(Y in K_ah){<lambda_ah,Y>+||X-Y||^2/2}.
Choose X_ah(0)!=0 with <lambda_ah,X_ah(0)> = 0, and run the actual updates
    X_ah(i+1)=X_ah(i)-h_i grad Phi_ah(X_ah(i)), 0<=i<n.

Then every such Phi_ah is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable with gradient Lipschitz constant at most 1, and has minimum value 0 and complete minimum set {0}. In particular the denominator in its payoff is its true squared initial distance to the entire minimum set. For every finite n and nonnegative h,
    W_aff_n(h)=W_n(h).

More precisely, the following constructive assertion holds. Let f:R^d->R be mu-strongly convex and ell-smooth, with mu>0 and 0<ell<1, minimum f(0)=0, and x_0!=0. Strong convexity means
    f(v)>=f(u)+<grad f(u),v-u>+(mu/2)||v-u||^2
for all u,v; ell-smooth means the gradient is ell-Lipschitz. Run the same word with advertised smoothness 1, so x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad f(x_i). For all i=0,...,n, including the terminal sample, put
    g_i=grad f(x_i), v_ah(i)=f(x_i),
    y_ah(i)=x_i-g_i, delta_ah(i)=v_ah(i)-||g_i||^2/2.
Use an extra optimum label star with x_star=g_star=y_ah(star)=0 and v_ah(star)=delta_ah(star)=0. Define
    gamma_ah=(1/ell-1)/2,
    z_ah(0)=0,
    z_ah(i+1)=(1-h_i)z_ah(i)+h_i delta_ah(i), 0<=i<n,
    e_ah(i)=z_ah(i)-delta_ah(i), 0<=i<=n.
For a real number t let [t]_+=max(t,0). Define the finite scalar
    M_ah=max { [e_ah(i)(delta_ah(j)-delta_ah(i))]_+
                    /(gamma_ah||g_i-g_j||^2):
               0<=i<=n, j in {star,0,...,n}, g_i!=g_j },
where the maximum of an empty set is defined to be zero. For every finite B_ah>0 with B_ah^2>=max(1,2M_ah), put, in R^(d+1),
    lambda_ah=(0,B_ah),
    Y_ah(i)=(y_ah(i),delta_ah(i)/B_ah),
    X_ah(i)=(x_i,z_ah(i)/B_ah),
    G_ah(i)=(g_i,e_ah(i)/B_ah), 0<=i<=n,
    Y_ah(star)=0,
    K_ah=conv{0,Y_ah(0),...,Y_ah(n)}.
Here conv denotes convex hull and the zero in lambda_ah is the horizontal zero vector in R^d. This K_ah, lambda_ah, and X_ah(0) belong to the stated restricted class. Writing P_ah for Euclidean projection onto K_ah, all the following hold simultaneously:
    P_ah(X_ah(i)-lambda_ah)=Y_ah(i),
    grad Phi_ah(X_ah(i))=G_ah(i),
    Phi_ah(X_ah(i))=v_ah(i)+e_ah(i)^2/(2B_ah^2), 0<=i<=n,
    X_ah(i+1)=X_ah(i)-h_i G_ah(i), 0<=i<n,
    <lambda_ah,X_ah(0)> = 0,
    dist(X_ah(0),argmin Phi_ah)^2=||x_0||^2,
    2Phi_ah(X_ah(n))/||X_ah(0)||^2
       =2f(x_n)/||x_0||^2+e_ah(n)^2/(B_ah^2||x_0||^2).
Thus the horizontal trajectory is exact in one extra coordinate and the terminal objective correction is nonnegative and is of order B_ah^(-2) for this fixed source and word.

For an arbitrary original source, the equality of suprema uses translation of its selected minimizer to zero, division of the objective by L, and then the regularizations
    f_eps(x)=(1-2epsilon)f(x)+(epsilon/2)||x||^2, 0<epsilon<1/2.
For each fixed source and finite word these are epsilon-strongly convex and (1-epsilon)-smooth with unique minimum at zero; their actual terminal values converge to that of the translated, normalized original source. The lift is chosen separately for each epsilon. This covers arbitrary selected original minima, including nonunique original minimum sets.

The assertion includes repeated samples, equal source gradients, zero source gradients, and all normals involving the original terminal. It asserts neither a quantitative convergence rate or benchmark comparison, nor a uniform approximation across words or horizons, attainment of either supremum, simplex geometry, a uniform bound on B_ah, or an adversary selectable from the schedule alone without a source trajectory.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000519 -->
# Affine-height universality: independent audit handoff

**Status: NO GAP FOUND AT DECLARED SCOPE — INDEPENDENT UNVERIFIED AUDIT.**

Project `gd-exact-profile-20260926`. The exact-profile minimax target remains OPEN. This audit is neither Danus verifier acceptance nor a fact submission.

## Frozen object and scope

The complete `DRAFT_UNVERIFIED.md` was read and independently attacked. Its checked size is 11,382 bytes and its SHA256 is

    0cdf678b000d165b1a352fc16d0dd726763e5553944f79d9f9da62fdcb22eebd

The draft claims, for every fixed finite predetermined nonnegative word, equality of the original normalized supremum W with its supremum over

    Phi(X)=min_(Y in K) [<lambda,Y>+||X-Y||^2/2],

where K has at most n+2 vertices, contains zero, lambda strictly exposes zero as its unique minimum on K, and the nonzero initial point is orthogonal to lambda. It does not claim a Zhang–Jiang quantitative inequality. Candidate identifier `235e485e10077887` was provenance only, not an accepted premise.

## Findings

No structural gap or missing hypothesis was found. The full reasoning and coverage table are in `AUDIT.md`. In particular:

1. Global convexity, 1-smoothness, and the complete unique minimizer set `{0}` are valid, including degenerate polytopes.
2. Strict smoothness gives a negative ordered interpolation margin whenever source gradients differ. Strong convexity makes equal gradients imply equal source points, values, and lifted polytope points.
3. Equal source gradients need not imply equal lifted iterates: the vertical z-values can differ. This is harmless because the normal comparison direction is then zero. This distinction was explicitly checked.
4. The lifted normal is exactly `Q_ij+e_i(delta_j-delta_i)/B^2`. Its signs are correct. The finite M includes all sample-to-star, terminal, reversed, and nonadjacent rows. The star-to-sample rows hold directly by exposure.
5. If the source hits its minimum while the lift retains vertical motion, the nontrivial normal is `-delta_j+z_i delta_j/B^2`; it is already controlled by M. Later finite large steps cause no unhandled case.
6. A single finite B makes one static function realize every displayed lifted sample. The exact recursion retains all vertical motion. Its terminal value is `f_n+e_n^2/(2B^2)`.
7. The target's unique minimum makes its initial radius exactly `||x_0||`, and z_0=0 gives initial orthogonality. No coordinate or minimizer-distance term is dropped.
8. Regularization covers arbitrary selected source minimizers, including nonunique source minima. The source's selected-minimizer denominator is preserved. Fixed-word finite induction justifies the scalar payoff limit; no uniform B, convergence of constructed functions, or horizon/supremum interchange is required.
9. The vertex count, d+1 lift dimension, empty word, zero steps, finite large steps, the identity W=2R, and normalization for arbitrary advertised L are all within scope.

**Nonblocking editorial issue:** in Section 2 immediately before (2.3), “squared distance” should read “half the squared distance” when its gradient is identified as I-P_K. The displayed (2.1) already has the correct factor one-half and (2.3) is correct. `AUDIT.md` includes a direct two-trial-point differentiability argument with the correct normalization. No source edit was made.

# max4 invocation 85: affine-height universality gate

Outcome: ACCEPTED AND PERSISTED. The original Zhang--Jiang exact constant/profile problem remains OPEN.

## Accepted theorem and its boundary

For every finite integer n>=0 and every finite nonnegative gradient-descent word h, the original supremum W_n(h), with W_n=2R_n and arbitrary advertised L>0, equals its restriction W_aff_n(h) to single static functions
    Phi(X)=min_(Y in K){<lambda,Y>+||X-Y||^2/2},
where K is a compact polytope with at most n+2 vertices containing zero, <lambda,Y> is strictly positive for every nonzero Y in K, and the nonzero initial point has <lambda,X_0> = 0. Each such function has unique global minimum zero at zero, so its denominator is the true squared initial distance to its entire minimum set.

The accepted constructive assertion retains both strict smoothness ell<1 and strong convexity mu>0. It lifts every strict-source finite trajectory into one extra coordinate, preserves its horizontal dynamics exactly, checks every normal including all terminal and optimum comparisons, and adds the nonnegative terminal correction e(n)^2/(B^2||x_0||^2). Regularization covers arbitrary original selected minima. Empty words, zero steps, repeated samples, equal gradients, and zero gradients are included.

This is full-class representation only. No quantitative upper or lower rate, Zhang--Jiang constant/profile comparison, attainment, uniform approximation over horizons, simplex restriction, uniform lift bound, or schedule-only adversarial selector was established. # ONE independent audit of a complete structural candidate

frozen DRAFT_UNVERIFIED.md in this directory: 11382 bytes, SHA256 0cdf678b000d165b1a352fc16d0dd726763e5553944f79d9f9da62fdcb22eebd.
This was directly derived by main under explicit direct-reasoning authorization.
It is UNVERIFIED, not an accepted premise. Your role is an independent hostile
check, not agreement or a rewrite of its conclusion.

The proposed theorem says that for every finite predetermined nonnegative
word, the unrestricted original W equals its supremum over Moreau envelopes
min_(Y in K)[lambda.Y+||X-Y||^2/2], where K has at most n+2 vertices, lambda
strictly exposes its minimum0, and the nonzero initial X0 is orthogonal to
lambda. It proposes exact horizontal GD dynamics in one extra coordinate,
an EXACT original initial radius, and a nonnegative terminal objective error.
This is not the earlier projection-gradient potential with polar minima.

Independently check every assertion at its exact scope. In particular attack:

   actual target class, including degeneracies.

   points/heights under the chosen regularization.

   entire finite sample simultaneously legal, while retaining one STATIC
   function and the actual original terminal.
