<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000522 -->
**UNVERIFIED — the original minimax problem remains unresolved.** The intermediate result below has a complete derivation, but has not been accepted by a verifier. It gives a concrete obstruction to projection-based adversaries and identifies precisely how a slightly richer geometric construction escapes that obstruction.

The result is an exact distinction:

- Arbitrary squared distances to convex sets, even allowing every dimension and curvature, have precisely the same worst-case power as scalar quadratics. Their optimal profile is \((2n+1)^{-2}\), which is too small to prove the requested lower bound.
- Squared distances to polyhedra with one coordinate held fixed are already sufficient to approximate every finite GD run, including its terminal objective. At horizon \(n\), at most \(n+2\) defining inequalities suffice. The approximation error below is explicit and accommodates arbitrary nonnegative steps.

The second statement is a representation theorem, not a constant-preserving adversary construction for the Zhang–Jiang benchmark.

Normalize \(L=1\), \(x_\star=0\), \(f_\star=0\), and \(\|x_0\|=1\). This loses no generality: for an original initial distance \(r>0\), replace \(f\) by
\[
F(z)=\frac{f(x_\star+rz)-f_\star}{Lr^2}.
\]
The normalized performance is then \(2F(z_n)\).

**UNVERIFIED proposition: ordinary squared-distance adversaries collapse exactly to quadratics.**

For every \(n\ge0\) and every \(h\in[0,\infty)^n\), consider the class
\[
f_{\lambda,K}(x)=\frac{\lambda}{2}\operatorname{dist}(x,K)^2,
\qquad 0<\lambda\le1,
\]
where \(K\) is any nonempty closed convex set in any finite dimension. Its normalized worst-case performance equals
\[
Q_n(h):=\max_{0\le\lambda\le1}
\lambda\prod_{t=0}^{n-1}(1-\lambda h_t)^2.
\tag{1}
\]
Moreover,
\[
\inf_{h\in[0,\infty)^n}Q_n(h)=\frac1{(2n+1)^2}.
\tag{2}
\]

To prove (1), let \(p_t=P_Kx_t\). Squared distance is convex and differentiable, with
\[
\nabla f_{\lambda,K}(x)=\lambda(x-P_Kx).
\]
Its gradient is \(\lambda\)-Lipschitz. These properties follow from the projection optimality inequality
\[
\langle x-P_Kx,z-P_Kx\rangle\le0\qquad(z\in K);
\]
applying it at two points shows that \(I-P_K\) is firmly nonexpansive.

The GD update is
\[
x_{t+1}=(1-\lambda h_t)x_t+\lambda h_t p_t.
\]
Since \(p_t\in K\), for every nonnegative step, including steps exceeding \(2/\lambda\),
\[
\operatorname{dist}(x_{t+1},K)
\le \|x_{t+1}-p_t\|
=|1-\lambda h_t|\operatorname{dist}(x_t,K).
\]
Consequently, for any \(x_\star\in K\),
\[
\frac{2f_{\lambda,K}(x_n)}{\|x_0-x_\star\|^2}
\le
\lambda\prod_{t=0}^{n-1}(1-\lambda h_t)^2.
\]
Equality is attained by \(K=\{0\}\) in dimension one. This proves (1), including the supremum over all convex sets.

Here is a self-contained proof of (2). Put \(m=2n+1\) and
\[
q(x)=x\prod_{t=0}^{n-1}(1-h_tx^2).
\]
Then \(q\) is odd, has degree at most \(m\), satisfies \(q'(0)=1\), and
\[
Q_n(h)=\|q\|_{\infty,[-1,1]}^2.
\]

Define
\[
q_\ast(x)=\frac{\sin(m\arcsin x)}m.
\]
For odd \(m\), this is an odd polynomial of degree \(m\), with derivative \(1\) at zero and supremum norm \(1/m\).

No odd polynomial \(q\) of degree at most \(m\), with \(q'(0)=1\), can have strictly smaller norm. Indeed, at the \(n+1\) positive points
\[
\xi_j=\sin\frac{(2j+1)\pi}{2m},
\qquad 0\le j\le n,
\]
we have
\[
q_\ast(\xi_j)=\frac{(-1)^j}{m}.
\]
If \(\|q\|_\infty<1/m\), the difference \(q-q_\ast\) changes sign between every consecutive pair of these points. It therefore has \(n\) positive roots and, by oddness, \(n\) negative roots. Its root at zero has multiplicity at least three because its derivative there vanishes. That gives at least \(2n+3\) roots counted with multiplicity, exceeding its degree.

Finally,
\[
q_\ast(x)
=x\prod_{j=1}^{n}
\left(1-\frac{x^2}{\sin^2(j\pi/m)}\right).
\]
Thus equality is attained by the nonnegative steps
\[
h_j=\csc^2\frac{j\pi}{2n+1},
\qquad 1\le j\le n,
\tag{3}
\]
in any order.

This supplies a precise obstruction. Writing \(B_n=2S_\bullet(n)+1\), the supplied growth estimate gives
\[
\frac{\inf_h Q_n(h)}{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
=\frac{B_n}{(2n+1)^2}
=\Theta(n^{\rho-2})\longrightarrow0.
\tag{4}
\]
Therefore even this unrestricted collection of nonlinear squared-distance objectives cannot establish (Q). Equation (4) is **not** a full-class upper bound and gives no negative resolution of (P).

**UNVERIFIED proposition: fixing one extra coordinate makes squared-distance geometry universal for finite GD runs.**

For every fixed \(n\), every \(h\in[0,\infty)^n\), every normalized admissible GD run, and every \(\eta>0\), there exist:

- a dimension \(d\le n+1\);
- a finite convex function
  \[
  p(y)=\max\{0,\ell_0(y),\ldots,\ell_n(y)\},
  \qquad p(0)=0,
  \tag{5}
  \]
  with affine \(\ell_i\);
- a number \(R>0\);

such that the single static function
\[
F_R(x)
=
\frac12\operatorname{dist}\!\left((x,-R),K_R\right)^2
-\frac{R^2}{2},
\qquad
K_R=\left\{(y,s):s\ge\frac{p(y)}R\right\},
\tag{6}
\]
is globally convex, differentiable, and \(1\)-smooth, has minimum zero at \(x=0\), and its GD run with the original schedule satisfies
\[
\left|2F_R(x_n^{(R)})-2f(x_n)\right|\le\eta.
\tag{7}
\]
The polyhedron \(K_R\subset\mathbb R^{d+1}\) has at most \(n+2\) defining inequalities.

Here is the construction and its quantitative proof.

First restrict the original function to
\[
V=\operatorname{span}\{x_0,\nabla f(x_0),\ldots,\nabla f(x_{n-1})\}.
\]
Its dimension is at most \(n+1\). Every iterate belongs to \(V\), and every gradient used by the algorithm belongs to \(V\), so restricting \(f\) to this subspace preserves the entire run and its objective values. Convexity, smoothness, the minimizer, and the initial normalization are preserved.

Work henceforth in this subspace. For \(0\le i\le n\), write
\[
f_i=f(x_i),\qquad g_i=\nabla f(x_i),\qquad
y_i=x_i-g_i,\qquad
a_i=f_i-\frac12\|g_i\|^2.
\tag{8}
\]

We need the following inequality, which we derive rather than assume:
\[
f_i\ge
f_j+\langle g_j,x_i-x_j\rangle
+\frac12\|g_i-g_j\|^2.
\tag{9}
\]
The function \(z\mapsto f(z)-\langle g_j,z\rangle\) is convex and \(1\)-smooth and is minimized at \(x_j\). Applying the smoothness descent inequality at \(x_i\), with step equal to the negative gradient, proves (9).

Taking \(j\) to be the minimizer in (9) gives \(a_i\ge0\). Rearranging (9) also gives
\[
a_i\ge a_j+\langle g_j,y_i-y_j\rangle.
\tag{10}
\]

Now define
\[
p(y)=
\max\left\{
0,\;
a_i+\langle g_i,y-y_i\rangle:
0\le i\le n
\right\}.
\tag{11}
\]
Equation (10) implies
\[
p(y_i)=a_i,\qquad g_i\in\partial p(y_i).
\tag{12}
\]
Furthermore, (9) with the minimizer on its left gives
\[
a_i-\langle g_i,y_i\rangle
=f_i-\langle g_i,x_i\rangle+\frac12\|g_i\|^2
\le0.
\]
Thus \(p(0)=0\), as required.

Consider its quadratic envelope
\[
\bar f(x)=\min_y\left\{p(y)+\frac12\|x-y\|^2\right\}.
\tag{13}
\]
The minimizer is unique because the objective is strongly convex and coercive. If it is denoted by \(y(x)\), then
\[
\nabla\bar f(x)=x-y(x).
\tag{14}
\]
For completeness, the optimality condition is
\[
x-y(x)\in\partial p(y(x)).
\]
Monotonicity of the subdifferential, applied at two points, yields
\[
\|\nabla\bar f(x)-\nabla\bar f(z)\|^2
\le
\langle \nabla\bar f(x)-\nabla\bar f(z),x-z\rangle.
\tag{15}
\]
Comparing the objectives in (13) at their respective minimizers proves differentiability and (14); inequality (15) proves \(1\)-Lipschitz continuity of the gradient. Convexity follows from joint convexity of the minimized expression.

By (12), \(y_i\) is exactly the minimizer in (13) at \(x_i\). Consequently,
\[
\bar f(x_i)=f_i,\qquad
\nabla\bar f(x_i)=g_i.
\tag{16}
\]
Thus one static envelope already reproduces all the recorded values and gradients exactly.

We now realize an arbitrarily accurate version through the polyhedron in (6). Since \(p\ge0\), minimizing over its epigraph gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
F_R(x)
&=\min_y\left\{
\frac12\|x-y\|^2
+\frac12\left(R+\frac{p(y)}R\right)^2
-\frac{R^2}{2}
\right\}\\
&=\min_y\left\{
\frac12\|x-y\|^2+p(y)+\frac{p(y)^2}{2R^2}
\right\}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{17}
\]
The function
\[
p_R=p+\frac{p^2}{2R^2}
\]
is convex because \(p\) is convex and nonnegative. The envelope argument just given therefore proves that \(F_R\) is globally convex and \(1\)-smooth. It is nonnegative and satisfies \(F_R(0)=0\).

At every original query point, evaluating (17) at \(y_i\) gives
\[
0\le F_R(x_i)-f_i\le\frac{a_i^2}{2R^2}.
\tag{18}
\]
Let \(y_i^{(R)}\) be the minimizer in (17). Strong convexity of
\[
y\mapsto p(y)+\frac12\|x_i-y\|^2
\]
and (18) imply
\[
\frac12\|y_i^{(R)}-y_i\|^2\le\frac{a_i^2}{2R^2}.
\]
Using the gradient formula,
\[
\|\nabla F_R(x_i)-g_i\|\le\frac{a_i}{R}.
\tag{19}
\]

To control the entire perturbed trajectory, define
\[
M(h)=\max\{1,|1-h|\}
\]
and
\[
A_0=0,\qquad
A_{t+1}=M(h_t)A_t+h_ta_t.
\tag{20}
\]
For any convex \(1\)-smooth function \(F\), the map \(I-h\nabla F\) is \(M(h)\)-Lipschitz for every \(h\ge0\). Indeed, setting
\[
u=x-z,\qquad v=\nabla F(x)-\nabla F(z),
\]
inequality (15), or the sum of two instances of (9), gives
\[
\langle u,v\rangle\ge\|v\|^2.
\]
Hence
\[
\|u-hv\|^2
\le\|u\|^2+h(h-2)\|v\|^2
\le M(h)^2\|u\|^2.
\tag{21}
\]

Start the \(F_R\)-run at the same \(x_0\). Equations (19)–(21) yield inductively
\[
\|x_t^{(R)}-x_t\|\le\frac{A_t}{R}.
\tag{22}
\]
Every quantity \(A_t\) is finite for every allowed schedule. No bounded-step assumption has entered.

Finally, smoothness, (18), (19), and (22) give
\[
\begin{aligned}
|F_R(x_n^{(R)})-f_n|
&\le
\frac{a_n^2}{2R^2}
+\left(\|g_n\|+\frac{a_n}{R}\right)\frac{A_n}{R}
+\frac{A_n^2}{2R^2}\\
&=
\frac{\|g_n\|A_n}{R}
+\frac{(a_n+A_n)^2}{2R^2}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{23}
\]
Thus the normalized performance error is bounded by
\[
\boxed{
|2F_R(x_n^{(R)})-2f_n|
\le
\frac{2\|g_n\|A_n}{R}
+\frac{(a_n+A_n)^2}{R^2}.
}
\tag{24}
\]
Choosing
\[
R\ge
\max\left\{
1,\,
\frac{4\|g_n\|A_n}{\eta},\,
\frac{\sqrt2\,(a_n+A_n)}{\sqrt\eta}
\right\}
\]
proves (7).

This proves, in particular, the exact identity
\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n(h)
=
\sup_{\substack{
1\le d\le n+1,\ R>0,\ \|x_0\|=1\\
p=\max(0,\ell_0,\ldots,\ell_n),\ p(0)=0
}}
2F_R(x_n^{(R)}).
}
\tag{25}
\]
One direction holds because every \(F_R\) is admissible. The other follows by approximating each admissible normalized run using (24). No uniform limit interchange is required.

The geometric distinction is essential. In (6), the algorithm operates on \(x\); the extra coordinate stays fixed at \(-R\). The minimizer used in the normalization is \(x_\star=0\), and the initial distance remains \(1\). Replacing that distance by the ambient distance to \((0,0)\), or updating the extra coordinate, would change the problem.

All steps above concern a single static globally smooth convex function. Zero steps cause no difficulty in (20), and arbitrarily large finite steps merely increase the explicitly tracked \(A_t\).

The unresolved theorem-level step is now precise. A positive resolution still requires selecting the affine functions in (5), and \(R\), from an arbitrary schedule so that
\[
2F_R(x_n^{(R)})\ge\frac{1-\varepsilon}{B_n}
\]
for every sufficiently large \(n\), with a threshold independent of the schedule. The construction above instead starts from an already existing adverse run; it does not produce that lower bound. Ordinary squared-distance constructions cannot fill this gap, by (1)–(4). No improving full-class upper certificate has been obtained either.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000523 -->
# Direct Codex analytical research call — UNVERIFIED

The human explicitly authorized direct Codex reasoning in addition to Danus worker searches: "可以直接使用codex 先进行推理一些结果". They now require gpt-6-astra and ultra. This overrides the default main-agent no-mathematics rule for THIS isolated analytical call. You are a mathematical researcher for this call, not the Danus main orchestrator.

One invocation only. Work from the complete original problem below. No tools, subagents, mathematical programs, numerical experiments, formal submissions, installations, or external communications. Your final mathematical text is exported by the driver; do not write files yourself. Only a later verifier-accepted fact can establish correctness. Label every new result UNVERIFIED, distinguish complete proof from conditional reduction, and state precise quantifiers and missing theorem-level steps.

Seek a materially different path to the original horizon-by-horizon exact minimax profile. Think across probability, polynomial methods, finite fields, combinatorial decompositions, and smooth convex geometry where useful. Do not force these labels onto a familiar checkpoint argument. Explore several mechanisms privately, then develop the strongest substantive derivation fully. Try to resolve the original problem; if it remains open, deliver an explicit proved candidate intermediate result or a concrete obstruction with full reasoning and identify exactly why it does not yet resolve the target. A vague research agenda is not sufficient.

Current verified scope: the project has 15 accepted facts but NO accepted original-target solution. Several prescribed hard-function classes are insufficient on particular small-amplitude silver-shaped words; that never gives a full-class worst-case upper bound. A recently accepted static multi-stage completion construction handles its own growing/padded special word families and exceeds the exact benchmark there, while their all-quadratic and universal-lens screens are weak. Thus polynomial-only and finite-library screens need additional nonlinear information. No all-word constant-preserving decomposition or complete improving full-class certificate is known. Do not assume one as routine.

Useful exact benchmark notation, equivalent to the supplied recursion: C_n=S_circle(n)+1, B_n=2*S_bullet(n)+1, C_0=B_0=1, U_n=1/B_n. For every n>=1, C_n=max_{k+l=n-1}(C_k+C_l+sqrt(C_k^2+6*C_k*C_l+C_l^2))/2 and B_n=max_{k+l=n-1}(2*C_k+B_l/2+sqrt(B_l^2+8*C_k*B_l)/2), with ALL integer splits. Keep W=2R. Zero, huge, unordered steps and every sufficiently large actual horizon remain essential. One static globally convex smooth function must realize every query of a lower-bound run. A negative answer needs a rigorous full-class upper certificate, not merely weak known adversaries.

Full original problem follows verbatim.

# Exact Constant Matching Lower Bound for Predetermined-Step Gradient Descent

Resolve the following minimax problem completely.

The objective is not merely to recover the silver exponent. The goal is to determine whether the best possible predetermined nonnegative stepsize schedule for gradient descent matches, asymptotically and with the correct leading constant/profile, the objective-value schedule constructed by Zehao Zhang and Rujun Jiang.

Throughout, “complexity” means the convergence/iteration-complexity rate as a function of the number of gradient steps \(n\), not the computational complexity of constructing a stepsize schedule.

---

## 1. Optimization model

Let \(L>0\), and let \(\mathcal C^{1,1}_L\) denote the class of all finite-dimensional convex differentiable functions

\[
f:\mathbb R^d\to\mathbb R
\]

whose gradient is \(L\)-Lipschitz and which possess at least one minimizer \(x_\star\).

For a predetermined nonnegative stepsize schedule

\[
h=(h_0,\ldots,h_{n-1})\in[0,\infty)^n,
\]

gradient descent is

\[
x_{t+1}
=
x_t-\frac{h_t}{L}\nabla f(x_t),
\qquad
t=0,\ldots,n-1.
\]

The schedule may depend on the horizon \(n\), but it must be chosen before seeing \(f\), \(x_0\), gradients, function values, or any other oracle information.

Define the normalized worst-case objective error

\[
\mathcal W_n(h)
:=
\sup
\frac{2\bigl(f(x_n)-f_\star\bigr)}
{L\|x_0-x_\star\|^2},
\]

where the supremum is over

- every finite dimension \(d\);
- every \(f\in\mathcal C^{1,1}_L\);
- every minimizer \(x_\star\in\arg\min f\);
- every \(x_0\neq x_\star\).

Define the minimax value

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n^\star
:=
\inf_{h\in[0,\infty)^n}\mathcal W_n(h).
}
\]

The normalization is chosen so that it agrees directly with the constants in Zhang–Jiang.

If instead one uses the normalization

\[
\mathcal R_n(h)
=
\sup
\frac{f(x_n)-f_\star}{L\|x_0-x_\star\|^2},
\]

then

\[
\mathcal W_n(h)=2\mathcal R_n(h).
\]

Do not mix the two normalizations.

---

## 2. The Zhang–Jiang benchmark

Write

\[
\rho
:=
\log_2(1+\sqrt2)
=
1.271553303\ldots.
\]

Define

\[
\varphi(x,y)
=
\frac{
-x-y+\sqrt{(x+y+2)^2+4(x+1)(y+1)}
}{2},
\]

and

\[
\psi(x,y)
=
\frac{
3-2y+\sqrt{(2y+1)(2y+8x+9)}
}{4}.
\]

Define two scalar sequences recursively.

First,

\[
S_\circ(0)=0,
\]

and for every \(n\ge1\),

\[
\boxed{
S_\circ(n)
=
\max_{0\le k<n}
\Bigl[
S_\circ(k)
+
\varphi\!\bigl(S_\circ(k),S_\circ(n-k-1)\bigr)
+
S_\circ(n-k-1)
\Bigr].
}
\]

Second,

\[
S_\bullet(0)=0,
\]

and for every \(n\ge1\),

\[
\boxed{
S_\bullet(n)
=
\max_{0\le k<n}
\Bigl[
S_\circ(k)
+
\psi\!\bigl(S_\circ(k),S_\bullet(n-k-1)\bigr)
+
S_\bullet(n-k-1)
\Bigr].
}
\]

These are the sums of the primitive and dominant schedules generated by the Zhang–Jiang concatenation dynamic program.

Let \(h_\bullet^{(n)}\) be any Zhang–Jiang dominant schedule attaining \(S_\bullet(n)\).

Their analysis gives the exact worst-case objective profile

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n\!\left(h_\bullet^{(n)}\right)
=
\frac{1}{2S_\bullet(n)+1}.
}
\]

The equality is important: the associated Huber instance attains the bound.

Define

\[
\boxed{
U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}
:=
\frac{1}{2S_\bullet(n)+1}.
}
\]

Consequently,

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star
\le
U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}
\qquad
\text{for every }n.
\]

This exact sequence, rather than the decimal \(0.4239\), is the benchmark that must be matched.

---

## 3. Exact problem to resolve

Determine whether

\[
\boxed{
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}
{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
=
1.
}
\tag{P}
\]

Equivalently, determine whether

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n^\star
\ge
\frac{1-o(1)}
{2S_\bullet(n)+1}.
}
\tag{LB}
\]

Since the reverse inequality

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star
\le
\frac{1}{2S_\bullet(n)+1}
\]

is already supplied by the Zhang–Jiang schedule, proving (LB) establishes asymptotic minimax optimality of their schedule horizon by horizon.

The quantifiers in (LB) are strict:

for every \(\varepsilon>0\), there must exist \(N_\varepsilon<\infty\) such that for every \(n\ge N_\varepsilon\) and every predetermined schedule \(h\in[0,\infty)^n\),

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n(h)
\ge
\frac{1-\varepsilon}
{2S_\bullet(n)+1}.
}
\tag{Q}
\]

The threshold \(N_\varepsilon\) must be independent of the particular schedule \(h\).

The adversarial function and its dimension may depend on \(n\) and on \(h\).

---

## 4. What “matching the complexity and constant” means

A complete affirmative solution must prove (P), not merely the silver exponent.

In particular, since

\[
S_\bullet(n)=\Theta(n^\rho),
\]

(P) automatically implies the optimal polynomial convergence exponent

\[
\rho=\log_2(1+\sqrt2).
\]

It also matches the complete asymptotic constant profile, including any genuine oscillation of \(S_\bullet(n)/n^\rho\).

For reference, define

\[
\bar\nu_\bullet
:=
\liminf_{n\to\infty}
\frac{S_\bullet(n)}{n^\rho}
\]

and

\[
C_{\mathrm{ZJ}}
:=
\frac{1}{2\bar\nu_\bullet}.
\]

Then

\[
\limsup_{n\to\infty}
n^\rho U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}
=
C_{\mathrm{ZJ}}.
\]

Therefore (P) implies

\[
\boxed{
\limsup_{n\to\infty}
n^\rho\mathcal W_n^\star
=
C_{\mathrm{ZJ}}.
}
\]

However, proving only this limsup identity is weaker than (P) and does **not** count as a complete affirmative solution.

Likewise, proving merely

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star=\Theta(n^{-\rho})
\]

does not count.

---

## 5. Important warning about the number \(0.4239\)

Do not formulate the theorem as

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star
\sim
0.4239\,n^{-\rho}.
\]

That is not the precise Zhang–Jiang theorem.

Their rigorous asymptotic statement is expressed through

\[
\bar\nu_\bullet
=
\liminf_{n\to\infty}
S_\bullet(n)/n^\rho
\]

and the coefficient

\[
(2\bar\nu_\bullet)^{-1}.
\]

The frequently quoted number \(0.4239\) comes from an additional numerical estimate for \(\bar\nu_\bullet\), and is not itself the exact rigorously identified asymptotic constant.

The paper also notes numerical evidence for still smaller constants.

Thus:

- \(0.4239\) may be used as a numerical sanity check;
- it may not be assumed as an exact constant;
- a proof that only matches \(0.4239\) numerically is not sufficient;
- the rigorous benchmark is \(U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}=(2S_\bullet(n)+1)^{-1}\).

---

## 6. Current lower-bound benchmark

A recent lower bound of Ye and Liu considers, after setting \(L=1\),

\[
\mathcal R_n(h)
=
\sup_{f,x_0}
\frac{f(x_n)-f_\star}
{\|x_0-x_\star\|^2},
\qquad
r_n^\star
=
\inf_{h\ge0}\mathcal R_n(h),
\]

and proves that for some absolute \(C>0\),

\[
r_n^\star
\ge
n^{-\left(
\rho+
C\sqrt{\frac{\log\log n}{\log n}}
\right)}
\]

for all sufficiently large \(n\).

Equivalently, in the normalization of this problem,

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star
\ge
2
n^{-\left(
\rho+
C\sqrt{\frac{\log\log n}{\log n}}
\right)}.
\]

This establishes the correct polynomial exponent only up to a subpolynomial loss.

It does **not** establish (P).

Removing the exponent loss alone is also not sufficient: the leading asymptotic constant/profile must be matched.

---

## 7. Existing hard-function tool

The Ye–Liu lower bound may be used as a starting point.

For checkpoints

\[
1\le t_1<\cdots<t_k\le n,
\]

write

\[
b_i=h_{t_i},
\qquad
s_i
=
\sum_{j=t_{i-1}+1}^{t_i-1}h_j,
\]

with \(t_0=0\), and let \(s_{k+1}\) denote the sum of the steps after the last checkpoint.

For suitable parameters \(\epsilon,\gamma>0\), putting

\[
c=\sqrt{1+2\gamma+\epsilon^2},
\]

their construction yields constants \(a=a(\epsilon,\gamma)\) and \(B_0=B_0(\epsilon)\) such that, when every checkpoint obeys \(b_i\ge B_0\),

\[
\mathcal R_n(h)
\ge
\frac{1}{4(1+s_{k+1})}
\prod_{i=1}^k
\left[
\frac{b_i}
{c(a+s_i)+\epsilon b_i}
\right]^2.
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n(h)
\ge
\frac{1}{2(1+s_{k+1})}
\prod_{i=1}^k
\left[
\frac{b_i}
{c(a+s_i)+\epsilon b_i}
\right]^2.
}
\tag{Y--L}
\]

You may sharpen, replace, or completely abandon this construction.

Do not assume that merely optimizing its existing parameters is sufficient.

A central obstruction in the current proof is that the auxiliary constant \(a(\epsilon,\gamma)\) deteriorates rapidly when parameters are sent toward the regime needed to recover the exact silver exponent. Any proposed constant-level proof must track such losses quantitatively and cannot hide them inside \(o(1)\) unless that \(o(1)\) is genuinely negligible relative to \(U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}\).

---

## 8. Acceptable complete resolutions

Assume for purposes of this task that the question admits a rigorous resolution, but do not assume in advance that the answer to (P) is affirmative.

### Affirmative resolution

Prove

\[
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}
{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
=
1.
\]

Because the upper bound is known, the substantive requirement is the uniform lower bound (Q).

A complete proof must apply to every sufficiently large horizon \(n\) and every predetermined nonnegative schedule of length \(n\).

### Negative resolution

Prove rigorously that (P) is false.

For example, it is sufficient to establish that there exist

\[
\delta>0,
\qquad
n_j\to\infty,
\qquad
h^{(j)}\in[0,\infty)^{n_j},
\]

such that

\[
\mathcal W_{n_j}\!\left(h^{(j)}\right)
\le
(1-\delta)
U_{n_j}^{\mathrm{ZJ}}
\]

for every \(j\), with a rigorous worst-case upper-bound certificate.

Equivalently, it is sufficient to prove

\[
\liminf_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}
{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
<1.
\]

Numerical evidence is not a negative resolution.

Failure to prove the lower bound is not evidence that it is false.

---

## 9. Results that do not count as a solution

Do **not** stop for any of the following:

- reproducing the known \(n^{-\rho-o(1)}\) lower bound;
- proving \(n^{-\rho-\varepsilon}\) for every fixed \(\varepsilon>0\);
- proving only that \(\rho\) is the optimal polynomial exponent;
- proving \(\mathcal W_n^\star=\Theta(n^{-\rho})\);
- obtaining an unspecified positive constant times \(n^{-\rho}\);
- obtaining a constant strictly smaller than the Zhang–Jiang benchmark;
- proving the desired constant only along \(n=2^m-1\);
- proving it along any other subsequence;
- proving only a limsup or liminf lower bound;
- proving only
  \[
  \limsup n^\rho\mathcal W_n^\star=C_{\mathrm{ZJ}};
  \]
- showing that a candidate hard instance works for the silver schedule but not for every schedule;
- restricting stepsizes to a bounded interval;
- assuming stepsizes are monotone, ordered, separated, or bounded away from zero;
- assuming the schedule has a prescribed recursive/composable form;
- assuming all large steps can be selected as checkpoints without proving this;
- proving a lower bound only for Zhang–Jiang's class of concatenated schedules;
- proving a lower bound only for “basic”, “composable”, periodic, symmetric, or silver-like schedules;
- replacing nonnegative schedules by positive schedules without treating zero steps;
- permitting the adversary to choose the schedule after seeing the function;
- allowing adaptive stepsizes, momentum, averaging, restarting, or additional state;
- proving a result for gradient norm rather than last-iterate objective gap;
- solving the anytime version instead of the finite-horizon non-anytime problem;
- changing the performance normalization;
- dropping the factor \(1/2\) in the Zhang–Jiang normalization;
- treating \(0.4239\) as an exact proven constant;
- using floating-point calculations as proof of an asymptotic constant;
- producing a stronger lower bound than the Zhang–Jiang upper bound without noticing the resulting contradiction;
- hiding a subpolynomial loss inside a claimed constant;
- obtaining a bound of the form
  \[
  n^{-\rho}\exp[-o(\log n)]
  \]
  rather than
  \[
  (1-o(1))U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}};
  \]
- proving finite-\(n\) inequalities for finitely many computed horizons;
- providing only a conjectured extremal schedule;
- providing only a conjectured hard function;
- reducing the problem to an unproved statement of essentially the same minimax strength;
- reporting a promising lemma, numerical pattern, or “substantial progress”.

Intermediate positive progress is not a stopping condition.

---

## 10. Proof standards

Every invoked theorem must be stated with the hypotheses needed here.

In particular, carefully verify:

- convexity;
- \(L\)-smoothness;
- existence of a minimizer;
- the exact GD recursion;
- nonnegativity of all stepsizes;
- the distinction between a schedule chosen for a fixed horizon and an infinite anytime schedule;
- the normalization by \(L\|x_0-x_\star\|^2/2\);
- uniformity over every schedule \(h\in[0,\infty)^n\);
- whether constants are absolute or parameter dependent;
- every use of \(o(1)\), including its sign and uniformity;
- every limit interchange;
- every passage between \(\mathcal R_n\) and \(\mathcal W_n\);
- every use of finite-dimensional interpolation;
- every claim that a constructed collection of values and gradients is realizable by a convex \(L\)-smooth function.

A lower-bound construction may depend on \(n\) and on the entire predetermined schedule \(h\), because the worst-case function is chosen after the schedule.

Its dimension may grow with \(n\).

---

## 11. Promising proof families to investigate

Use genuinely different approaches rather than repeatedly varying one incomplete argument.

Relevant families include:

- exact smooth-convex interpolation and primal PEP constructions;
- dual PEP certificates and complementary slackness;
- deriving a dual dynamic program to the Zhang–Jiang concatenation recursion;
- minimax/Bellman formulations in which \(\varphi\) and \(\psi\) arise from primal-dual optimality;
- hard functions whose recursive composition exactly mirrors ConPP/ConPD;
- sharper checkpoint constructions without the \(\epsilon\)-dependent constant explosion;
- curved-gradient or Moreau-envelope constructions with exact rather than approximate transfer factors;
- piecewise quadratic or Huber-type interpolation constructions;
- finite-dimensional resisting-oracle constructions;
- composition/duality of extremal first-order methods;
- exact characterization of the PEP value for arbitrary nonnegative stepsizes;
- identification of a scalar functional \(V(h)\) satisfying both a universal lower bound and the same concatenation recursion as \(S_\bullet\);
- structural reductions proving that an arbitrary schedule can be decomposed at essentially no constant loss;
- induction on \(n\) with a sharp split inequality;
- continuous scaling limits of the recursion, but only when converted back to rigorous discrete estimates with the correct constant;
- exact algebraic or semialgebraic certificates for small horizons that reveal the correct general recursion.

Do not assume one of these approaches must succeed.

---

## 12. Multiagent search protocol

Use multiagent v2 aggressively and dynamically, with up to four concurrent agents if available.

Begin with a genuinely diverse portfolio.

Do not tell most early agents what the currently favored proof route is.

Maintain an explicit registry of mathematical approach families, organized by actual mechanism rather than wording.

If multiple agents converge to the same checkpoint construction, PEP dual, or recurrence argument, redirect some agents to genuinely different formulations.

Keep both possibilities alive:

1. the Zhang–Jiang schedule is asymptotically minimax optimal;
2. another schedule beats its constant/profile.

Do not discard the second possibility merely because the exponents match.

When an approach reaches a missing lemma whose strength is essentially equivalent to (Q), mark the route as blocked. Do not call that lemma “technical” or “routine”.

Only reopen such a route if a new mechanism appears.

---

## 13. Computational agents

Use computation as an adversarial and discovery tool, not as proof.

For moderate \(n\), agents should attempt to compute or tightly bound:

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star,
\qquad
U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}},
\qquad
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}},
\]

using exact or high-precision PEP/SDP/branch-and-bound methods where possible.

They should also:

- compute \(S_\circ(n)\) and \(S_\bullet(n)\);
- inspect the maximizing split indices;
- search for schedules beating \(h_\bullet^{(n)}\);
- test proposed universal hard-function inequalities;
- search for counterexamples to intermediate lemmas;
- inspect primal and dual PEP optimizers;
- identify rank patterns and active interpolation constraints;
- attempt rational/algebraic reconstruction of numerical certificates;
- verify normalization factors independently.

A numerical counterexample to a proposed lemma is useful and should kill that lemma.

A numerical confirmation of the main conjecture is not a proof.

---

## 14. Adversarial audit

Every proposed complete affirmative proof must be independently attacked for at least the following failure modes:

- it actually proves only the exponent;
- a fixed \(\varepsilon>0\) remains in the exponent;
- \(\varepsilon=\varepsilon_n\to0\) creates a hidden constant blowup;
- it proves only infinitely many horizons;
- it proves only dyadic horizons;
- its \(o(1)\) depends on the schedule;
- its hard function is not globally convex and smooth;
- the interpolation data are inconsistent;
- the minimizer used in the normalization is not valid;
- the dimension is implicitly infinite without justification;
- very large stepsizes are excluded;
- zero stepsizes break the argument;
- a checkpoint-selection lemma silently loses a constant;
- a product inequality loses a nonvanishing factor;
- a factor \(2\) is lost when translating between normalizations;
- the Zhang–Jiang constant is replaced by \(0.4239\);
- a limsup statement is mistaken for horizon-by-horizon asymptotics;
- a numerical observation is used inside a supposedly analytic proof;
- the proof establishes optimality only within a restricted schedule family;
- a PEP dual certificate is feasible only numerically rather than exactly;
- strong duality is invoked without verifying its hypotheses;
- an asymptotic recurrence is substituted for the exact discrete one;
- the final lower bound exceeds a known rigorous upper bound because of a normalization error.

A proof survives only if all such issues are resolved explicitly.

---

## 15. Output requirements

Do not return an exploratory essay.

Do not return a research diary.

Do not return a collection of partial lemmas.

Do not return a “most promising approach”.

Do not return merely because several approaches fail.

Do not return merely because agents agree that the problem is difficult.

Do not return a positive intermediate result.

Return only one of the following:

### A. Complete affirmative proof

A rigorous proof of

\[
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}
{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
=
1,
\]

including every necessary lemma and every constant/normalization check.

### B. Complete negative proof

A rigorous proof that the above limit is not \(1\), with an explicit asymptotic separation or another logically conclusive disproof.

Before returning either answer, subject the argument to an independent adversarial audit and repair every identified gap.

If the argument still contains an unproved lemma of theorem-level strength, the problem is not resolved and you must continue working.

Do not label a missing minimax inequality, uniformity statement, interpolation theorem, structural decomposition, or constant-preserving recursion as “routine”.

The stopping criterion is mathematical completion, not positive progress.

---

## 16. Literature usage

The following sources may be treated as background starting points, but their statements and normalizations must be checked carefully:

- Zehao Zhang and Rujun Jiang, *Accelerated Gradient Descent by Concatenation of Stepsize Schedules*, arXiv:2410.12395 / SIAM Journal on Optimization.
- Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, *Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent*, arXiv:2609.09152 and the accompanying lower-bound exposition.
- standard smooth-convex interpolation and PEP results;
- standard results concerning the silver stepsize schedule and concatenation constructions.

Public search may be used for standard named theorems, definitions, and the cited papers.

Do not use public search merely to locate a posted solution to this exact benchmark.

The task is to produce and verify the mathematical resolution itself.

# Handoff: strictly damped correlated Gray law remains open in a narrowed domain

**JOINT UNVERIFIED. Original minimax Goal OPEN.**
This ONE distinct allocation is complete on return. ONE new substantive
law was examined; no second mechanism was begun. The mathematical task
was not completed: no legal initialization and terminal gain, and no
full-family impossibility theorem, were obtained. The complete joint
derivations and explicit scope limitations are in this directory's
`RESULT.md`. All old artifacts remain frozen.

## Unchanged target and current scope

The original target is W_n^star/U_n^ZJ ->1 with
W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), W=2R,
and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. An affirmative lower bound must be uniform over
every sufficiently large n and every predetermined nonnegative word.
A separating subsequence requires a full-function worst-case upper
certificate. The results here do neither.

Fix the SAME m>=1536, N=2^m, n=N-1,
eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,
H_l=1+a c^l, h_i=H_(nu_2(i+1)), S=sum h_i, D_m=1+2S.
No child resets m. The task-supplied B_n/D_m<47/50 is an UPPER
comparison and was not used to invent a lower gain.

## The full correlated dictionary and the attempted law

Let tau_k=N-2^k, 0<=k<m, and Kmix=N-1-m. Every nonzero character
t of F_2^m is present, with

    b_(tau_k)^2=2^k/(N-1),
    b_t^2=zeta/(N^2 Kmix) on all other nonzero labels,
    U_s(t)=b_t(-1)^(t dot s), 0<zeta<=1,
    M=1+zeta/N^2, T=M+R^(-2), R>0,
    w_s=(U_s,1/R), Gamma(i)=i xor floor(i/2),
    v_i=sigma_i w_(Gamma(i)),
    D=conv(0,v_0,...,v_n).

Direct Walsh orthogonality proves this is a full-dimensional simplex
in R^N. The static potential F_D(z)=max_(g in D)(<z,g>-||g||^2/2)
has gradient P_D, is convex and 1-smooth, and has the COMPLETE minimum

    C^polar={(x,y): <x,U_s>+y/R<=0 for every s},
    C=cone{v_i}.

The cone is independent of the positive radii. The supplied
z_star=(0,-R) is a strict polar minimum but need not be closest.

Telescoping Gray bits proves chi_(tau_k)(Gamma(i))=(-1)^(bit_k(i)).
The exact marginal and intersection energies are

    E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)
          +zeta(N/2-l-1)/(N^2 Kmix),
    J_01=1/(N-1)+zeta(N/4-1)/(N^2 Kmix),
    E_1-2J_01=1/(N-1).

Let rho_l be the positive root of

    r^2+(H_l-2)(1-2E_l/T)r+1-H_l=0.

The attempted strict law is

    r_l=rho_l(1-delta_l/N), l=0,1,
    r_l=1+theta(rho_l-1), l>=2,
    sigma_0=1, sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i r_(nu_2(i+1)),
    0<delta_0,delta_1<=16, 0<=theta<=1.

Parameters may depend explicitly on m and are fixed before the run.
Every factor is positive because N>16. All adjacent necessary firm
inequalities hold, strictly at the two short levels. This has NOT
been promoted to global legality.

## Required main screen, jointly checked

If both short levels instead use their undamped roots, original
queries 1,2,3, with physical flips 1 then 0, are inconsistent.
Writing u=rho_1, v=rho_0, e=E_1, f=E_0 and starting scale s>0,
the two root equalities force the original final-to-first normal to
equal

    s^2u[(a+v)A+2vf(1-u)-4vJ_01],
    A=2e beta(1+u)/(u+beta)>2e beta.

This is positive since
2beta E_1-4J_01=2/(N-1)-2eps E_1>0. The strict law breaks both
orthogonality equalities, so this is only a motivation audit; it is
NOT a proof against all positive damping.

## New screen A: a whole-domain sum of original zero normals

If R^(-2)>=M, every frame Gram entry is nonnegative. For a legal
prescribed run from z_0=(x_0,-R), set P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k v_k.
The original zero-vertex normal at query i, divided by sigma_i,
requires

    <z_0,w_(Gamma(i))>
       >=<P_i,w_(Gamma(i))>+sigma_i T>0.

Sum over ALL N original queries, including the terminal. Walsh row
sums make the left side -N, a contradiction. Therefore

    R^(-2)<M

is necessary for the prescribed full itinerary, for any positive
radii. This screen uses the initial vertical slice and all terminal
normal rows. It does not claim impossibility if the prescribed
terminal vertex is omitted.

## New screen B: a remote original firm pair

Let j=floor(m/2), L=2^j, Q=sigma_L, s=sigma_(L-1), and let B be
the orthogonal diagonal sign reflection associated with Gamma(L).
It flips dominant coordinate tau_j only, plus some mixed coordinates,
and leaves the vertical coordinate fixed. Exact dyadic counting gives

    Q=r_j product_(l<j)r_l^(L/2^(l+1)),
    v_(L+i)=Q B v_i for 0<=i<L.

Set p=v_(L-1), P=sum_(i=0)^(L-2)h_i v_i, and
S_j=sum_(i=0)^(L-2)h_i. The ORIGINAL displacement to query 2L-1 is

    z_(L-1)-z_(2L-1)=H_j p+Q BP,
    v_(2L-1)=Q Bp.

Writing +/- for B's eigenspaces, the firm-pair residual that must
be NONNEGATIVE is exactly

    F=(1-Q)[(H_j-1+Q)||p^+||^2+Q<P^+,p^+>]
       +(1+Q)[(H_j-1-Q)||p^-||^2-Q<P^-,p^->].

The proof in RESULT.md gives the uniform, explicit bounds

    -1/16<=log sigma_i<=1/16 for 0<=i<=L,
    <P^+,p^+>>0,
    <P^-,p^->/||p^-||^2 >=(49/64)S_j>(3/4)S_j,
    H_j<(3/4)S_j.

The minus-space lower bound uses its sole dominant energy
2^j/(N-1), all first-block signs in that coordinate being positive,
and the TOTAL mixed energy at most zeta/N^2. Every physical child
step, including its final leaf, is included in P. Thus when Q>=1
the first term is nonpositive and the second strictly negative:
F<0. This is an original remote-pair contradiction independent of
x_0, not a local equal-root argument.

In the surviving domain R^(-2)<M one has T<9/4. Elementary exact
root bounds give

    log r_0>=-22/N, log r_1>-18/N,
    log r_l>=(2/3)theta 2^l/N for 4<=l<=j.

Consequently

    log Q >=(L/N)[theta(j-2)/3-16].

This proves the uniform parameter exclusion

    theta(j-2)>=48 ==> impossible.

Any surviving prescribed law must therefore satisfy STRICTLY

    R^(-2)<M,
    0<=theta<48/(floor(m/2)-2).

For a fixed positive theta, all sufficiently large m are excluded.
Explicit selections theta_m=O(1/m), including theta=0, remain outside
this obstruction. No claim closes them.

## The full damping product is not asymptotically fatal by itself

Exact physical counts give the additional damping factor

    D_add=(1-delta_0/N)^(N/2)(1-delta_1/N)^(N/4),

with

    exp[-(delta_0/2+delta_1/4)/(1-16/N)]
       <=D_add<=exp[-delta_0/2-delta_1/4].

In the declared domain D_add>exp(-13). The undamped two-short-root
product is greater than exp(-3), and all higher radial ratios are
at least one. Thus the complete prescribed terminal radius satisfies

    sigma_n>exp(-16).

This is a constant bound on radii, not a legality result or a paid
performance bound. It rules out the shortcut of declaring failure
only because O(1/N) damping occurs O(N) times.

## What remains unresolved and must not be advertised as done

Within R^(-2)<M and theta<48/(floor(m/2)-2), no explicit x_0 has been
proved to realize all prescribed projections. Its exact closest-cone
distance and terminal objective comparison are also missing. Were
the prescribed run legal, its actual vertical coordinate would be

    (z_i)_vertical=-R-R^(-1)sum_(k<i)h_k sigma_k,

and its attained score would have numerator

    2<(x_0,-R),v_n>-2 sum_(i<n)h_i<v_i,v_n>-||v_n||^2,

divided by dist((x_0,-R),C^polar)^2, not automatically ||x_0||^2.
These are diagnostic identities, not the missing construction.
Positive origin-normal cap height must also be distinguished from
slack relative to one convenient polar minimum.

The first law was not fully closed, so no second profile or transverse
inverse terminal was begun. The previous maximum-radius-ray result
was not recycled. There is no all-simplex, full-W, or minimax theorem
here. The original goal remains OPEN and the bounded allocation ends.

# Correlated-character repair still needs strict short-step margins

MAIN-AUTHORED UNVERIFIED derivation. Original target OPEN. No program was used.
This is not part of the completed native two-law allocation.

Use the SAME m>=1536 hierarchy, N=2^m, K=N-1-m,
beta=1-1/(4m), a=beta/[(1+sqrt2)(1+1/(4m))].
Let dominant character labels be tau_k=N-2^k,0<=k<m, with weights
b_(tau_k)^2=2^k/(N-1). Give every remaining nonzero character weight
zeta/(N^2 K),0<zeta<=1. All characters remain present. Set
U_s=(b_t chi_t(s))_(t!=0), M=sum b_t^2=1+zeta/N^2,
T=M+R^(-2), w_s=(U_s,1/R), R>0.

If Gamma(i)=i xor floor(i/2), then
chi_(tau_k)(Gamma(i))=(-1)^(bit_k(i)), because the xor sum of Gray bits
k through m-1 telescopes to bit_k(i). No chronology is permuted.
For a flip of bit l the exact marginal energy is

 E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)
       +zeta*(N/2-l-1)/(N^2 K).

The joint energy for bits0 and1 is
 J=1/(N-1)+zeta*(N/4-1)/(N^2 K),
 E_1-2J=1/(N-1).                                    (1)

The improved overlap may fix the old first triple, but it does not repair
the undamped short-step root law. Consider ORIGINAL queries1,2,3, whose
two physical steps are1+beta and1+a and whose Gray bit flips are1 then0.
Let u be the positive adjacent firm-equality radius root at the first
step and v the corresponding root at the second; both lie in(0,1).
Write e=E_1,f=E_0, and retain the positive overall initial radius scale s.
Both adjacent equality conditions force both corresponding projection
normal rows to be0. The remote query-3 normal against the query-1 vertex
is then exactly

 B=s^2*u*[(a+v)A+2v f(1-u)-4vJ],
 A=T(u-1)+2e=2e*beta*(1+u)/(u+beta)>2e*beta.         (2)

To rederive(2), use three unscaled frame vectors W0,W1,W2 with inner
products T-2e,T-2f,T-2e-2f+4J. Set g0=s W0,g1=s u W1,g2=s u v W2.
At the middle point the residual is orthogonal to both differences.
The last residual differs by -a*g1-g2. Its normal against g0 is
(a*g1+g2) dot(g2-g0), which expands to(2), using the second root
equality. The first root equation rearranges as
(1-u)(u+beta)=2(1-beta)e*u/T, yielding the expression for A.

Every omitted factor is positive, so B>s^2*u*v*(2beta E_1-4J).
Equation(1) gives

 2beta E_1-4J=2/(N-1)-2(1-beta)E_1>0.

Indeed K>=4 and zeta<=1 imply E_1<4/(N-1), while1-beta<=1/6144.
Thus B>0 for every declared R,zeta,m. This is a uniform original-row
contradiction, independent of every later radius choice and the initial
point. It applies only when BOTH low-level radius ratios retain their
adjacent equality roots. It does not exclude strict short-step damping,
different overlaps or a different actual itinerary.

The next proposed law must therefore alter at least one of these low-level
equalities, rather than claim that correlated characters alone fixed the
global constraints. Feasibility and payoff of a damped law remain OPEN.

# Strict-margin correlated Gray radii: two uniform necessary screens, remaining law unresolved

**JOINT UNVERIFIED RESULT.** Project `gd-exact-profile-20260926`.
This is the complete report of ONE DISTINCT bounded analytical allocation,
after the completed `direct_gray_radial_vertices_20261003` allocation.
Exactly ONE new substantive mechanism was examined: the specified strictly
damped correlated-character radial itinerary. No second mechanism was
begun. The first law has NOT been completely excluded or made into a
legal quantitative witness. The new scoped conclusions below are joint
draft derivations, not verifier-accepted facts.

The new results are as follows.

1. The task's undamped correlated-character screen is confirmed jointly:
   original queries 1,2,3 are inconsistent if both short-level ratios are
   their firm-equality roots. This does not transfer to strict damping.
2. For the strictly damped law, the entire parameter domain R^(-2)>=M
   is impossible, by summing all ORIGINAL zero-vertex normal rows.
3. Independently of the initialization, a remote ORIGINAL firm pair at
   query indices 2^j-1 and 2^(j+1)-1, j=floor(m/2), is inconsistent
   whenever sigma_(2^j)>=1. In the remaining domain R^(-2)<M, an explicit
   all-m estimate makes this a parameter exclusion:

       theta (floor(m/2)-2)>=48  ==>  no prescribed trajectory.

   Thus any selection legal for every m would necessarily have
   theta_m<48/(floor(m/2)-2), not a fixed positive theta.
4. The total added damping is exactly a product bounded below by a
   positive absolute constant. In fact the entire terminal radius ratio
   satisfies sigma_n>exp(-16) throughout the declared parameter family.
   Counting the short steps does NOT prove exponential loss in m or N.

What remains is genuine: 0<R^(-2)<M and
0<=theta<48/(floor(m/2)-2), with both positive damping parameters allowed,
have not been made feasible or excluded. No explicit legal x_0, exact
closest-radius terminal lower bound, or gain over the COMPLETE channel
has been proved there. Ending this bounded allocation is not a claim
that the requested mathematical search objective was achieved.

## 1. Preserved original target and declared parameters

The original minimax problem remains

    W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2),
    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i),
    W_n^star=inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h), W=2R,
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n=1/(2S_bullet(n)+1).

The affirmative target is W_n^star/U_n^ZJ -> 1, with one threshold for
EVERY sufficiently large n and EVERY predetermined nonnegative word of
length n. A negative resolution requires a full-function upper
certificate proving a nonvanishing separation on an unbounded
subsequence. Neither restricted witness infeasibility nor an attained
witness score is that certificate. The complete original PROBLEM.md
was read and is unchanged. Its integer-split benchmark and original
normalization are not replaced.

Fix the SAME m>=1536 in every subword and define

    N=2^m, n=N-1, eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,
    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,
    H_l=1+a c^l, h_i=H_(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n,
    S=sum h_i, D_m=1+2S.

Elementary bounds used below are

    0<eps<1/100, beta>99/100, 2<c<5/2,
    1/3<a<1/2, H_0=1+a<2, H_1=1+beta<2,
    H_l>2 for l>=2, H_l>6 for l>=4.

For example 1+sqrt(2)<49/20 and 1+eps<101/100 prove c<5/2;
a>99/250>1/3. Also a c^2=beta c>1, while for l>=4,
a c^l>2^l/3>=16/3, giving the claimed signs.

The strict law fixes parameters BEFORE the run:

    R>0, 0<zeta<=1, 0<delta_0,delta_1<=16, 0<=theta<=1,

possibly as explicit functions of m. Since N>16, all factors
1-delta_l/N are strictly positive. No parameter is fit to gradients
observed during the run.

The COMPLETE K/cut reference contains the full-word Huber term 1/D_m;
the task-supplied accepted comparison B_n/D_m<47/50 is an UPPER
comparison. No lower gain is inferred by reversing it. In fact no use
of that comparison is required for any new conclusion in this report.

## 2. Correlated characters, real metric, and one static body

For each k=0,...,m-1 let tau_k=N-2^k, the character label whose bits
k through m-1 are 1. Let Kmix=N-1-m, and for all nonzero character
labels t in F_2^m define

    b_(tau_k)^2=2^k/(N-1),
    b_t^2=zeta/(N^2 Kmix) for the remaining labels,
    U_s(t)=b_t (-1)^(t dot s).

There are exactly m distinct dominant labels. Every other nonzero
label remains present with strictly positive weight. Let

    M=sum_(t!=0)b_t^2=1+zeta/N^2,
    T=M+R^(-2), w_s=(U_s,1/R).

These are real Euclidean vectors, with the vertical contribution fully
included. Walsh orthogonality follows by pairing labels differing in
one bit:

    sum_s chi_t(s)=0 for nonzero t,
    sum_s chi_t(s)chi_u(s)=N*1_(t=u).

Including the constant row proves the N by N full Walsh matrix is
invertible. Thus the N vectors w_s are linearly independent, as all
coordinate weights and 1/R are positive. Positive radial rescaling
preserves independence. With Gamma(i)=i xor floor(i/2), set

    v_i=sigma_i w_(Gamma(i)), 0<=i<=n,
    D=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n}, C=cone{v_0,...,v_n},
    F_D(z)=max_(g in D) {<z,g>-||g||^2/2}.

This is one full-dimensional simplex in R^N, not an expected Gram or
a changing collection of functions. No universality representation is
used as a proof premise. The cone is independent of the positive
radii and is closed because it is an invertible linear image of the
nonnegative orthant. Its full polar is

    C^polar={(x,y): <x,U_s>+y/R<=0 for EVERY original s}.

Completing the square makes the unique maximizer P_D(z). Its exact
characterization is

    g in D, <z-g,w-g><=0 for EVERY vertex w of D, including 0. (2.1)

The usual two-normal addition directly gives

    <z-z',P_D(z)-P_D(z')> >= ||P_D(z)-P_D(z')||^2.       (2.2)

Comparing the two maximizing affine expressions gives differentiability
with gradient P_D; (2.2) gives 1-Lipschitz continuity. F_D is finite
and convex, and on the open interior of D it equals ||z||^2/2, so its
least Lipschitz gradient constant is exactly 1.

The FULL minimum is

    argmin F_D=C^polar, min F_D=0.                       (2.3)

Indeed the value is zero on the polar. Conversely, if <z,v_i>>0,
then a sufficiently small positive multiple of v_i in D makes the
defining objective strictly positive. In particular z_star=(0,-R)
is a strict polar point, with <z_star,v_i>=-sigma_i. For the required
slice z_0=(x_0,-R), this particular minimum has distance ||x_0||;
the closest minimum may be different. Multiplying F_D by L>0 gives
the same physical GD run and the same W=2R score under step h_i/L.

## 3. Physical ordering and exact overlap identities

Writing the binary bits of i as i_k, Gray bit l is i_l xor i_(l+1),
where i_m=0. The xor sum of Gray bits k through m-1 telescopes, hence

    chi_(tau_k)(Gamma(i))=(-1)^(i_k).                   (3.1)

Thus the dominant physical coordinates run in binary-counter order;
the actual Gray labels have NOT been chronologically permuted. Also,
if l=nu_2(i+1), incrementing i changes bits 0 through l, whose Gray
changes cancel except at l. Therefore

    Gamma(i+1)=Gamma(i)+e_l.

Among all characters, N/2 have bit l set; among the dominant labels,
exactly tau_0,...,tau_l do. The exact flip energies are consequently

    E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)
          +zeta (N/2-l-1)/(N^2 Kmix).                   (3.2)

For the intersection of physical bits 0 and 1, the only dominant label
is tau_0. Of all characters exactly N/4 have both bits set. Thus

    J=1/(N-1)+zeta (N/4-1)/(N^2 Kmix),
    E_1-2J=1/(N-1).                                    (3.3)

For any labels s,u let E(s+u) denote the sum of b_t^2 on characters
with t dot (s+u)=1. Then

    <w_s,w_u>=T-2E(s+u), ||w_s||^2=T.                  (3.4)

For three successive frames with flips l then k, their pairwise
inner products are T-2E_l, T-2E_k, and
T-2E_l-2E_k+4J_lk. This includes the vertical component in every row.

## 4. Adjacent roots, the jointly checked undamped screen, and strictness

Let t_l=1-2E_l/T. Since 0<E_l<M<T, -1<t_l<1. If a physical step
H_l connects projected gradients p=s w and q=s r w', (2.2), or the
two original normal rows, requires

    r^2+(H_l-2)t_l r+1-H_l<=0.                          (4.1)

Its unique positive root is

    rho_l=[-(H_l-2)t_l+
        sqrt((H_l-2)^2 t_l^2+4(H_l-1))]/2.

Since the polynomial at 1 equals (2-H_l)(1-t_l), one has
0<rho_l<1 at l=0,1 and rho_l>1 at all l>=2.
At equality, the adjacent residuals are both perpendicular to the
gradient difference: subtracting the residuals gives (H_l-1)p+q,
whose inner product with q-p is zero; the two original normals have
opposite required signs and therefore must both be zero.

Here is a joint verification of the task's UNVERIFIED repair screen,
used only as motivation for damping. Original queries 1,2,3 have
physical steps H_1 then H_0 and character flips 1 then 0. Suppose both
ratios are their roots. Put u=rho_1, v=rho_0, e=E_1, f=E_0, and let
s>0 be the overall starting scale at query 1. Write the gradients as
p_0=sW_0, p_1=suW_1, p_2=suvW_2. The middle residual is perpendicular
to both differences. The final original normal against p_0 is forced
to equal

    B=s^2 u[(a+v)A+2vf(1-u)-4vJ],
    A=T(u-1)+2e=2e beta(1+u)/(u+beta)>2e beta.           (4.2)

To check this, use the three inner products in (3.4), the last
residual difference -a p_1-p_2, and the second root equality to replace
(a p_1+p_2) dot (p_2-p_0) by its dot product with p_1-p_0. Expanding
gives the first expression. The first root equation rearranges as
(1-u)(u+beta)=2(1-beta)e u/T, giving the second expression.

All other displayed contributions are positive, so

    B>s^2uv(2beta E_1-4J)
     =s^2uv[2/(N-1)-2eps E_1]>0.

The last sign follows from E_1<4/(N-1), Kmix>=4, zeta<=1 and
eps<=1/6144. This contradicts the original query-3 normal against
the original query-1 vertex. This reconfirms the screen WITHOUT
adopting it as an accepted fact.

The actual NEW substantive law is instead

    r_l=rho_l(1-delta_l/N), l=0,1,
    r_l=1+theta(rho_l-1), 2<=l<m,
    sigma_0=1, sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i r_(nu_2(i+1)).         (4.3)

Both low ratios are strictly between 0 and rho_l, so (4.1) is now
strict at those levels. The two orthogonality equalities used in
(4.2) are lost. No claim that (4.2) remains a contradiction under
arbitrary strict damping is made. All higher ratios belong to
[1,rho_l], so every local adjacent necessary inequality holds.
This local fact is not a whole-word construction.

## 5. Complete exclusion of the acute-generator parameter domain

**Screen A, joint draft.** For every m>=1536 and every strict-law
parameter, if R^(-2)>=M, there is no prescribed trajectory from
z_0=(x_0,-R) having the vertices v_0,...,v_n as all original
projections, including the terminal one. This statement in fact
holds for arbitrary positive radii in this full dictionary.

For any two frames, Cauchy-Schwarz on their horizontal coordinates
gives

    <w_s,w_u>=<U_s,U_u>+R^(-2)>=-M+R^(-2)>=0.

If the prescribed run existed, put
P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k v_k, so z_i=z_0-P_i. Its ORIGINAL normal against
the zero vertex is

    <z_i-v_i,v_i>>=0.

Divide by the positive sigma_i and substitute the run:

    <z_0,w_(Gamma(i))>
       >=<P_i,w_(Gamma(i))>+sigma_i T>0,                (5.1)

because all h_k are positive and all frame Gram entries are
nonnegative. Sum (5.1) over every original i=0,...,n. Gamma is a
bijection and the nontrivial Walsh rows sum to zero, so

    sum_i <z_0,w_(Gamma(i))>
      =<(x_0,-R),(0,N/R)>=-N.

This contradicts its strict positivity. The contradiction is an
explicit sum of original zero-vertex normal rows, with the positive
weights 1/sigma_i. It uses no fitted function values or numerical
feasibility calculation. The terminal row is included; no claim is
made about an itinerary that omits that prescribed terminal vertex.

Hence the only surviving geometric domain is

    0<R^(-2)<M, 1<T<2M<9/4.                            (5.2)

## 6. Whole-word radius product: no exponential-loss shortcut

The number of transitions at level l among the n=N-1 original steps
is exactly N/2^(l+1). Thus the full terminal radius ratio is

    sigma_n=rho_0^(N/2) rho_1^(N/4)
       * product_(l=2)^(m-1) [1+theta(rho_l-1)]^(N/2^(l+1))
       * D_add,
    D_add=(1-delta_0/N)^(N/2)(1-delta_1/N)^(N/4).        (6.1)

For 0<=x<1, integration of 1/(1-u) over [0,x] gives

    -x/(1-x)<=log(1-x)<=-x.

Consequently the EXACT extra damping factor obeys

    exp[-(delta_0/2+delta_1/4)/(1-16/N)]
       <=D_add<=exp[-delta_0/2-delta_1/4].               (6.2)

As N>=512, the lower exponent is greater than -13. This is a
positive absolute lower bound, even when both delta parameters depend
on m. The short-step count does not give a loss tending to zero.

For later bounds, direct counting in (3.2) gives E_0<2/N and E_1<4/N.
For a short step H=1+d, its root identity is

    (1-rho)(rho+d)=2(1-d)E rho/T.

Since T>1, this yields 1-rho_0<=4/N and
1-rho_1<=8eps/N. The logarithmic inequality above gives, for the
present N,

    log rho_0>=-5/N, log rho_1>=-9eps/N.                (6.3)

The first uses 4/(N-4)<=5/N, and the second uses
8eps/(N-8eps)<=9eps/N; both hold uniformly here. Thus the product of
the two undamped short-root contributions is greater than exp(-3).
Every higher-level contribution in (6.1) is at least 1. Combining
these observations proves the all-parameter bound

    sigma_n>exp(-16).                                  (6.4)

This is only a statement about the prescribed radii. It does not
assert the trajectory exists or that its objective score has a
positive asymptotic gain; the initial true distance has not been paid.

## 7. Exact dyadic self-similarity and prefix estimates

Put

    j=floor(m/2), L=2^j, Q=sigma_L, s=sigma_(L-1).

Thus j>=768 and 2L-1 is an original query index strictly inside
the full horizon. No child resets m. For any integer i>=0, the count
of transitions of level l before query i is

    c_l(i)=floor(i/2^l)-floor(i/2^(l+1)).

For 0<=i<L this gives

    sigma_(L+i)=Q sigma_i,
    Q=r_j product_(l=0)^(j-1) r_l^(L/2^(l+1)).          (7.1)

Indeed c_l(L+i)=L/2^(l+1)+c_l(i) for l<j, while the level-j
count is 1 and all higher counts are zero. Also L+i=L xor i, and
the Gray map is linear over bitwise xor. Let B be the real diagonal
sign map associated with Gamma(L)=e_j+e_(j-1), leaving the vertical
coordinate fixed. Then

    B^2=I, B^T=B,
    w_(Gamma(L+i))=B w_(Gamma(i)),
    v_(L+i)=Q B v_i.                                   (7.2)

By (3.1), B flips exactly the dominant coordinate tau_j and leaves
all other dominant coordinates unchanged. Some mixed coordinates
also flip. Their entire squared energy is at most zeta/N^2.

Here are explicit UNIFORM estimates for every prefix index 0<=i<=L.
For l<=j, (3.2) gives E_l<=3*2^l/N. Since L/N<=2^(-j),
E_l<1/4 and t_l>=1/2. At a high level, substituting 1/t_l into the
root polynomial gives 1/t_l^2-1>=0, so

    1<=r_l<=rho_l<=1/t_l,
    rho_l-1<=4E_l<=12*2^l/N, 2<=l<=j.                 (7.3)

In particular rho_l<=9/8. For the short levels, (6.3), and
delta/(N-delta)<=17/N for delta<=16 and N>=272, imply

    log r_0>=-22/N,
    log r_1>=-(17+9eps)/N>-18/N.                       (7.4)

All high-level logarithms are nonnegative. Counts up to i<=L do not
exceed the counts at L. Equations (7.3)-(7.4) therefore give

    -16 L/N <= log sigma_i <= 6(j+1)L/N.               (7.5)

For the lower bound the two short contributions are bounded by
-(22/2+18/4)L/N=-(31/2)L/N>-16L/N. For the upper bound each level
2<=l<j contributes at most 6L/N and level j at most 12L/N; the
displayed coarser bound also covers small prefix indices.

For completeness, 2^j>=j^2 for j>=4 follows by induction from
2j^2>=(j+1)^2. Since j>=768, (7.5) implies

    -1/16<=log sigma_i<=1/16,
    sigma_i/s>=exp(-1/8)>=7/8, 0<=i<=L.                (7.6)

For example 6(j+1)/2^j<=7/j<1/16, and
16/2^j<=16/j^2<1/16. These are symbolic all-j estimates, not an
executed trajectory or a finite horizon check.

## 8. A remote ORIGINAL firm-pair obstruction when Q>=1

**Screen B, joint draft.** For the full strict-law family, if
Q=sigma_(2^floor(m/2))>=1, there is no prescribed original trajectory,
from ANY initial point. No initial-slice assumption is needed for this
screen. The violated condition uses the two original query indices
L-1 and 2L-1, far apart in physical time.

Let p=v_(L-1) and decompose every vector into B's orthogonal eigenspaces,
v=v^++v^- with Bv^+=v^+, Bv^-=-v^-. Let

    P=sum_(i=0)^(L-2) h_i v_i,
    S_j=sum_(i=0)^(L-2)h_i,
    d_j=a c^j, H_j=1+d_j.

The original word between the two stated query indices is its level-j
bridge followed by one whole child of length L-1. Equations (7.1)-(7.2)
therefore give the EXACT original gradient and displacement

    v_(2L-1)=Q Bp,
    z_(L-1)-z_(2L-1)=H_j p+Q BP.                       (8.1)

No chronological permutation or omitted last child leaf occurs in
(8.1). In particular P includes all the L-1 steps of that child,
including its actual final H_0 leaf.

The firm condition (2.2) for these two original points requires the
following explicitly computed quantity to be NONNEGATIVE:

    F=<H_j p+Q BP, p-Q Bp>-||p-Q Bp||^2
     =(1-Q)[(H_j-1+Q)||p^+||^2+Q<P^+,p^+>]
       +(1+Q)[(H_j-1-Q)||p^-||^2-Q<P^-,p^->].          (8.2)

We show that it is strictly negative whenever Q>=1.

First, <P^+,p^+>>0. All binary labels in the first block have bit j
and every higher bit zero. In B's plus space, the dominant coordinates
above j have common positive signs, those below j have arbitrary
products, and the total mixed energy is at most zeta/N^2. Therefore
for every 0<=i<L,

    <w_(Gamma(i))^+,w_(Gamma(L-1))^+>
       >=R^(-2)+(N-3L+1)/(N-1)-zeta/N^2>0.             (8.3)

The last sign follows from N>=L^2, L>=4 and zeta<=1; for instance
3L<=3N/4 and the positive dominant term is larger than 1/4, whereas
1/N^2<1/4. Multiplying by positive h_i sigma_i s proves the assertion.

Second, the minus-space ratio is bounded BELOW. Its only dominant
coordinate is tau_j, with squared weight

    b_j^2=L/(N-1).

All first-block vectors have the same positive sign in that coordinate.
Put eta=zeta/N^2. Then

    ||p^-||^2<=s^2(b_j^2+eta),
    <P^-,p^->>=s (b_j^2-eta) sum_(i=0)^(L-2)h_i sigma_i.

Here eta/b_j^2<=1/N. Hence, using (7.6) and N>=15,

    <P^-,p^->/||p^-||^2
      >=[(1-1/N)/(1+1/N)] (7/8) S_j
      >=(49/64)S_j > (3/4)S_j.                        (8.4)

Third, the bridge is smaller than (3/4)S_j. Exact counting of levels
in the child gives

    S_j=(2^j-1)+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2)
       =[a/(c-2)]c^j+[1-a/(c-2)]2^j-1.

Let lambda=1+sqrt(2). Since lambda(lambda-2)=1,
c>lambda and beta<1 imply a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)]<1.
Thus S_j>d_j/(c-2)-1>2d_j-1. Also d_j>2^j/3>7/2,
so

    H_j=1+d_j < (3/4)(2d_j-1) < (3/4)S_j.             (8.5)

Now if Q>=1, the first term of (8.2) is nonpositive by (8.3).
Its second bracket, divided by the positive ||p^-||^2, is

    H_j-1-Q-Q<P^-,p^->/||p^-||^2
       <H_j-Q(3/4)S_j<0

by (8.4)-(8.5). The second term is strictly negative. Hence F<0,
contradicting the original firm pair. This proves Screen B.

This obstruction differs from a root-saturated three-query argument:
both low-level inequalities may be strict, the compared queries are
remote, and the derivation never sets an adjacent normal slack to zero.

## 9. An explicit parameter exclusion: theta must be O(1/m)

It remains to turn Q>=1 into a proved all-parameter subdomain,
without computing Q. Work in the surviving geometric domain (5.2),
so T<9/4. For 4<=l<=j, H_l>6, and subtracting the root polynomial
at 1 from its value at rho_l gives

    (rho_l-1)[rho_l+1+(H_l-2)t_l]=2(H_l-2)E_l/T.

As rho_l<=9/8<2 and t_l<=1, the bracket is at most H_l+1.
Since (H_l-2)/(H_l+1)>4/7 and T<9/4, it follows that

    rho_l-1 >= (32/63) E_l.

For 1<=r_l<=9/8, log r_l>= (r_l-1)/r_l. Thus

    log r_l >= (8/9)theta(rho_l-1)
       >= (256/567)theta E_l
       > (4/9)theta E_l
       >= (2/3)theta 2^l/N, 4<=l<=j.                  (9.1)

At theta=0 replace the one strict inequality by equality; the weak
conclusion remains valid. The final bound uses
E_l>=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)>=(3/2)2^l/N for l>=1.

Insert these bounds into (7.1), keep the high levels 2 and 3 merely
nonnegative, and use (7.4) for the two negative short terms. The
levels 4,...,j-1 each contribute at least theta L/(3N), while
level j contributes at least 2theta L/(3N). Consequently

    log Q >= (L/N)[theta(j-2)/3-16].                   (9.2)

In particular theta(j-2)>=48 implies Q>=1 and hence Screen B.
Together with Screen A, this proves the following exact necessary
parameter restrictions for the whole prescribed trajectory:

    R^(-2)<M,
    theta < 48/(floor(m/2)-2).                         (9.3)

The inequalities are strict: their equality boundaries are excluded
by the preceding contradictions. Parameters delta_0,delta_1 may be
anywhere in (0,16] and may depend explicitly on m; the proof is uniform
over all such choices. For any fixed theta>0, every sufficiently large
m violates the second necessary condition. Parameters of order 1/m,
including theta=0, are NOT excluded by this argument.

Equation (9.3) is a scoped obstruction, not a complete answer to the
strict-law task. In particular it does not justify setting theta=0
and declaring the remaining normals feasible.

## 10. Actual rows, vertical price, and the unclosed performance interface

For any legal projection trajectory of the single F_D, set

    g_i=P_D(z_i), f_i=<z_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,
    r_i=z_i-g_i.

Expansion and the original normal tests give every ordered row,
including both terminal directions:

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,z_j-z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<r_j,g_i-g_j><=0.

For any minimizer q in the FULL polar cone, with gradient and value
zero, the two rows are Q_(i,star)=<g_i,q><=0 and
Q_(star,i)=-<r_i,g_i><=0. The real Gram is PSD by its construction.
These facts do not supply the missing z_0 that makes the prescribed
v_i equal the actual g_i; that assertion remains unproved in (9.3).

If that assertion were established, the exact original run would have

    P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k v_k,
    z_i=(x_0,-R)-P_i,
    (z_i)_vertical=-R-R^(-1)sum_(k<i)h_k sigma_k.        (10.1)

Thus the changing vertical gradient is fully paid. Any candidate
that keeps the vertical coordinate fixed is not the required GD run.
The zero-vertex normal height <z_i-v_i,v_i> and slack relative to a
nonzero chosen polar minimum are different quantities. No claim of
cap-active legality is made merely from the latter slack.

The own closest initial radius is

    R_init=dist((x_0,-R),C^polar),

not automatically ||x_0||. The general elementary bounds, if the first
prescribed query is legal, are sqrt(T)<=R_init<=||x_0||: the upper
bound uses z_star=(0,-R), and the lower uses the 1-Lipschitz property
of P_D between z_0 and any minimizer. Neither bound pays the unknown
initialization sharply enough for a terminal gain. If the terminal
projection is indeed v_n, its exact score would be

    E=[2<(x_0,-R),v_n>-2 sum_(i<n)h_i<v_i,v_n>-||v_n||^2]
        /R_init^2.                                    (10.2)

Equations (10.1)-(10.2) are diagnostic identities, NOT a legal
construction or a quantitative lower bound. The surviving task still
requires an EXPLICIT x_0 satisfying all original normals, a proof
that the terminal projection is v_n, a closest-cone distance treatment,
and an all-m comparison of (10.2) with the COMPLETE K/ray channel.
No feasibility LP/PEP, unspecified linear potential, or unproved
matrix-sign condition is presented as a replacement for that work.

Even a future success for this one hierarchy would leave the original
every-n/every-nonnegative-word minimax bridge open. Conversely, Screens
A and B are not an upper bound on W_n(h), an all-simplex obstruction,
or evidence that the original minimax conjecture is false.

## 11. Exact closeout status and resource/provenance record

- ONE distinct fresh bounded analytical invocation, after the previous
  allocation was explicitly complete. ONE new substantive law studied;
  the undamped local check was a required premise audit, not a new law.
- The first law remains unresolved in the precisely stated surviving
  domain (9.3). Because there was no decisive full-law outcome, the
  conditional second-mechanism allowance was NOT used. The old
  maximum-radius-ray law was not repeated or counted as new work.
- Required allocation model/effort: `gpt-6-astra` / `ultra`; no alternate
  model or effort was requested. This records the supplied allocation,
  not independently measured backend usage.
- ZERO mathematical programs, numerical evaluations, arithmetic or
  trajectory/recurrence replays, solvers, formal calls, `fact_submit`,

- Tool actions were administrative public-file reading, text writing,
  and integrity checks. No worker-private memories or conversations
  were read; no Danus worker, service, task, truth store, or old research

- Initialization marker, live OPERATOR.md, the complete PROBLEM.md,
  TASK.md, ALLOCATION.json, and the complete main-authored screen were
  read. TASK.md SHA256 was confirmed as
  `49a32b3d3acf987e13f0fe293295299006f938af4a5d1e8430adffcdb73beabc`.
  The screen SHA256 was confirmed as
  `b716f897687709af1540f96c0eff55793875193d3b87f2af233cb502da1f22b6`.
- Every mathematical fact needed above was jointly derived. No
  accepted interpolation statement, prior unverified draft, memory
  result, universality claim, or benchmark upper ratio is used to
  conceal a new premise. Retained memory guidance concerns only the
  original scope and accepted-evidence boundary.
- All new assertions remain JOINT UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN.
  The ONE allocation ends on this return, with zero unallocated
  invocations and no automatic renewal. No publication, finalization,
  revocation, project shutdown, or machine shutdown occurred. No

Notation: D is the real body; D_m=1+2S is the word scalar. R is the
vertical geometry scale and R_init is the closest-minimum radius.
B in Sections 7-8 is an orthogonal sign reflection, distinct from the
positive local residual B in (4.2) and from the benchmark B_n. The

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. This is a DISTINCT allocation after the
previous direct Gray invocation completed. ONE invocation, gpt-6-astra/ultra,
at most TWO substantive laws. ZERO mathematical programs, numerical or
arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence replays, solvers, formal calls/fact_submit,
paid consults, installations or subagents. No Danus worker/service/task
changes and no worker-private reads. Public-file retrieval, writing and
integrity checks only. ConsultOFF; original target OPEN; no auto-renewal.

Read OPERATOR.md, initialization marker, the complete original PROBLEM.md,
and MAIN_REPAIR_SCREEN_UNVERIFIED.md in this directory. The main fully read
your previous26488-byte RESULT.md (SHA25badb56aa3dd645942fb686b3387e64c8d826ca0056bf73833d98e2a139c97e),
which excludes the original tiny-overlap exact-root law and evaluates the
maximum-ray inverse law exactly at Huber. Do not repeat either.

The new dominant characters tau_k=2^m-2^k have the weights and exact overlap
formulas in the main screen. That screen suggests that leaving BOTH short
levels at their adjacent firm-equality roots fails even for this correlated
dictionary, on physical queries1,2,3. Independently derive/check that screen
as part of your new law's motivation; it remains UNVERIFIED.

Study a STRICTLY damped, word-selected radial law. Keep the explicit full
correlated-character dictionary, the common coordinate1/R and the actual
minimum cone. For each physical level let rho_l be the exact positive root
of the adjacent firm inequality, computed in the TRUE full Euclidean metric
T=M+R^(-2) and cosine1-2E_l/T. Set sigma_0=1 and
 sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i*r_(nu_2(i+1)).
One explicit parameter family to pursue has
 r_l=rho_l*(1-delta_l/N) for l=0,1,
 r_l=1+theta*(rho_l-1) for l>=2,
with0<delta_0,delta_1<=16,0<=theta<=1,0<zeta<=1 and R>0. The constants
or parameters may depend EXPLICITLY on m but must be fixed before the run;
include every positivity guard. This is the first permitted substantive law.
Its small damping occurs at O(1/N) per short step, so do not declare it
exponentially fatal merely from counting leaves; compute the whole product
and true radius. Conversely do not assume local strictness ensures all rows.

The body is D=conv(0,v_0,...,v_n),
 v_i=sigma_i*(U_(Gamma(i)),1/R), Gamma(i)=i xor floor(i/2).
The desired prescribed trajectory uses these vertices in that PHYSICAL
order from z0=(x0,-R). Supply an EXPLICIT x0 satisfying every original
normal row, zero-vertex row and actual terminal, or prove a decisive full
parameter obstruction. Writing a feasibility LP/PEP, an unspecified linear
potential, or a matrix with unproved signs is not a construction. All values
come from the one static projection potential. The whole polar minimum set,
exact or rigorous closest-radius control, vertical drift and actual last
leaf must be included. Positive origin-normal cap height and positive slack
about the nearby minimum are distinct; a useful witness needs a paid cap-active
history, not only conic motion.

Second mechanism, only after a decisive first-law result, may change the
level-dependent strict-margin profile or select an explicit transverse
inverse terminal with a PROVED nonconstant itinerary. It must change the
identified obstruction and still deliver a whole-word terminal/true-radius
estimate. The old maximum-radius-ray inverse selector has exact Huber payoff
and is not an acceptable new mechanism. No third dictionary or law is allowed.

The target is a nonvanishing gain over the COMPLETE existing K/ray channel
on every SAME-m H_(m,1/4), m>=1536, ideally a valid exact B_n comparison.
All new results remain JOINT UNVERIFIED. The original all-word minimax
problem is not solved by one family or one representation. Accepted931's
B_n/D_m<47/50 is an UPPER comparison and cannot certify a lower gain by

Write RESULT.md and HANDOFF.md in this new public directory with complete
