<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000535 -->
# Free value level: a uniform midpoint obstruction and an unclosed blend family

**JOINT UNVERIFIED.** Project `gd-exact-profile-20260926`.
Public allocation `77fd0cf837cc3d01`; supplied model/effort
`gpt-6-astra / ultra`; ONE bounded native invocation, TWO analytical
mechanisms, and ZERO mathematical programs or formal calls.

The original exact-profile minimax Goal remains **OPEN**. This is a
bounded research report, not a verifier-accepted fact or a solution.

The free additive value level really does remove the former fixed-slice
terminal obstruction. The midpoint law nevertheless fails a different,
explicit NONZERO-vertex row for every enclosing integer m>=1536.
Writing ell=floor(m/2), d=2^ell, i=d-1, j=3d/2-1, and s=sigma_i,
the row has the uniform strict lower bound

    R_(j,i) > s^2 d H_ell / [16(N-1)] > 0.             (A)

This holds for every real additive level tau. Every original step and
all mixed characters are retained. The proof is a finite inequality,
not a numerical asymptotic check.

The second mechanism examines the ENTIRE constant-blend parameter
lambda in [0,1]. Two explicit original nonzero rows exclude

    0 <= lambda <= 1/(20N),
    47/96 <= lambda <= 1.                              (B)

**The interval (1/(20N),47/96) is not closed by this report.** No closed
lambda(m) with all original rows and quantitative payoff was obtained,
and no incompatibility excluding this remaining interval was proved.
Thus mechanism II does NOT meet the requested full-family closure.
An unevaluated intersection or unknown LP is not presented as closure.
This invocation stops under its explicit allocation cap; there is no
implicit renewal or request to promote partial work to the minimax answer.

## 1. The unchanged finite model

Keep the same enclosing m>=1536 in every subblock. Define

    N=2^m, n=N-1, eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,
    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,
    H_l=1+a c^l, h_t=H_(nu_2(t+1)), 0<=t<n.

All h_t are predetermined and strictly positive. Put

    R=sqrt(3), v=R^(-2)=1/3,
    Kmix=N-1-m, eta=1/(N^2 Kmix),
    tau_k=N-2^k, 0<=k<m,
    b_(tau_k)^2=2^k/(N-1),
    b_t^2=eta for the other nonzero characters t in F_2^m,
    chi_t(u)=(-1)^(t dot u),
    U_u=(b_t chi_t(u))_(t!=0), w_u=(U_u,1/R),
    Gamma(i)=i xor floor(i/2), w_i=w_(Gamma(i)),
    M=1+1/N^2, T=M+1/3=4/3+1/N^2.

The symbol tau without a subscript below is the free additive VALUE
level; tau_k above labels a character and is unrelated to that value.
Every b_t is positive. No small character is dropped.

The following coarse parameter bounds will suffice:

    N>=1024, Kmix>=N/2>=m,
    12/5<c<121/50, 2/5<a<1/2,
    4/3<T<27/20, eta*m<=1/N^2.                       (1.1)

For example sqrt(2)>7/5 and sqrt(2)<17/12, while
eps<1/6000, give the stated bounds on c. Also beta>999/1000,
so a>2/5. For a<1/2 use beta<1 and c>2. All elementary N and
Kmix bounds follow from m>=1536 and persist under doubling N.

The Gray singleton identity is

    chi_(tau_k)(Gamma(i))=(-1)^bit_k(i).              (1.2)

Indeed Gray bit l is bit_l(i) xor bit_(l+1)(i), and xoring
from k to m-1 telescopes. Walsh orthogonality follows by pairing
binary labels differing in a selected bit. Summing the complement
of the m singleton characters therefore gives the full Gram kernel

    <w_i,w_j>=1/3-eta
       +sum_(k<m)[2^k/(N-1)-eta](-1)^(bit_k(i)+bit_k(j))
       +eta N 1_(i=j).                               (1.3)

Its diagonal is T. Equivalently, for distinct original indices,

    <w_i,w_j>
      =T-2(i xor j)/(N-1)+2eta popcount(i xor j)-eta N. (1.4)

Here the integer xor in the rational term is the sum of its binary
weights 2^k. Thus (1.4) still includes all mixed-character corrections.

The level-l adjacent flip energy and Gram are

    E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)+eta(N/2-l-1),
    G_l=T-2E_l.

Let rho_l be the positive root of

    Q_l(r)=r^2+(H_l-2)(G_l/T)r+1-H_l=0.

The unchanged radial law is

    x=r_0=rho_0(1-4/N), y=r_1=rho_1(1-4/N),
    r_l=1 for l>=2,
    sigma_0=1, sigma_(t+1)=sigma_t r_(nu_2(t+1)),
    v_i=sigma_i w_i, D0=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n}.

For H_l<2, Q_l(1)=(2-H_l)(1-G_l/T)>0 and its constant
term is negative, so 0<rho_l<1. Thus 0<x,y<1 and every sigma_i
is positive and at most 1. H_0,H_1<2; all H_l with l>=2 exceed 2.
No high-level root replaces the prescribed high-level ratio 1.

## 2. Free reconstruction, all normals, and the actual minimum

This section is valid for any specified decrements Delta_t. Set

    C_i=sum_(t<i)Delta_t, f_i=tau-C_i,
    P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t v_t,
    A_i=||v_i||^2/2+<P_i,v_i>, F_i=f_i+A_i,
    q_i=F_i/sigma_i,
    x_(0,t)=(1/(N b_t))sum_(i=0)^n chi_t(Gamma(i)) q_i,
    Y_0=(R/N)sum_(i=0)^n q_i,
    z_0=(x_0,Y_0), z_i=z_0-P_i.                     (2.1)

The full N by N Walsh matrix, including its constant row, is
invertible. Its nonconstant kernel is N 1_(i=j)-1. Substitution in
(2.1), with bar(q)=N^(-1)sum_i q_i, yields, at EVERY original index j,

    <x_0,U_(Gamma(j))>=q_j-bar(q), Y_0/R=bar(q),
    <z_0,w_j>=q_j, <z_0,v_j>=F_j,
    <z_j,v_j>-||v_j||^2/2=f_j.                       (2.2)

This derives the requested free initializer directly. In particular
there is NO condition sum_i q_i=-N and NO prescription Y_0=-R.
The N frames and their positive rescalings are independent; D0 is a
full-dimensional simplex in R^N.

Define the static function

    F_D(z)=max_(g in D0)[<z,g>-||g||^2/2].            (2.3)

Completing the square shows that its unique maximizer is P_D0(z).
The exact projection characterization is

    g in D0 and <z-g,w-g><=0 for every vertex w of D0. (2.4)

Necessity follows by differentiating distance along the segment from
g to each vertex. Sufficiency follows by taking convex combinations
of the normals and expanding the squared distance. Adding the two
normal conditions for projections of z and z' proves

    ||P_D0(z)-P_D0(z')||^2
       <=<P_D0(z)-P_D0(z'),z-z'>.

It follows by Cauchy--Schwarz that projection is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing
the affine expressions attained at these two maximizers bounds the
differentiability remainder by ||z-z'||^2. Hence F_D is finite,
convex, differentiable, and grad F_D=P_D0 is 1-Lipschitz.

Write C=cone{v_0,...,v_n}. The complete minimum set is

    min F_D=0,
    argmin F_D=C^polar
       ={(x,Y): <x,U_u>+Y/R<=0 for every u}.         (2.5)

A polar point has value zero because every candidate inner product
is nonpositive and zero is available. If a generator has positive
inner product, a sufficiently short positive multiple of it gives
strictly positive value in (2.3), proving the converse. Positive
radii do not change this cone or its polar. The cone is closed as
an invertible linear image of the nonnegative orthant.

The exact normal array for the reconstruction is

    R_ij:=<z_i-v_i,v_j-v_i>
      =C_i-C_j+<v_j,P_j-P_i>+||v_j-v_i||^2/2,        (2.6)
    R_i,zero:=<z_i-v_i,-v_i>
      =||v_i||^2/2-f_i.                             (2.7)

These formulas follow by expanding (2.2). They include all ordered
nonzero-vertex pairs 0<=i,j<=n and every zero row. Their simultaneous
nonpositivity is necessary and sufficient for the prescribed vertices
to be the actual projections. The free tau cancels in EVERY (2.6).
The zero rows alone can always be paid by the finite closed choice

    tau >= max_(0<=i<=n)(C_i+T sigma_i^2/2).          (2.8)

This does not repair any failed row in (2.6). Actual objective values
equal the prescribed f_i only AFTER all these projection conditions
hold. Under that condition the recursion z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i v_i is the
actual gradient descent recursion, and

    (z_i)_vertical=Y_0-(1/R)sum_(t<i)h_t sigma_t.

The literal adjacent bounds are

    L_t=h_t<v_t,v_(t+1)>+||v_t-v_(t+1)||^2/2,
    U_t=h_t||v_t||^2-||v_t-v_(t+1)||^2/2,
    k_t=U_t-L_t=-sigma_t^2 T Q_l(r_l)>0,
       l=nu_2(t+1).                                (2.9)

For low levels Q_l(r_l)<0 because 0<r_l<rho_l and Q_l has
one positive root. For high levels k_t=2sigma_t^2(H_l-2)E_l>0.
The adjacent normals are L_t-Delta_t and Delta_t-U_t.

For any hypothetical fully legal nonstationary run define

    Mscore=max_i q_i,
    z_star=(x_0,Y_0-R Mscore).

Then <z_star,w_i>=q_i-Mscore<=0 at every original vertex, so
z_star belongs to the COMPLETE minimum set (2.5). Its exact distance
from z_0 is R|Mscore|. Legality at i=0 against zero gives
<z_0,v_0>>=||v_0||^2=T, so q_0>=T>0 and Mscore>0. Thus the
specified distance is R Mscore, and the legal lower witness would be

    2(tau-C_n)/(R^2 Mscore^2).                       (2.10)

For F=L F_D, the gradients and step h_i/L yield the same recursion,
and L cancels in the required normalization. The closest polar
minimizer has no larger distance, so a poor value of (2.10) is NOT
an upper bound on the closest-minimum normalized class. No cone
projection oracle or equality of this distance with ||x_0|| is used.

## 3. Finite radial estimates needed for the new obstruction

For a short step H_l=1+b, the root equation rearranges exactly to

    (1-rho_l)(rho_l+b)
       =2(1-b)E_l rho_l/T.                          (3.1)

Because rho_l<=1 and b>0,
rho_l/(rho_l+b)<=1/(1+b). Since Kmix>=N/2,

    NE_0 < 1+3/N < 28/27, NE_1<4.

At l=0, b=a>2/5, so 2(1-b)/(T(1+b))<9/14.
Consequently N(1-rho_0)<2/3. At l=1, b=beta and 1-b=eps,
so N(1-rho_1)<6eps<2/3. The damping factors therefore give

    0<1-x<14/(3N), 0<1-y<14/(3N).                   (3.2)

Exact level counts give

    sigma_t=x^ceil(t/2) y^floor((t+1)/4).            (3.3)

This is a counting identity, not a trajectory replay. Put alpha=14/3
and kappa=7/2. For any positive integer u in the range used below,

    r(u):=sigma_(u-1)=x^floor(u/2)y^floor(u/4),
    1-r(u)<=alpha[floor(u/2)+floor(u/4)]/N
           <=kappa u/N.                            (3.4)

The first inequality follows by expanding 1-product(1-e_j) and
bounding it by sum e_j, or by induction. In particular no exponential
approximation to the radius is needed.

## 4. Mechanism I: one original reverse row kills the midpoint

Freeze Delta_t=(L_t+U_t)/2, so cancellation of the distance terms gives

    Delta_t=(h_t/2)(||v_t||^2+<v_t,v_(t+1)>).        (4.1)

Choose

    ell=floor(m/2), d=2^ell, k=ell-1, B=d/2,
    i=d-1, j=d+B-1=3d/2-1,
    s=sigma_i>0, H=H_ell.

Here ell>=768, d>=2048, N>=d^2, and i,j are ORIGINAL indices
strictly below n. The first step of this interval is the original
level-ell bridge at i, with radius ratio exactly 1. The remaining
steps are t=i+u, 1<=u<B, with levels nu_2(u). The low-level counts
are not restarted or changed: because d is divisible by 4,

    sigma_(i+u)/s=r(u)=sigma_(u-1),
    i xor (i+u)=2d-u.                               (4.2)

Write r_l=1-a_l. The midpoint coefficient is exactly

    (T+r_l G_l)/2=T-T a_l/2-r_l E_l.

The reverse normal in (2.6) is

    R_(j,i)=sum_(t=i)^(j-1)[Delta_t-h_t<v_i,v_t>]
                +||v_j-v_i||^2/2.                  (4.3)

The first term t=i in (4.3), divided by s^2, equals -H E_ell.
For each subsequent t=i+u, (1.4) and (4.2) give exactly

    [Delta_t-h_t<v_i,v_t>]/(s^2 h_t)
      =-T r(u)(1-r(u))-r(u)^2 T a_l/2-r(u)^2 r_l E_l
        +2r(u)(2d-u)/(N-1)
        +eta r(u)[N-2 popcount(2d-u)].              (4.4)

The last term is NONNEGATIVE since N>2m; it is retained before
being bounded in its favorable direction. From (3.2)--(3.4),
0<r(u)<=1 and

    -T r(u)(1-r(u)) >= -T kappa u/N,
    -r(u)^2 T a_l/2 >= -T alpha/(2N),
    -r(u)^2 r_l E_l >= -E_l,
    2r(u)(2d-u)/(N-1)
       >=2(2d-u)/(N-1)-4kappa d u/[N(N-1)].         (4.5)

The a_l bound is deliberately allowed even at high levels where
a_l=0. No sign-indefinite summand is silently discarded.

Let

    S=sum_(u=1)^(B-1) H_(nu_2(u)),
    V=sum_(l=0)^(k-1) H_l, e_l=2^(l+1)-1.

Symmetry of the literal ruler word about B/2 and exact level counts
give the finite identities

    sum_(u=1)^(B-1) u H_(nu_2(u))=(B/2)S,
    sum_(u=1)^(B-1) H_(nu_2(u)) e_(nu_2(u))=BV-S.   (4.6)

For the first identity pair u and B-u, which have the same valuation.
For the second, level l occurs B/2^(l+1) times. Summing the geometric
series in H_l gives

    S=a c^k/(c-2)+(1-a/(c-2))B-1,
    V=k+a(c^k-1)/(c-1).                             (4.7)

There is no evolving recurrence evaluation here. The scalar
a/(c-2) is in (0,1), since c(c-2)>1>beta. Therefore

    S >= (H-1)/[c(c-2)]-1,
    S<c^k<H/beta<2H,
    V<k+H/[c(c-1)].                                 (4.8)

Drop the nonnegative final squared distance in (4.3), use (4.5)--(4.6),
and separate the mixed-character energy in E_l. This yields

    R_(j,i)/s^2
      >= d/(N-1)[-2H+(7/2-T kappa/4)S-V/2]
         -T alpha S/(2N)
         -2kappa d B S/[N(N-1)]
         -(eta N/2)(H+S).                           (4.9)

In deriving (4.9), the additional rational contribution
(H+S)/(N-1) is nonnegative and was dropped; the replacement
(N-1)/N<=1 in the negative radial term is in the adverse direction.
Thus (4.9) is a rigorous lower bound at the actual finite N.

Here T kappa<189/40, hence 7/2-T kappa/4>371/160.
By (1.1),

    (371/160)/[c(c-2)] > 9/4,
    1/[2c(c-1)] < 25/168 < 3/20.

For the first comparison use c(c-2)<2541/2500 and multiply the
positive rational denominators. It follows that

    -2+(371/160)/[c(c-2)]-1/[2c(c-1)] > 1/10.        (4.10)

The additive error from using (4.8) is less than 8+k/2<ell.
Also H>(2/5)2^ell>1000ell: 2^16>2500*16, and the inequality
2^r>2500r persists under doubling for r>=16. Equations
(4.8)--(4.10) consequently imply

    -2H+(7/2-T kappa/4)S-V/2 > H/12.               (4.11)

Finally normalize the three negative remainder terms in (4.9) by
dH/(N-1). Since S<2H, T alpha<7, N>=d^2, and Kmix>=N/2,
their total is strictly less than

    7/d + 7d/N + 3/(Nd) < 15/d < 1/100.             (4.12)

Combining (4.9)--(4.12), and using 1/12-1/100>1/16, proves

    R_(j,i) > s^2 d H/[16(N-1)] > 0.

This is exactly (A). It excludes the midpoint for EVERY m>=1536 and
EVERY tau, without assuming a vertical slice or checking a favorable
terminal surrogate. Mechanism I is decisively closed as an obstruction.

## 5. Mechanism II: the complete constant blend, with limited exclusions

Now, only after the midpoint failure, take the entire allowed family

    Delta_t(lambda)=L_t+lambda k_t, 0<=lambda<=1.

Use the same free reconstruction (2.1). All pairwise normals are affine
in lambda and independent of tau. Adjacent normals equal -lambda k_t
and -(1-lambda)k_t, so they impose no further restriction inside [0,1].

First reuse the exact reverse row from Section 4, not a new geometry.
Let K_I=sum_(t=i)^(j-1) k_t>0. Then

    R_(j,i)(lambda)=R_(j,i)(1/2)+(lambda-1/2)K_I.    (5.1)

We need an evaluated size bound, rather than an unknown ratio. For
every step, k_t<=h_t sigma_t^2(T-r_l G_l), since the subtracted
squared distance is nonnegative. For high levels the sharper exact
formula k_t=2sigma_t^2(H_l-2)E_l also holds. Using (4.2), r(u)<=1,
and (4.6),

    K_I/s^2
      <= [4dH+dV]/(N-1)+T alpha S/N+eta N(H+S).

Here V<k+H/[c(c-1)]<H/2, S<2H, T alpha<7, and N>=d^2.
After division by dH/(N-1), the right side is less than

    9/2+14/d+6/(Nd) < 6.

Consequently

    0<K_I<6s^2 dH/(N-1).                            (5.2)

Combining (A), (5.1), and (5.2) shows R_(j,i)(lambda)>0 whenever
lambda>=1/2-1/96=47/96. This includes all lambda>=1/2 directly,
and the remaining short interval by (5.2).

For the other end of the FULL family, use the original top center

    q=N/2-1, J=H_(m-1), s_top=sigma_q,
    v_q=s_top w_q, v_(q+1)=s_top w_(q+1),
    v_(q+2)=s_top x w_(q+2).

Put g=2/3-eta(m-1)>0 and e=2/(N-1)-2eta>1/N. From (1.3),

    <w_q,w_(q+1)>=-g,
    <w_(q+1),w_(q+2)>=T-2E_0,
    <w_q,w_(q+2)>=-g+e.

At lambda=0, direct expansion of the two literal L decrements gives

    R_(q,q+2)(0)
       =<(J-1)v_q+v_(q+1),v_(q+2)-v_(q+1)>
       =s_top^2[(J-1)((1-x)g+x e)-(1-x)T-2x E_0].   (5.3)

This rederives the row instead of importing a predecessor's claim.
Use x>1/2, 1-x<8/N, E_0<2/N, T<3/2, and J>66 to obtain

    R_(q,q+2)(0)>s_top^2(J-33)/(2N)
                 >s_top^2 J/(4N).                  (5.4)

The weaker bound 1-x<8/N follows from (3.2). All terms involving g
that were dropped are nonnegative. This row is independent of tau.

At the center k_q=2s_top^2(J-2)E_(m-1)<4s_top^2 J because
E_(m-1)<2. At the next low step,

    k_(q+1)<=s_top^2 H_0[T(1-x)+2xE_0]
              <32s_top^2/N<s_top^2 J.

Therefore K_top=k_q+k_(q+1)<5s_top^2 J, and the exact affine row is

    R_(q,q+2)(lambda)=R_(q,q+2)(0)-lambda K_top.

Equation (5.4) proves R_(q,q+2)(lambda)>0 whenever
0<=lambda<=1/(20N). Together with (5.1)--(5.2), this proves (B).

Both exclusions are from NONZERO vertices. Neither uses a fixed
vertical slice, a zero row, a terminal budget, a changed radial law,
or any mathematical program. Every m-dependent choice of lambda
falling in either excluded band is covered.

**Exact unresolved scope of mechanism II.** The necessary surviving
interval is

    1/(20N) < lambda < 47/96.                        (5.5)

The proved necessary bounds are compatible on (5.5); this does not
certify either exact row throughout that interval. The bounds are not
claimed to be sufficient. No proof certifies all original rows on
(5.5), and no legal lambda and payoff has been constructed. Conversely
no claim that some lambda in (5.5) is feasible follows from its
surviving these bounds. Finding or excluding such a lambda is the
unclosed part of this allocation.

## 6. Consequences and restrictions on interpretation

The main new resolved statement is a uniform obstruction to the
MIDPOINT law with arbitrary vertical initialization. This is stronger
in vertical scope than the former fixed-slice exclusion, but narrower
in decrement scope. The old assertion excluding every horizontal
initializer in Y_0=-R cannot be extended to arbitrary Y_0 by that old
budget. Here (2.8) shows exactly why its use would be invalid.

For the midpoint, no tau gives the prescribed actual itinerary, so
the formal terminal tau-C_n is not certified as the actual objective
at the true terminal n=N-1. There is no legal midpoint witness to
price, no improvement over the Huber/ray channel, and no obstruction
to a full-class upper bound 17/(16D), D=1+2sum_t h_t, from this failed
candidate. For the remaining constant blends, all these payoff
questions remain unproved. The explicit polar formula (2.10) is
conditional and is not evaluated on an illegal run.

The accepted comparison named in the task, B_n/D<47/50, is neither
used as a premise nor reversed into a lower comparison. No full-class
upper certificate, all-word lower theorem, or asymptotic minimax
separation follows from the present restricted obstruction.

The original target still asks for the exact benchmark
U_n^ZJ=1/(2S_bullet(n)+1), uniformly over all sufficiently large n
and EVERY predetermined nonnegative word, or a full-class asymptotic
separation. Even a future legal witness for this one H_m would not
close that universal requirement.

## 7. Resource, provenance, and stop record

- ONE native allocation, TWO mechanisms: the midpoint reverse-row
  obstruction, then the entire constant-blend investigation with
  only the explicitly stated partial exclusions. No third mechanism.
- Allocated model/effort: gpt-6-astra / ultra. This records supplied
  allocation metadata, not an independent backend-metering receipt.
- ZERO mathematical programs, numerical/arithmetic/trajectory or
  recurrence replays, solvers, optimizers, benchmark DP, formal calls,
  fact_submit, consultations, installations, or child subagents.
- Administrative actions only: public/shared text reads, writing
  RESULT.md and HANDOFF.md in this directory, reading them back,
  and SHA256 integrity hashing. The proofs above were derived as text.
- The initialization marker was present. Live OPERATOR.md, AGENTS.md,
  the COMPLETE PROBLEM.md, TASK.md, ALLOCATION.json, and the public
  persistence receipt were read. The initial ALLOCATION.md read
  reported that the path did not exist; the actual ALLOCATION.json
  was then read. The public JSON status was still the pre-spawn
  snapshot; this invocation did not edit that status.
- Both designated predecessor RESULT.md files were read COMPLETELY.
  A first combined output was truncated and was repaired with full
  separate reads. Their assertions were treated as UNVERIFIED
  awareness; all ingredients used in the present proofs are derived
  here. Their live hashes matched the supplied task:
  constructive-start 67a1ab35221bb58c95350b7fd113fd06c89df94ddf0b00a4e456acf5f99732db;
  binary-budget 9bd20e650cb2e4be0427159004fafa17b53dda3cdce1fb47c4bfe6d2c0d90e9a.
- TASK.md SHA256:
  c369a11db43bf34a858813fcc3a3052d239ca24d5c3bb6e2e573844d7c29530e.
- The danus-research skill and a brief MEMORY.md registry lookup were
  used only for scope/evidence boundaries. They supplied no mathematical
  premise or stale model choice; current model/effort came from the
  live operator profile and allocation.
- No private worker memory, service, worker, configuration, registry,
  allocation-status, or truth-store change was made. No finalization,
  revocation, publication, shutdown, or Goal completion occurred.
- Every mathematical assertion remains JOINT UNVERIFIED. Mechanism I
  is analytically closed as an obstruction; mechanism II's requested

  The bounded invocation ends on return, with ZERO automatic renewal.

Notation: D0 is the body, while D=1+2sum h_t is only the comparison
scalar mentioned in Section 6. H_l are physical steps; H=H_ell in

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. ONE fresh bounded native invocation, gpt-6-astra with ultra reasoning, consult OFF. At most TWO substantive mechanisms. ZERO mathematical programs, numerical/arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence replay, solvers, optimizers, benchmark DP, formal calls/fact_submit, installations, consults, or subagents. Only administrative public reads, text writes, and hashes. Do not renew. Do not read worker-private material or change worker/service/config/registry/truth stores. Original minimax Goal OPEN.

Read OPERATOR.md and the complete PROBLEM.md. Read the COMPLETE public reports research/direct_gray_constructive_start_20261003/RESULT.md (25859 bytes, SHA256 67a1ab35221bb58c95350b7fd113fd06c89df94ddf0b00a4e456acf5f99732db) and research/direct_gray_binary_budget_20261003/RESULT.md (25389 bytes, SHA256 9bd20e650cb2e4be0427159004fafa17b53dda3cdce1fb47c4bfe6d2c0d90e9a). Their assertions are UNVERIFIED awareness. Jointly derive every needed ingredient. The first report excludes every horizontal initializer in the fixed slice z0=(x0,-sqrt(3)); it does NOT exclude arbitrary vertical starts. Its lower-adjacent-decrement law also fails a remote nonzero-vertex row independently of any chosen value level. Do not reopen either claim as an unexplained scan.

Keep exactly the previous fixed correlated Walsh/Gray simplex and radial itinerary: every enclosing m>=1536, N=2^m, R=sqrt(3), v=R^-2=1/3, zeta=1, delta0=delta1=4, theta=0, r0=rho0(1-4/N), r1=rho1(1-4/N), r_l=1 for l>=2; sigma0=1, sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i r_(nu2(i+1)); all mixed characters strictly positive; v_i=sigma_i w_(Gamma(i)), i=0,...,N-1, and D0=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n}. Keep the SAME original H_m steps and true terminal n=N-1. The gradient is P_D0, and the actual minimum set is its entire polar cone. This is a different representation from residual-gradient affine-height triangles.

The new mechanism is a FREE ADDITIVE VALUE LEVEL. For any fixed relative decrements Delta_i and C_i=sum_(t<i)Delta_t, set
 f_i=tau-C_i,
 P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t v_t,
 A_i=||v_i||^2/2+<P_i,v_i>,
 F_i=tau-C_i+A_i.
Walsh reconstruction with NO prescribed vertical slice is
 x0_t=(1/(N b_t))sum_i chi_t(Gamma(i))*F_i/sigma_i,
 Y0=(R/N)sum_i F_i/sigma_i,
 z0=(x0,Y0), z_i=z0-P_i.
First derive these identities directly. Because the complete N frames are independent, the reconstruction should give <z0,v_i>=F_i at EVERY original index. Do not adopt the former negative-average formula fixing Y0=-R.

Main UNVERIFIED leads to rederive: all normals against NONZERO vertices depend only on differences F_j-F_i, so their tau cancels. The zero-vertex normal at query i is equivalent to f_i>=||v_i||^2/2. Thus tau>=max_i(C_i+||v_i||^2/2) pays all zero rows if the pairwise rows already hold. This does not repair a failed pairwise cycle. The actual objective equals the prescribed f_i only AFTER every original projection normal has been proved.

If all rows hold, put Mscore=max_i F_i/sigma_i. A specified finite polar minimizer is zstar=(x0,Y0-R*Mscore), since its inner product with each unscaled frame is F_i/sigma_i-Mscore<=0. Its EXACT distance from z0 is R*Mscore. Prove Mscore>0 for the legal nonstationary run before dividing. The score
 2*(tau-C_n)/(R^2*Mscore^2)
is a legal LOWER witness using that specific minimizer. A poor value is NOT an upper bound on the own-closest-normalized class. If choosing a different explicit polar point improves the score, derive its coordinates and cone inequalities; no unknown cone-projection oracle.

Mechanism1: freeze the midpoint adjacent law Delta_i=(L_i+U_i)/2, with the literal L_i,U_i from the previous report. Derive EVERY original nonzero-vertex row for the fixed geometry at all enclosing m. Either exhibit a uniform explicit violated row/cycle independent of tau, or prove all of them and select a specific closed tau(m) paying all zero rows and giving a rigorously bounded ACTUAL terminal score. Seek a nonvanishing improvement beyond the Huber/ray channel on this H_m or a decisive obstruction to the candidate full-class upper17/(16D), where D=1+2sum H_m. Mere feasibility without quantitative payoff is incomplete. Keep exact benchmark directions: accepted931's B_n/D<47/50 cannot be reversed into a lower comparison.

Mechanism2 only after a decisive midpoint failure or a fully legal but demonstrably insufficient specified score: use the ENTIRE constant blend Delta_i(lambda)=L_i+lambda(U_i-L_i), 0<=lambda<=1, while tau remains free. Find one CLOSED lambda(m) and tau(m) with all rows and payoff, or exclude the whole constant-blend family using explicit incompatible NONZERO-vertex rows. An unevaluated intersection of affine inequalities, a remaining unknown LP, or the old fixed-slice terminal budget is not closure. A global cycle sum independent of both lambda and tau is especially informative if valid. No new radial law, geometry, character weights, third mechanism or parameter scan.

Write complete JOINT UNVERIFIED RESULT.md and HANDOFF.md in this directory, read back and hash them, report exact quantifiers and resource counts, and stop this ONE invocation. <!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000537 -->
# Self-contained handoff: two radial-vertex laws exhausted at draft level

Status: **JOINT UNVERIFIED; original minimax Goal OPEN.**
One allocated direct invocation has ended on return; no remaining
unallocated invocation and no automatic renewal. No formal or
computational check was executed. The complete joint derivation is
`RESULT.md` in this same public directory.

## Preserve the target and resource boundary

The original objective remains

    W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2),
    W_n^star=inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n)W_n(h),
    W_n^star/U_n^ZJ -> 1, U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, W=2R.

The affirmative lower bound must hold at every sufficiently large n
uniformly over every predetermined nonnegative word. A negative
resolution requires a full-class upper certificate along a separating
subsequence. No such resolution is contained here. PROBLEM.md was not
changed. Required model/effort was gpt-6-astra/ultra. This invocation used
exactly two substantive laws and zero mathematical programs, numerical
evaluations, solvers, formal calls/fact_submit, paid consults,
installations, or subagents. Only administrative public-file reads,
text writes, and hashes were used. No workers, services, private worker
memories, truth stores, worker TASKs, publishing, finalization,
revocation, project stop, or machine shutdown were touched.

## Common exact geometry

Fix the SAME m>=1536 everywhere:

    N=2^m, n=N-1, eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,
    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,
    h_i=1+a c^(nu_2(i+1)), S=sum h_i, D_m=1+2S.

For every nonzero character t of F_2^m let

    U_s(t)=b_t(-1)^(t dot s),
    b_(e_l)^2=2^l/(N-1),
    b_t^2=zeta/[N^2(N-1-m)] for all other t,
    0<zeta<=1, M=sum b_t^2=1+zeta/N^2.

Keep every positive coordinate. For R>0 set T=M+R^-2,
w_s=(U_s,1/R), and positive radial vertices

    v_i=sigma_i w_(Gamma(i)), Gamma(i)=i xor floor(i/2),
    D=conv(0,v_0,...,v_n), F_D(z)=max_(g in D)(<z,g>-||g||^2/2).

These are N independent nonzero vertices in R^N; adding 0 gives one
full-dimensional simplex. Directly, F_D is convex and 1-smooth with
gradient P_D and

    argmin F_D=C^polar,
    C=cone{v_i},
    C^polar={(x,y): <x,U_s>+y/R<=0 for EVERY s}.

The cone is independent of all positive radii. The supplied
z_star=(0,-R) is a strict polar minimizer, but need not be the closest
one. Every gradient's vertical coordinate must participate in GD.

For actual samples g_i=P_D(z_i), r_i=z_i-g_i and
f_i=<z_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, the exact original rows are

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,z_j-z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<r_j,g_i-g_j><=0.

For any polar minimizer q with gradient/value zero,
Q_(i,star)=<g_i,q><=0 and Q_(star,i)=-<r_i,g_i><=0.
These are consequences of checking all original projection normals,
not of fitting unconstrained interpolation values.

## Law I: every parameter is rejected by queries 0,1,2

The original Gray identity is
Gamma(i+1)=Gamma(i)+e_(nu_2(i+1)). For

    E_l=2^l/(N-1)+zeta(N/2-1)/[N^2 Kmix], Kmix=N-1-m,
    H_l=1+a c^l, t_l=1-2E_l/T,
    rho_l=[-(H_l-2)t_l+
        sqrt((H_l-2)^2 t_l^2+4(H_l-1))]/2,

the adjacent necessary inequality for radius ratio r is

    r^2+(H_l-2)t_l r+1-H_l<=0.

Equality forces BOTH adjacent normal inner products against the edge
to be zero. The rejected first law is

    sigma_0=1, sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i r_(nu_2(i+1)),
    r_l=rho_l for H_l<2,
    r_l=1+theta(rho_l-1) for H_l>2, 0<=theta<=1.

Here precisely levels 0 and 1 have H_l<2. Put

    u=rho_0 in (0,1), v=rho_1 in (0,1), e=E_0, f=E_1,
    J=sum_(t:t_0=t_1=1)b_t^2=zeta/(4N Kmix),
    p_0=w_0, p_1=u w_(e_0), p_2=uv w_(e_0+e_1).

If these first three gradients were legal, the two root equalities
would make r_1 orthogonal to both p_1-p_0 and p_2-p_1. Since
r_2=r_1-beta p_1-p_2, the ORIGINAL row against p_0 at query 2 is
forced to be

    <r_2,p_0-p_2>
      =u[(beta+v)A+2vf(1-u)-4vJ],
    A=T(u-1)+2e=2ea(1+u)/(u+a)>2ea.

But 4J/e<1/Kmix<=1/4 while 2a>2/3. Hence the displayed row is
STRICTLY POSITIVE, contradicting the required nonpositive normal.
This is uniform for all m>=1536, R>0, 0<zeta<=1 and theta in [0,1].
It excludes ANY z_0, not only starts in the y=-R slice. Changing later
large-step radii cannot repair the two root equalities. There is no
legal trajectory and therefore no first-law terminal payoff.

Do not transplant this rejection to different character intersections,
to a small-step rule that breaks at least one of the first equalities,
to general shifted conjugates, or to all simplex functions.

## Law II: legal inverse selection, exact closest price, no gain

Use the same dictionary but fix theta=0, so the ratios are rho_0<1,
rho_1<1, and 1 at all higher levels. All sigma_i for i>=1 are below
sigma_0=1. Thus p=v_0=(U_0,1/R) is the explicit longest vertex,
||p||^2=T, and all unequal-radius vertices remain in the body.

For ANY explicit terminal amplitude t>=0 choose

    lambda_t=(1+(S+t)/R^2)/M,
    q_t=(lambda_t U_0,-lambda_t M R),
    z_n=q_t+t p.

Then q_t is in the full polar cone, q_t dot p=0, and is strictly polar
against every other nonzero vertex. The finite inverse rule at a
physical step h>1 maximizes

    score(v)=<y,v>+(h-1)||v||^2/2

over every vertex, including 0, and sets x=y+h v. The identity

    <x-v,w-v>=score(w)-score(v)-(h-1)||w-v||^2/2<=0

proves its legality. For the present terminal, the rule chooses p
uniquely at EVERY physical step: at y=q_t+A p with A>=0, the score
difference for any other vertex is a sum of nonpositive terms,
with strict maximum-norm or strict polar slack. The original run is
therefore explicitly

    A_i=t+sum_(k=i)^(n-1)h_k,
    z_i=q_t+A_i p,
    g_i=p for i<n, g_n=min(t,1)p.

The initialization satisfies the required slice exactly:

    z_0=(((1+(S+t)T)/M)U_0,-R).

The actual vertical coordinate at query i is
-R-(sum_(k<i)h_k)/R. It has not been frozen. Every pre-step original
normal height is (A_i-1)T>0; this is a genuinely active finite cap,
not a zero-origin-height cone run.

For EVERY q' in the polar cone,

    ||q_t+A_i p-q'||^2
       =A_i^2T+||q_t-q'||^2-2A_i<p,q'> >= A_i^2T.

Equality holds at q_t, proving the exact full minimum price
R_init^2=(S+t)^2T. The original supplied z_star is further away;
its distance is (1+(S+t)T)/sqrt(M). The attained W=2R score is

    E(t)=t^2/(S+t)^2 for 0<=t<=1,
    E(t)=(2t-1)/(S+t)^2 for t>=1.

The exact identity

    (S+t)^2-(1+2S)(2t-1)=(t-(S+1))^2

proves

    sup_(t>=0)E(t)=1/D_m, attained at t=S+1.

The COMPLETE K/cut functional contains 1/D_m as its first entry, so
this law cannot beat the whole channel. For near-minimum terminal
0<=t<=1,

    E(t)/(1/D_m)<=D_m/(S+1)^2<2/N ->0.

The task-supplied accepted B_n/D_m<47/50 is used in the valid direction:
B_n E(t)<47/50 for all t and <47/(25N) for 0<=t<=1. No direction of
this inequality is reversed, and no full-class upper bound is claimed.

## What remains, without a new allocation

The first route needs a different small-step norm law or a different
character-intersection structure, followed by all original remote
normals and an exact true-price terminal lower bound. The second route
needs a genuinely transverse terminal or another mechanism that avoids
the longest-vertex ray collapse, again with its full true price. A
finite inverse rule alone, local sign flip, formal recurrence, or
implicit feasibility problem is insufficient.

The main agent suggested dominant labels tau_k=2^m-2^k with weights
2^k/(N-1) and a tiny positive remainder. This arrived after the second
law was derived. It is recorded ONLY as an UNATTEMPTED possible next
direction: this file provides no derivation or assertion for it, and
the present small-intersection contradiction must not be assumed to
hold there. No third law or fresh allocation was begun.

No result here uses the separate UNVERIFIED simplex-universality draft
as a premise. The original every-n/every-word minimax bridge remains
separate and open. The one invocation is complete on return;
finalization, publication, revocation, shutdown, further formal calls,
or autonomous renewal are not authorized by this handoff.

# Radial Gray vertices: an original-pair obstruction and a paid inverse-law screen

**JOINT UNVERIFIED RESULT.** Project `gd-exact-profile-20260926`.
One bounded direct analytical allocation; exactly TWO substantive laws.
All new assertions below form one joint proof draft. No assertion in this
file has been independently verified or entered into the fact graph.

The first law is impossible on the original physical word: its first two
root-saturated transitions force a strictly positive ORIGINAL remote
projection-normal row at query 2. This holds for every declared parameter,
including every later bridge parameter theta and every vertical scale R.
The second law uses an explicit unequal-radius dictionary and the finite
power-score inverse rule. Its complete actual trajectory, all original
rows, full minimum cone, and exact closest-minimum price are derived.
Its supremum is exactly the complete-word Huber score 1/D_m, and terminals
on the segment within one vertex length of the chosen closest minimum
have a vanishing relative score. Neither result excludes all radial
simplices, all inverse terminals, or all smooth convex functions.

## 1. Original target and the exact scope of this allocation

For every finite dimension, every convex differentiable f with
L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, every minimizer x_star,
and every initial point different from that minimizer, the original
normalization is

    W_n(h) = sup 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2),
    x_(i+1) = x_i - (h_i/L) grad f(x_i),
    W_n^star = inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h),  W = 2 R.

The original goal is W_n^star/U_n^ZJ -> 1, with
U_n^ZJ = 1/(2 S_bullet(n)+1) = 1/B_n. Its affirmative quantifiers are:
for every eta>0, one threshold N_eta works for EVERY n>=N_eta and EVERY
predetermined nonnegative word of length n. A negative answer requires a
full-function worst-case upper certificate giving a nonvanishing
separation along an unbounded subsequence. Neither an obstruction to a
selected witness nor an attained lower-witness score is that certificate.
The complete `PROBLEM.md` is unchanged; its benchmark uses all integer
splits and is not replaced by a dyadic or exponent-only surrogate.

Here, throughout every child and every formula, fix the SAME integer
m>=1536 and put

    N=2^m, n=N-1, epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_l=1+a c^l, 0<=l<m,
    h_i=H_(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n,
    S=sum_(i=0)^(n-1) h_i, D_m=1+2S.

Thus H_0=1+a, H_1=1+beta, and all H_l with l>=2 exceed 2.
For later elementary signs it suffices to note

    0<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2, c>2, S>n>=1.

Indeed epsilon<=1/6144<1/100, beta>99/100,
c<(49/20)(101/100)<5/2, and a>99/250>1/3;
a<1/(1+sqrt(2))<1/2. Also beta c> (99/100)*2>1,
which proves H_2>2 and hence the assertion about all higher levels.
These are symbolic inequalities, not evaluated numerical trajectories.

The complete cut functional referenced in the public task is

    K_n(h)=max { 1/(1+2 sum h),
       max_(0<=i<n) (h_i-1)_+^2 /
       [(1+sum_(t<i)h_t)^2 (1+2sum_(t>i)h_t)] }.

Our comparison with the COMPLETE K_n uses only its first entry:
K_n(h)>=1/(1+2 sum h). We do not choose a favorable single cut and call
it the whole channel. The task also supplies the accepted scoped input
931b9653ce0f7201, B_n/D_m<47/50 on this hierarchy. This input is used
only to multiply an UPPER bound on this selected family's attained
score. It is never reversed into a lower-witness gain. The exact
1/D_m conclusions below do not depend on that input.

## 2. One body, all coordinates, and the full minimum set

Let G=F_2^m. For every nonzero character label t and every s in G set

    chi_t(s)=(-1)^(t dot s),
    U_s=(b_t chi_t(s))_(t!=0) in R^(N-1),
    Kmix=N-1-m,
    b_(e_l)^2=2^l/(N-1), 0<=l<m,
    b_t^2=zeta/(N^2 Kmix) otherwise, 0<zeta<=1.

All N-1 coordinates remain present and strictly positive. Introduce
positive R and define

    M=sum_(t!=0)b_t^2=1+zeta/N^2,
    T=M+1/R^2,
    w_s=(U_s,1/R) in R^N.

For positive radii sigma_i and the physical Gray labels Gamma(i), put

    v_i=sigma_i w_(Gamma(i)), 0<=i<=n,
    D=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n}, C=cone{v_0,...,v_n}.

The physical Euclidean inner product is unchanged. In particular the
vertical coordinate contributes sigma_i sigma_j/R^2 to every Gram
entry and sigma_i^2/R^2 to every vertex norm. It is not a free carrier.

Pairing labels that differ in a bit gives

    sum_s chi_t(s)=0 for t!=0,
    sum_s chi_t(s)chi_u(s)=N*1_(t=u),

where the same statement includes the constant character when needed.
Consequently the full Walsh matrix is invertible. Since all b_t and
1/R are nonzero, the N vectors w_s are linearly independent. Multiplying
the columns by positive radii preserves this property. Thus D is one
full-dimensional simplex in R^N with N+1=n+2 vertices, including 0.
This is proved directly; the separate unverified simplex-universality
candidate is not a premise.

Let

    K(s,u)=<U_s,U_u>,
    E(d)=sum_(t:t dot d=1)b_t^2.

Then

    K(s,u)=M-2E(s+u),
    <w_s,w_u>=T-2E(s+u), ||w_s||^2=T.

The cone C is independent of the positive radii. It is closed: the
linear map with the independent columns w_s is invertible and maps the
closed nonnegative orthant onto C. Its full polar cone is

    C^polar = { (x,y): <x,U_s>+y/R<=0 for EVERY s in G }
            = { (x,y): y<=-R max_s <x,U_s> }.

Define the single static objective

    F_D(z)=max_(g in D) { <z,g>-||g||^2/2 }.

Completing the square proves that its maximizer is the unique Euclidean
projection P_D(z). For g in D the exact condition is

    <z-g,w-g><=0 for every original vertex w of D, including 0.  (2.1)

Necessity follows by differentiating squared distance along each
segment from g; sufficiency follows by expansion and convex combination.
Summing this condition at two points gives

    <z-z',P_D(z)-P_D(z')> >= ||P_D(z)-P_D(z')||^2.

It follows that P_D is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing affine
expressions shows that the first-order remainder of F_D lies between
0 and ||z-z'||^2, so F_D is differentiable with gradient P_D. It is
finite and convex. An open subset of D has projection equal to the
identity, so its least global Lipschitz gradient constant is exactly 1.

Because 0 belongs to D, F_D>=0. If z belongs to C^polar, every inner
product <z,g> is nonpositive and F_D(z)=0. Conversely, if <z,v_j>>0,
the segment t v_j in D gives

    F_D(z)>=t<z,v_j> - t^2||v_j||^2/2 >0

for a sufficiently small positive t. Hence the FULL attained minimum is

    argmin F_D=C^polar, min F_D=0.                         (2.2)

In particular z_star=(0,-R) is a strict polar point since
<z_star,v_i>=-sigma_i. For an initialization z_0=(x_0,-R), the distance
to this particular minimizer is ||x_0||, but it need not be the distance
to the whole minimum cone. Section 6 computes the latter exactly for
the legal law. Section 4 proves that the first proposed law has no
initialization at all, even without the slice y=-R.

Multiplying F_D by any L>0 gives an L-smooth objective with the same
physical iterates under step h_i/L and the same W=2R score.

## 3. Physical Gray order and the adjacent necessary condition

Let Gamma(i)=i xor floor(i/2), with m binary bits. If l=nu_2(i+1),
then the bits of i below l are 1 and bit l is 0. Incrementing i flips
exactly bits 0 through l. Taking xor with the shifted label cancels
these changes except at bit l. Thus

    Gamma(i+1)=Gamma(i)+e_l, l=nu_2(i+1).                  (3.1)

This is the original chronological order, not a permutation of the
word. In particular the first three labels are

    Gamma(0)=0, Gamma(1)=e_0, Gamma(2)=e_0+e_1,

and the two corresponding physical steps are H_0 and H_1.

For a fixed bit l, exactly N/2 character labels have t_l=1. Of these,
exactly one is independent, namely e_l. Consequently

    E_l=E(e_l)=2^l/(N-1)+zeta (N/2-1)/(N^2 Kmix),
    t_l=1-2E_l/T.                                        (3.2)

Every E_l is strictly between 0 and M, so -1<t_l<1.
For an adjacent pair p=sigma w_s and q=sigma r w_(s+e_l),
suppose one physical step H=H_l gives

    P_D(z)=p, z'=z-Hp, P_D(z')=q.

Write d=q-p, r_z=z-p, r_z'=z'-q. The two original normal
inequalities against the other vertex are

    <r_z,d><=0, <r_z',d>>=0,
    r_z-r_z'=(H-1)p+q.

Therefore

    <(H-1)p+q,q-p><=0,

or, after dividing by sigma^2 T,

    r^2+(H-2)t_l r+1-H<=0.                              (3.3)

The constant term is negative because H>1, so the quadratic has one
negative root and one positive root. The positive root is exactly

    rho_l=(-(H_l-2)t_l+
        sqrt((H_l-2)^2 t_l^2+4(H_l-1)))/2.

At r=1 its polynomial equals (2-H_l)(1-t_l). Thus

    0<rho_l<1 if H_l<2,
    rho_l>1 if H_l>2.

When r=rho_l, (3.3) is equality. The two original normal
inequalities then have the SAME inner product, one nonpositive and one
nonnegative, and hence

    <r_z,d>=<r_z',d>=0.                                 (3.4)

This equality consequence is the source of the remote obstruction.
Condition (3.3) by itself is only necessary, not sufficient for a
single static projection potential or for global interpolation.

## 4. Law I: uniform rejection by an original remote row

The first law is exactly

    sigma_0=1,
    sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i r_(nu_2(i+1)),
    r_l=rho_l for H_l<2,
    r_l=1+theta(rho_l-1) for H_l>2, 0<=theta<=1.           (4.1)

**Claim I, joint draft.** For EVERY m>=1536, R>0,
0<zeta<=1, theta in [0,1], there is NO point z_0 in R^N for which
P_D(z_i)=v_i at all the prescribed Gray queries under the original GD
steps. Already queries 0,1,2 are inconsistent. In particular no explicit
x_0 in the required slice z_0=(x_0,-R) can exist. Every later radius or
bridge choice, even one different from (4.1), is irrelevant to this
obstruction if these first two root ratios are retained.

Proof. Abbreviate

    u=rho_0, v=rho_1, e=E_0, f=E_1,
    J=sum_(t:t_0=t_1=1)b_t^2=zeta/(4N Kmix).

The formula for J uses the N/4 labels with both bits 1; none is an
independent label. The first three gradient vectors would be

    p_0=w_0, p_1=u w_(e_0), p_2=u v w_(e_0+e_1).

They have the exact inner products

    <w_0,w_(e_0)>=T-2e,
    <w_(e_0),w_(e_0+e_1)>=T-2f,
    <w_0,w_(e_0+e_1)>=T-2e-2f+4J.                      (4.2)

The last identity is the inclusion-exclusion formula for characters
that change sign in exactly one of bits 0 and 1. The vertical part has
already been included in T in every one of these entries.

Let z_1=z_0-(1+a)p_0, z_2=z_1-(1+beta)p_1, and
let r_i=z_i-p_i. If the three projections existed, (3.4), once for
each of the root-saturated adjacent pairs, would give

    <r_1,p_1-p_0>=0, <r_1,p_2-p_1>=0.

Thus <r_1,p_2-p_0>=0. Also

    r_2=r_1-beta p_1-p_2.

The ORIGINAL query-2 normal against the ORIGINAL vertex p_0 requires

    <r_2,p_0-p_2><=0.

But the left side is forced to equal

    B=(beta p_1+p_2) dot (p_2-p_0)
     =(beta p_1+p_2) dot (p_1-p_0),                     (4.3)

where the last equality uses the root equality at the second physical
step. Substituting (4.2) and collecting terms gives exactly

    B=u[(beta+v) A + 2 v f(1-u) - 4 v J],
    A=T(u-1)+2e.                                       (4.4)

We now prove B>0 uniformly, without an approximation in 1/N or 1/R.
The level-0 root equation is

    u^2-(1-a)(1-2e/T)u-a=0.

Rearranging it gives

    (1-u)(u+a)=2(1-a)e u/T,
    A=2e a(1+u)/(u+a)>2e a,                            (4.5)

since a<1 and u>0. Both u and v lie strictly between 0 and 1 by
Section 3. Thus the middle term in (4.4) is strictly positive and

    B > u v (2e a-4J).

It remains to check this last sign over the entire domain. For m>=3,
N=2^m>=8 and Kmix=N-1-m>=4; the latter follows at m=3 and increases
by 2^m-1 when m is incremented. Also e>=1/(N-1), so

    4J/e <= (N-1)/(N Kmix) < 1/Kmix <= 1/4,
    2a>2/3>1/4.

Therefore 2e a>4J and B>0. This contradicts the required normal row.
It proves Claim I for every m in the requested domain, with no
restriction on R and with no dependence on theta. QED.

This is not a finite-case scan or a sign guessed from small m. It is
one symbolic contradiction uniform in all declared parameters. It is
value-independent within the declared static-body construction: x_0
cancels, and F_D values have no free offsets that could repair the
normal inequality. It is NOT asserted as an impossibility for arbitrary
smooth interpolation with unrelated conjugate offsets, or for every
radial dictionary. In particular a different character-intersection
structure changes J in (4.4) and is outside the rejected scope.

## 5. Every original interpolation row and the inverse rule

These identities are derived jointly and will justify the second law.
For any actual projection run of this same F_D, set

    g_i=P_D(z_i), r_i=z_i-g_i,
    f_i=<z_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,
    z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i g_i.

For every ordered pair of original indices, including terminal
directions, direct expansion gives

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,z_j-z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<r_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                              (5.1)

For any minimizer q in C^polar, give it gradient 0 and value 0. Then

    Q_(i,star)=<g_i,q><=0,
    Q_(star,i)=-<r_i,g_i><=0,                           (5.2)

the second inequality following from the normal test against vertex 0.
Thus all ordered original rows, all minimizer rows, and the real PSD
Gram follow from the body rather than a selected subset of comparisons.

For completeness the finite inverse rule also has a direct proof.
For a finite set of dictionary vertices V including 0, fix h>1 and y,
choose a vertex v maximizing

    score_y,h(v)=<y,v>+(h-1)||v||^2/2,

and put x=y+h v. For every original dictionary vertex w,

    <x-v,w-v>
      =score_y,h(w)-score_y,h(v)-(h-1)||w-v||^2/2<=0.

Condition (2.1) therefore proves P_D(x)=v, and x-hP_D(x)=y.
This proof includes the zero vertex and every remote vertex. It is
only a legality rule until an initial-radius/terminal bound is added.

## 6. Law II: an explicit inverse terminal with an exact full price

Use the SAME dictionary construction with theta=0 in (4.1); do not
prescribe its Gray order as the itinerary. Thus r_0=rho_0<1,
r_1=rho_1<1, and r_l=1 for l>=2. Keep all the N+1 original vertices.
For i>=1, sigma_i<sigma_0=1, because all ratios are at most 1 and the
first ratio is strictly less than 1. Consequently the explicit vertex

    p=v_0=w_0=(U_0,1/R)

is the UNIQUE maximum-norm vertex; ||p||^2=T. This is a genuinely
unequal-radius dictionary, although its actual selected itinerary will
be constant. No maximum search or feasibility optimization is required
to specify p.

For an explicit scalar t>=0, let A_0=S+t and set

    lambda_t=(1+A_0/R^2)/M,
    q_t=(lambda_t U_0,-lambda_t M R),
    y_t=q_t+t p.                                       (6.1)

The whole terminal and initialization are known functions of the word,
the geometry, and t before any inverse selection. They are not fit to
observed iterates. For every nonzero vertex v_j,

    <q_t,v_j>=lambda_t sigma_j(K(0,Gamma(j))-M)<=0,

with equality precisely at j=0, because each distinct label flips at
least one positive character coordinate. Also <q_t,0>=0 and
<q_t,p>=0. Hence q_t belongs to the complete minimum cone (2.2).
The terminal is within t||p|| of this explicitly chosen polar minimum.

The actual initialization obtained below is

    z_0=q_t+(S+t)p
       =(((1+(S+t)T)/M) U_0, -R).                      (6.2)

Thus Law II respects the requested initial vertical slice exactly.
No vertical drift is suppressed or absorbed into an unpaid carrier.
The original supplied z_star=(0,-R) remains a minimizer, but q_t,
rather than z_star, will be the closest minimizer.

For a generic y=q_t+A p with A>=0 and any h>1, comparison of its
inverse scores with p gives

    score(v_j)-score(p)
      =<q_t,v_j> + A(<p,v_j>-T)
         +(h-1)(||v_j||^2-T)/2 <0, j!=0.               (6.3)

Here <p,v_j><=||p||||v_j||<T and ||v_j||^2<T; all signs follow from
the strict maximum radius. For the zero vertex the difference is
-(A+(h-1)/2)T<0. Therefore the inverse rule chooses p uniquely for
EVERY original step, beginning at y_t. The actual reverse construction
has the closed form

    A_i=t+sum_(k=i)^(n-1)h_k,
    z_i=q_t+A_i p, 0<=i<=n.                            (6.4)

This is not left as a recurrence. For i<n, A_i>=h_(n-1)>1.
Consequently every queried gradient is p, which can also be checked
directly: for r_i=q_t+(A_i-1)p, every original vertex w obeys

    <r_i,w-p>=<q_t,w>+(A_i-1)(<p,w>-T)<=0.              (6.5)

The vertical coordinate is the actual physical one:

    (z_i)_vertical=-R-(sum_(k<i)h_k)/R,

and each step changes it by exactly -h_i/R, the required vertical
gradient component of -h_i p. The terminal gradient is

    g_n=min(t,1)p.                                     (6.6)

For 0<=t<=1, this point belongs to the edge [0,p], and the residual
is q_t, which is normal to D and perpendicular to p. For t>=1,
(6.5) with A_i=t proves projection p. Thus every original query,
the actual terminal projection, and every face-boundary case are fixed
by the single body. Equations (5.1)-(5.2) apply to all their original
ordered rows without any interpolation oracle.

The finite-cap normal height is strictly positive at EVERY pre-step
query:

    <z_i-g_i,g_i>=(A_i-1)T>0, i<n.                      (6.7)

Thus this example is not within a zero-origin-height cone-distance
trajectory. Having genuine positive finite-cap height alone does not
give a gain over Huber.

We now pay for the FULL minimum set, not just z_star. For every
q' in C^polar and every A>=0,

    ||q_t+A p-q'||^2
      =A^2 T+||q_t-q'||^2-2A<p,q'> >= A^2 T,

since <p,q_t>=0 and <p,q'><=0. Equality is attained at q'=q_t.
Therefore

    dist(z_i,argmin F_D)=A_i sqrt(T),
    R_init^2=dist(z_0,argmin F_D)^2=(S+t)^2 T.           (6.8)

In contrast, the distance to the specified but nonclosest z_star is

    ||z_0-z_star||=(1+(S+t)T)/sqrt(M).

It is larger, and using it instead would only reduce the score.
Equation (6.8) is an exact closest-radius identity proved against
EVERY polar point, not an upper bound from one convenient minimum.

Finally <q_t,p>=0 and (6.6) give

    2F_D(z_n)=T t^2,          0<=t<=1,
    2F_D(z_n)=T(2t-1),        t>=1.

The complete attained W=2R performance of Law II is exactly

    E(t)=t^2/(S+t)^2,         0<=t<=1,
    E(t)=(2t-1)/(S+t)^2,      t>=1.                     (6.9)

These formulas are independent of R and zeta AFTER the full true price
has been paid. They hold for every m>=1536, R>0, 0<zeta<=1, t>=0,
with the fixed theta=0 dictionary. The initialization is nonoptimal
even at t=0 because S>0, and its radius is nonzero.

## 7. The all-terminal optimization and comparison to the complete channel

For 0<=t<=1, the function t/(S+t) increases with t, so

    E(t)<=1/(S+1)^2<1/(1+2S).

For every t>=1 the exact identity

    (S+t)^2-(1+2S)(2t-1)=(t-(S+1))^2

gives E(t)<=1/(1+2S), with equality at the explicit t=S+1.
Thus the WHOLE-law supremum, including all near-polar terminals,
all exterior terminals, and the actual terminal transition, is

    sup_(t>=0) E(t)=1/D_m <= COMPLETE K_n(h).             (7.1)

The particular t=S+1 member attains it with the full closest-radius
denominator. No nonvanishing gain over the complete cut/Huber channel
is possible for this second law. Any channel containing COMPLETE K_n
is at least as large. A scalar recurrence alone would not prove this;
the exact all-t optimization is essential.

If terminal-near-minimum is imposed concretely as 0<=t<=1, then

    E(t)/(1/D_m) <= D_m/(S+1)^2 < 2/(S+1) < 2/N.         (7.2)

It follows uniformly that the relative score tends to zero as m grows.
Using ONLY the task-supplied accepted comparison B_n/D_m<47/50 gives

    B_n E(t)<47/50 for the entire Law II,
    B_n E(t)<47/(25N) for 0<=t<=1.                      (7.3)

This is the valid direction of that benchmark comparison: a bound on
a selected witness's score, not a lower bound on W_n(h), and certainly
not a full-function worst-case upper bound. The original minimax goal
has not been decided in either direction.

The same proof of (6.3)-(7.1) extends, within this same inverse terminal
mechanism, to any positive radial dictionary whenever p is chosen as a
maximum-norm vertex and q is an explicitly chosen polar normal
perpendicular to p. In that extension replace ||p||^2=T by its actual
norm and arrange the initial slice with its actual vertical component.
No such extension is needed for the stated explicit theta=0 law, and
no claim about terminals having a component transverse to p is made.

## 8. Exact rejected scopes and the remaining quantitative interface

The following are the actual outcomes, all at joint-draft status.

1. **Rejected Law I:** the specified independent-character energies,
   tiny positive mixed remainder, positive R and zeta, all m>=1536,
   original Gray order, exact root ratios at the two H<2 levels, and
   every theta in [0,1] for the higher levels. It fails already on an
   original pair involving queries 0 and 2. There is no legal run and
   hence no terminal/radius score to advertise. Later damping of only
   the H>2 levels cannot repair this law.
2. **Quantitatively exhausted Law II:** theta=0's explicit radial
   dictionary, terminal q_t+t p as in (6.1), all t>=0, and actual
   finite power-score inverse selection under every physical step.
   All normals and the true price are legal, but the best score is
   exactly 1/D_m. Positive finite-cap height does not prevent this
   collapse. This says nothing about all inverse-rule terminals.

For a genuinely different damped Gray law, changing only later steps
is insufficient: at least one of the first two root equalities, or
their character overlap structure, must change. The old local
necessary inequality remains (3.3), but it is not a whole-word
selector. A repaired construction still has to provide an explicit
initialization satisfying EVERY original vertex normal, including
the zero-vertex normal, throughout the actual word. It must then
compute or sharply control its distance to the entire cone (2.2)
and prove a terminal payoff above the COMPLETE channel by a
nonvanishing factor. Neither first-step legality nor an implicit
projection/PEP feasibility problem is this interface's solution.

For a genuinely different inverse law, a terminal with an explicit
component transverse to the chosen maximum-radius ray may avoid
(6.3), but then the first nonconstant inverse selection, the complete
initial price, and the full terminal payoff must all be controlled.
There is no argument here that rules it out. One must not turn the
constant-ray supremum (7.1) into an all-simplex or full-W upper bound.

During this allocation the main agent suggested a different dictionary
whose dominant characters are tau_k=2^m-2^k, with weight
2^k/(N-1), and a tiny positive remainder on the other characters.
That suggestion arrived after the second substantive law had been
derived. It is recorded only as an UNATTEMPTED possible next direction;
no identity or performance claim for it was used or verified here.
In particular Claim I must not be transplanted to that new overlap
structure. This allocation does not authorize beginning that search.

Even a future gain on this one hierarchy would leave the separate
original every-n/every-nonnegative-word minimax bridge open. Conversely,
closing these two witness laws is not evidence of a separating
full-class schedule certificate.

## 9. Resource, provenance, and closeout record

- ONE fresh bounded native analytical invocation; TWO substantive laws.
- Required allocation model/effort: `gpt-6-astra` / `ultra`. No alternate
  model or effort was requested by this invocation. This records the

# One bounded direct search: radial Gray-code vertices with a paid polar minimum

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. ONE direct analytical invocation, requested
gpt-6-astra/ultra, at most TWO substantive laws. No mathematical programs,
arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence replays, solvers, numerical evaluations,
formal calls, fact_submit, paid consults, installations or subagents.
Only public-file reading, text writing and integrity checks are permitted.
Do not change Danus workers, services, truth stores or their TASKs. The
operator explicitly authorized direct Codex reasoning and autonomous bounded
waves. Consult remains off. Finish this allocation on return; no renewal.

Read OPERATOR.md and initialization marker; preserve the full PROBLEM.md.
The original objective is the exact constant/profile comparison W_n^*/U_n^ZJ,
W=2R, over all nonnegative words and all smooth convex functions with minima.
A restricted-family result, numerical evidence or exponent does not solve it.

The new public direct theorem candidate is in
research/direct_sparse_polytope_lift_20261003/RESULT_UNVERIFIED.md
(27346bytes,SHA29095a40ec7ffc4a27a2a5ada51719542f8b9359ca978b768970dd37eb855f72).
It proposes simplex universality in supremum, <=p+2 vertices/dimension<=p+1
for p positive steps, with queried gradients all vertices of UNEQUAL norms.
It is UNVERIFIED, not a proof premise and not a schedule-only selector.
The public main advisory in that directory gives a finite inverse rule for
any h>1: choose v maximizing <y,v>+(h-1)||v||^2/2, set x=y+h v, then
<x-v,w-v>=score(w)-score(v)-(h-1)||w-v||^2/2<=0.
Jointly derive this elementary rule if used. It ensures legality only.

Prior complete public mirrors:
research/shared_evidence/dc755a6e62f17395.md (equal-norm vertex-query freeze
on the whole hierarchy and a narrowly quantified thin mixed-weight law),
research/shared_evidence/8bf359c98a737933.md (three different carried laws),
research/gray_norm_recall_20261003.json (bounded public recall summaries).
Do not read workers' private directories or conversation/local memory.
The equal-norm freeze does NOT by itself exclude radial norm changes.

Fix SAME enclosing m>=1536, N=2^m, n=N-1,
epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
h_i=1+a*c^nu_2(i+1), i=0,...,n-1. Children never reset m.
Let Gamma(i)=i xor floor(i/2) be the m-bit reflected Gray label for each
gradient index0<=i<=n. Gamma(i+1) flips exactly bit nu_2(i+1), matching
the actual physical step's level. No chronological permutation is allowed.

Investigate the following genuinely radial, origin-vertex geometry. Coordinates
of U_s are ALL nonzero t in F_2^m, with U_s(t)=b_t*(-1)^(t dot s).
For independent coordinate labels e_l take
 b_(e_l)^2=2^l/(N-1), 0<=l<m.
For mixed labels, with Kmix=N-1-m, take
 b_t^2=zeta/(N^2*Kmix), 0<zeta<=1.
Every coordinate is real and present. M=sum b_t^2=1+zeta/N^2, and the
exact sign-flip energy is
 E_l=2^l/(N-1)+zeta*(N/2-1)/(N^2*Kmix).
These formulas define an explicit family, not an executed finite-field scan.

For R>0 and positive radii sigma_i, define
 v_i=sigma_i*(U_(Gamma(i)),1/R),
 D=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n},
 F_D(z)=max_(v in D)(<z,v>-||v||^2/2),
 z_star=(0,-R), z_0=(x_0,-R).
The chosen z_star is a true polar-cone minimizer since z_star dot v_i=-sigma_i.
Its initial radius is||x_0||; the own closest radius needs a complete treatment.
Vertical gradients sigma_i/R MUST move the added coordinate under ordinary GD.
The common vertical direction is SCALED with the changing vertex radius, not
an unpriced constant-gradient carrier. Full geometry, minimum set and every
actual row must be proved from the body, not inferred from the unverified
universality candidate.

One concrete first norm law to attack is based on the exact adjacent firm
inequality. Put H_l=1+a*c^l (a scalar step here, not the full word),
 t_l=1-2E_l/(M+1/R^2),
 rho_l=[-(H_l-2)t_l+sqrt((H_l-2)^2*t_l^2+4(H_l-1))]/2.
For a prescribed adjacent Gray pair with radius ratio r, firm nonexpansiveness
requires r^2+(H_l-2)t_l*r+1-H_l<=0. This gives only a NECESSARY local condition.
Jointly verify all signs and the level-matching identities.
Choose sigma_0=1 and sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i*r_(nu_2(i+1)). Permitted explicit
subfamilies are r_l=rho_l for H_l<2 and
r_l=1+theta*(rho_l-1) for H_l>2, 0<=theta<=1. A strict schedule-dependent
damping may be introduced as the second substantive law if a complete
scope attack motivates it. Do not silently assume the adjacent root law
ensures cyclic monotonicity or one static function.

First determine whether the prescribed Gray itinerary can occur in ONE
static body for a suitable EXPLICIT x_0, with all original remote normal
inequalities. A value-independent positive cycle or full original-pair
contradiction is a decisive obstruction to its declared parameter domain.
If it is legal, derive every original query and an all-m terminal score
using the whole minimum cone and the true initial price. The desired outcome
is a NONVANISHING gain over the COMPLETE Huber/cut channel, ideally a valid
comparison with B_n. An exact recurrence or an implicit feasibility/PEP
optimization is not this quantitative result.

As an alternative second mechanism after a decisive first-law failure,
keep one explicit radially rescaled dictionary but let the finite power-score
inverse rule choose the ACTUAL itinerary backwards from an explicitly chosen
terminal near the polar minimum. Then prove full initial-radius/terminal
control; legality alone is insufficient. Do not count a local Gray flip or
an unquantified projection recurrence as success. At most TWO laws total.

Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 gives the UPPER benchmark ratio B_n/D_m<47/50,
D_m=1+2sum h. It cannot be reversed to certify a lower witness gain.
Acceptedb98bb807d514e80d defines the COMPLETE K/cut channel, and
1252b8823bb5a290 gives original interpolation. Use them only at scopes.
No all-simplex impossibility follows from a failure of this selected law.

Write a complete JOINT UNVERIFIED RESULT.md and self-contained HANDOFF.md
in this public directory. Include exact rejected scopes and the remaining
quantitative interface. Original goal remains OPEN unless the ACTUAL original
target is proved and separately accepted; no such acceptance is authorized
here. No outward publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown or automatic

# Handoff: evaluated budget and positive strict-damping cycle

**JOINT UNVERIFIED; original exact minimax target OPEN.**
ONE new bounded allocation, TWO analytical mechanisms, now ended on
return. No formal or computational check was performed. The complete
joint proof is `RESULT.md` in this NEW public directory. All earlier
artifacts remain frozen. The full radial family is still unresolved
outside the explicitly excluded regions below.

## Preserve the original goal and parameter family

The original quantity is W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/
(L||x_0-x_star||^2), W=2R, with W_n^star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h) and
U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. The desired ratio tends to 1 uniformly in EVERY
predetermined nonnegative word at EVERY sufficiently large horizon.
These restricted obstructions are not a full-function upper bound or
a minimax resolution. PROBLEM.md and the all-integer-split benchmark
are unchanged; the accepted upper ratio B_n/D_m<47/50 is not reversed.

Keep the SAME m>=1536, N=2^m, n=N-1,
eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,
H_l=1+a c^l, h_i=H_(nu_2(i+1)), S=sum h_i, D_m=1+2S.
All original leaves and the terminal are retained.

The full correlated dictionary uses tau_k=N-2^k and

    b_(tau_k)^2=2^k/(N-1),
    b_t^2=zeta/[N^2(N-1-m)] for every other nonzero character,
    U_s(t)=b_t(-1)^(t dot s), M=1+zeta/N^2,
    T=M+R^(-2), w_i=(U_(Gamma(i)),1/R),
    Gamma(i)=i xor floor(i/2), v_i=sigma_i w_i,
    D=conv(0,v_0,...,v_n).

All coordinates are real and positive-weighted. Parameters satisfy
R>0, 0<zeta<=1, 0<delta_0,delta_1<=16, 0<=theta<=1. The exact
flip energies and positive adjacent roots are

    E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)
          +zeta(N/2-l-1)/[N^2(N-1-m)],
    rho_l^2+(H_l-2)(1-2E_l/T)rho_l+1-H_l=0.

The prescribed ratios remain

    r_l=rho_l(1-delta_l/N), l=0,1,
    r_l=1+theta(rho_l-1), l>=2,
    sigma_0=1, sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i r_(nu_2(i+1)).

## Full body, minimum, and row convention

Direct Walsh orthogonality proves a simplex in R^N. The single
potential F_D(z)=max_(g in D)(<z,g>-||g||^2/2) is convex, 1-smooth
and has gradient P_D. Its COMPLETE minimum is

    C^polar={(x,y): <x,U_s>+y/R<=0 for every s},
    C=cone{v_i}, min F_D=0.

The positive radial scales do not change this cone. Actual data obey

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,z_j-z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<z_j-g_j,g_i-g_j><=0.

The zero-vertex normal requires f_i>=||g_i||^2/2. The original
initial slice is z_0=(x_0,-R). Its actual vertical motion, if legal,
is -R-R^(-1)sum_(t<i)h_t sigma_t. Its closest radius is distance
to ALL of C^polar, not automatically ||x_0||.

For completeness the acute domain R^(-2)>=M is jointly excluded:
all frame Gram entries are nonnegative, and the original zero normals
give <z_0,w_i>>0 for every original query including the terminal.
Their sum is -N by the Walsh mean, a contradiction. Thus possible
members must have R^(-2)<M, where T<2001/1000.

## Mechanism I: the global terminal budget is actually evaluated

For prescribed vertices set

    P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t v_t,
    A_i=||v_i||^2/2+<P_i,v_i>,
    H=sum_i1/sigma_i, B_t=sum_(i<=t)1/sigma_i,
    L_t=h_t<v_t,v_(t+1)>+||v_t-v_(t+1)||^2/2.

The initial slice forces sum_i(f_i+A_i)/sigma_i=-N. All adjacent
rows require Delta_t=f_t-f_(t+1)>=L_t, so

    f_n<=Fmax=(-N-Z)/H,
    Z=sum_i A_i/sigma_i+sum_(t<n)B_t L_t.

If q<=sigma_i<=b, the proof lower-bounds EVERY term and explicitly
keeps the two level-(m-2) transitions at times N/4-1 and 3N/4-1.
Their full-metric Gram is

    T-2E_(m-2)=R^(-2)+1/(N-1)
                  +zeta(m-3)/[N^2(N-1-m)]>R^(-2).

Exact mass estimates give H_(m-2)>S/20. Therefore, with
gamma=M-R^(-2)>=0,

    Z>=NS[q^2 R^(-2)/(20b)-gamma(b+b^2/q)].

This is an evaluated sign criterion. Define
kappa(q,b)=q^3/[20b^2(q+b)]. Then

    M/(1+kappa(q,b))<=R^(-2)<M

forces Z>=0 and Fmax<=-q<0, incompatible with the ORIGINAL terminal
zero row. It excludes every x_0, not just a midpoint initialization.

For the FULL damping range delta_l<=16, direct root and count bounds
prove every sigma_i>exp(-16). If theta<=1/(16S), every sigma_i<9/8.
Hence with

    q=exp(-16), b=9/8,
    kappa_*=exp(-48)/[20(9/8)^2(exp(-16)+9/8)],

the all-m nonacute band

    theta<=1/(16S), M/(1+kappa_*)<=R^(-2)<M

is excluded for ALL delta_0,delta_1 in (0,16]. Its width is an
explicit positive constant fraction of M, although small. This does
not cover general theta=O(1/m).

For the cleaner subcase delta_0,delta_1<=1/8 and R^(-2)>=M/2, one
can use q=1/2,b=9/8 instead. The evaluated budget is then

    Z>=NS[R^(-2)/90-(117/32)(M-R^(-2))].

It excludes R^(-2)>=(5265/5281)M under theta<=1/(16S). In the
interior R^(-2)>=999M/1000, the explicit bound is

    Fmax< -1/2-SM/400.

No computational sum or recurrence evaluation was used. The terminal
and every original contribution are retained; omitted positive terms
are discarded only in a proved lower bound, and negative terms are
charged explicitly.

## Mechanism II: explicit lower-decrement state and its failed remote row

The extreme-decrement/Walsh candidate is explicitly

    C_i=sum_(t<i)L_t,
    f_0=[-N-sum_i(A_i-C_i)/sigma_i]/H,
    f_i=f_0-C_i, F_i=f_i+A_i,
    x_(0,t)=(1/(N b_t))sum_i chi_t(Gamma(i))F_i/sigma_i,
    z_0=(x_0,-R), z_i=z_0-P_i.

Walsh inversion and sum_iF_i/sigma_i=-N prove
<z_0,v_i>=F_i and <z_i,v_i>-||v_i||^2/2=f_i exactly. This does
NOT make the formal values legal unless all projection normals hold.
Its adjacent lower rows Q_(t+1,t) are zero. In the budget band it
already fails the terminal zero row by at least ||v_n||^2/2+exp(-16).

A separate original remote cycle gives a stronger result. On queries
1,2,3 put

    s=sigma_1, u=r_1, v=r_0,
    p_0=v_1, p_1=v_2, p_2=v_3,
    e=E_1, f=E_0,
    J=1/(N-1)+zeta(N/4-1)/[N^2(N-1-m)],
    A=T(u-1)+2e,
    B=s^2u[(a+v)A+2vf(1-u)-4vJ],
    k_0=-<beta p_0+p_1,p_1-p_0>,
    k_1=-<a p_1+p_2,p_2-p_1>.

The strict firm gaps k_0,k_1 are NONNEGATIVE and are paid, rather
than silently set to zero. Exact algebra yields the value-independent
cycle

    Q_(2,1)+Q_(3,2)+Q_(1,3)=B-k_0-k_1=C.

In the nonacute domain the proof obtains

    B>(s^2/N)[437/100-(14/5)delta_1],
    k_0+k_1<(s^2/N)(401/100)(delta_0+delta_1),
    C>(s^2/(100N))[437-401delta_0-681delta_1],

whenever 401delta_0+681delta_1<437. Every constant and full-metric
overlap is derived in RESULT.md. Thus the entire prescribed law is
impossible in this damping wedge, for any theta. The acute-domain
screen handles the complementary R-domain, so the wedge is excluded
for ALL R>0.

In particular BOTH delta_0,delta_1 in (0,2/5] give

    C>21s^2/(500N)>s^2/(25N)>0.

For the lower-decrement candidate Q_(2,1)=Q_(3,2)=0, so the failed
ORIGINAL normal is precisely

    <z_3-v_3,v_1-v_3>=Q_(1,3)=C>0.

For any other values all three original rows would have to be
nonpositive, contradicting their positive sum. This therefore goes
beyond merely rejecting one initialization. It is also different
from the former exact-root obstruction: both short steps are strictly
damped and their available firm margins are explicitly subtracted.

## Remaining scope and resource closeout

As-yet-unexcluded parameters must have R^(-2)<M,
401delta_0+681delta_1>=437, and avoid the stated near-acute budget band
when theta<=1/(16S). This is not a feasibility claim. General small
theta of order1/m at larger damping remains open. No complete
original-row trajectory, closest-polar price, terminal gain over
COMPLETE K/ray, or original minimax bridge was obtained there.

ONE native allocation with requested gpt-6-astra/ultra; TWO mechanisms;
ZERO mathematical programs, numerical evaluations, arithmetic or
trajectory/recurrence replays, solvers, formal calls/fact_submit,
consults, installations, or subagents. Only public reads, text writes
and hashes were used. The frozen midpoint REPORT.md and evaluate_case.py
were read as text only; their m6/m8 results are not proof premises.
That separate batch remains CLOSED at 10 charged jobs,50 accounted

The new task and main-candidate hashes were confirmed as
21ab26053cd26e22972cba64891782843d48d0ffbb9f0756712983a11a6ee5d3
and 8e73c76eec7cdbb2438559f9455a4d9b1b297dd45f6552c54487c1d27a6468f9.

# A terminal-value budget forced by the initial polar slice

MAIN-AUTHORED UNVERIFIED analytical candidate. No numerical evaluation is
asserted as proof. Original target OPEN.

Take the prescribed vertices of the strictly damped correlated Gray law:
v_i=sigma_i*w_i, w_i=(U_Gamma(i),1/R), i=0,...,n=N-1,
with sigma_i>0 and sum_i U_Gamma(i)=0. Fix the required initial slice
z0=(x0,-R), one body D=conv(0,v_0,...,v_n), and the original word h.
Suppose the prescribed vertices really are the actual projection gradients.
Write P_i=sum_{t<i}h_t v_t, z_i=z0-P_i, and actual values
f_i=<z_i,v_i>-||v_i||^2/2. Then, exactly,

 A_i=||v_i||^2/2+<P_i,v_i>,
 <z0,v_i>=f_i+A_i,
 sum_i (f_i+A_i)/sigma_i=-N.                         (1)

The last equality follows from the full nontrivial Walsh sum and the
moving-coordinate INITIAL slice, not from a chosen minimum normalization.
Set H=sum_i1/sigma_i and B_t=sum_{i=0}^t1/sigma_i>0. If
Delta_t=f_t-f_(t+1), summing f_i=f_n+sum_{t=i}^{n-1}Delta_t in(1) gives

 f_n=[-N-sum_i A_i/sigma_i-sum_{t<n}B_t Delta_t]/H.  (2)

The original adjacent interpolation rows require

 L_t=h_t<v_t,v_(t+1)>+||v_t-v_(t+1)||^2/2
       <=Delta_t
       <=h_t||v_t||^2-||v_t-v_(t+1)||^2/2=U_t.      (3)

The local firm inequality is exactly L_t<=U_t. Since B_t>0, (2) yields
the initialization-independent necessary upper bound

 f_n<=Fmax=[-N-sum_i A_i/sigma_i-sum_{t<n}B_t L_t]/H. (4)

Any legal terminal also has f_n>=||v_n||^2/2 from the original zero-vertex
normal. Thus Fmax<||v_n||^2/2 would exclude the WHOLE prescribed itinerary,
not merely its midpoint values or one chosen x0. Formula(4) alone is an
unevaluated bound, not such an exclusion; its all-m sign remains OPEN.

The extremal-adjacent candidate saturates every lower bound Delta_t=L_t.
Let C_i=sum_{t<i}L_t. It has

 f0=[-N-sum_i(A_i-C_i)/sigma_i]/H,
 f_i=f0-C_i,
 F_i=f_i+A_i,
 x0_t=(1/(N*b_t))sum_i chi_t(Gamma(i))*F_i/sigma_i,
      for every nonzero character t.               (5)

The affine score relation in(1) ensures the constant component equals-1;
Walsh inversion gives <(x0,-R),v_i>=F_i. These are explicit finite formulas
from the known word and parameters. They are NOT asserted feasible. Every
remote normal, zero-vertex row, true minimum/radius, and terminal value still
needs proof. The construction's candidate values are not legal F_D values
if the projection normals fail. Negative formal values are an immediate
failure signal, not negative values of a nonnegative projection potential.

All preceding strict-law parameter screens remain UNVERIFIED awareness:
R^(-2)<M and theta<48/(floor(m/2)-2), with sigma_n>exp(-16).
The completed main midpoint batch found finite m6/m8 violations but cannot
decide any of these asymptotic inequalities. No new computational allowance
is implicit here; all numerical ledgers are closed.
