# Evaluated terminal budget and a strict-damping positive original cycle

**JOINT UNVERIFIED RESULT.** Project `gd-exact-profile-20260926`.
ONE distinct bounded analytical invocation; TWO mechanisms. Every new
assertion below belongs to this joint draft and has not been accepted by
the verifier. The original minimax target remains OPEN.

The terminal-budget mechanism is evaluated on an explicit all-m region,
including a nonempty band of the nonacute domain for the FULL damping
range 0<delta_0,delta_1<=16. The second mechanism reconstructs the explicit
lower-decrement/Walsh initial point, then finds a positive ORIGINAL
three-query cycle. That cycle excludes every possible initialization,
not just the reconstructed point, whenever

    401 delta_0 + 681 delta_1 < 437

in the nonacute domain. In particular BOTH deltas in (0,2/5] are
impossible for EVERY R>0, zeta in (0,1], theta in [0,1], and m>=1536.
This is strict positive damping, not the already rejected equal-root law.

The full declared family is still not excluded. Larger damping outside
the stated wedge, away from the evaluated terminal-budget band, has no
legal explicit witness or terminal/true-radius gain here. No value
sequence, finite prototype, or unevaluated recurrence is called such a
witness. No third mechanism or additional computation was used.

## 1. Original target, geometry, and parameter scope

The original quantity is

    W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2),
    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i),
    W_n^star=inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h), W=2R,
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n=1/(2S_bullet(n)+1).

The affirmative target is W_n^star/U_n^ZJ ->1, uniformly over EVERY
nonnegative predetermined word at EVERY sufficiently large horizon. A
negative resolution requires a full-function worst-case upper certificate
giving a nonvanishing separating subsequence. Restricted witness
infeasibility is neither kind of resolution. The original PROBLEM.md,
all-integer-split benchmark, and normalization are unchanged.

Keep the SAME enclosing integer m>=1536 in every child and set

    N=2^m, n=N-1, eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,
    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,
    H_l=1+a c^l, h_i=H_(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n,
    S=sum_(i=0)^(n-1)h_i, D_m=1+2S.

The fixed parameters, permitted to depend explicitly on m before the run,
are R>0, 0<zeta<=1, 0<delta_0,delta_1<=16 and 0<=theta<=1.
Elementary bounds used below are

    beta>999/1000, 2<c<49/20<5/2,
    2/5<a<1/2, H_0=1+a<2, H_1=1+beta<2,
    H_l>2 for l>=2.

Indeed eps<=1/6144<1/1000 and 1+sqrt(2)<17/7 imply
c<(17/7)(1001/1000)<49/20. Thus
a>(999/1000)/(49/20)=999/2450>2/5. All inequalities are
symbolic uniform estimates, not a numerical replay.

Let tau_k=N-2^k, Kmix=N-1-m, and retain every nonzero character
t in F_2^m with positive weight

    b_(tau_k)^2=2^k/(N-1), 0<=k<m,
    b_t^2=zeta/(N^2 Kmix) on the remaining labels,
    U_s(t)=b_t(-1)^(t dot s),
    M=sum b_t^2=1+zeta/N^2, T=M+R^(-2),
    w_s=(U_s,1/R), Gamma(i)=i xor floor(i/2).

Write w_i for w_(Gamma(i)) only when i is an original time index.
The actual proposed vertices and static body are

    v_i=sigma_i w_i, D=conv(0,v_0,...,v_n),
    F_D(z)=max_(g in D) [<z,g>-||g||^2/2].

For a physical Gray flip of bit l the exact marginal energy and cosine
are

    E_l=(2^(l+1)-1)/(N-1)
          +zeta(N/2-l-1)/(N^2 Kmix),
    t_l=1-2E_l/T.

The positive root rho_l solves

    rho_l^2+(H_l-2)t_l rho_l+1-H_l=0.

The law under examination is

    r_l=rho_l(1-delta_l/N), l=0,1,
    r_l=1+theta(rho_l-1), 2<=l<m,
    sigma_0=1, sigma_(i+1)=sigma_i r_(nu_2(i+1)).        (1.1)

Because N>16 all ratios are positive. The root polynomial at 1 is
(2-H_l)(1-t_l), so 0<rho_l<1 for l=0,1 and rho_l>1 otherwise.
Thus the low ratios are strictly below their roots, while the high
ratios are between 1 and rho_l. All local necessary firm inequalities
hold. No global feasibility is inferred from this.

## 2. Needed geometry and all-original-row conventions, derived jointly

Gray bit l equals i_l xor i_(l+1). Xoring bits k through m-1 telescopes,
so chi_(tau_k)(Gamma(i))=(-1)^(i_k). Incrementing i flips Gray bit
nu_2(i+1), with no permutation of chronological samples. Among the
characters with bit l set, exactly l+1 are dominant; this proves the
formula for E_l above. For the overlap of bits 0 and 1,

    J=1/(N-1)+zeta(N/4-1)/(N^2 Kmix),
    E_1-2J=1/(N-1).                                   (2.1)

Every frame has squared norm T, and

    <w_s,w_u>=T-2E(s+u),

where E(d) sums weights of all characters changing sign under d.
Walsh orthogonality, proved by pairing group elements differing in one
bit, gives sum_s U_s=0 and makes the full Walsh matrix invertible.
The N vectors w_s, and hence the positively rescaled v_i, are linearly
independent. D is a full-dimensional simplex in R^N including vertex 0.

Completing the square identifies the unique maximizer of F_D as P_D(z).
The exact projection conditions are

    g in D, <z-g,w-g><=0 for EVERY original vertex w, including 0. (2.2)

Expanding squared distance proves sufficiency; differentiation along
each segment proves necessity. Adding the two normal inequalities at
two points proves firm nonexpansiveness and thus 1-Lipschitz continuity
of P_D. Comparison of the maximizing affine expressions proves that
F_D is differentiable with gradient P_D. It is finite and convex; on
the interior of D it equals ||z||^2/2, so its least smoothness constant
is exactly 1.

The complete minimum is the polar of the closed cone generated by D:

    C=cone{v_i},
    argmin F_D=C^polar
       ={(x,y): <x,U_s>+y/R<=0 for EVERY s}, min F_D=0.   (2.3)

Indeed 0 gives value 0, every polar point has value 0, and any positive
dot product with a generator gives a positive value on a sufficiently
short segment from 0 to that generator. The cone is closed because it
is an invertible linear image of the nonnegative orthant. Its polar is
independent of the positive radii. The specified z_star=(0,-R) is a
strict polar minimizer, with dot product -sigma_i against v_i.

For any ACTUAL run, let g_i=P_D(z_i), r_i=z_i-g_i and
f_i=<z_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Its original ordered interpolation rows are

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,z_j-z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<r_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                            (2.4)

For any polar minimizer q with gradient/value 0,
Q_(i,star)=<g_i,q><=0 and Q_(star,i)=-<r_i,g_i><=0.
In particular the zero-vertex row requires

    f_i>=||g_i||^2/2.                                 (2.5)

These facts are derived from one body. No prior interpolation draft,
universality result, or numerical Gram is adopted as a premise.
Multiplication of F_D by L gives the original L-smooth normalization
and leaves the physical iterates and W=2R payoff unchanged.

We also rederive a useful whole-domain screen. If R^(-2)>=M, then
all frame Gram entries are nonnegative. For a prescribed run from
z_0=(x_0,-R), put P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t v_t. The original zero-vertex
normal, divided by sigma_i>0, requires

    <z_0,w_i> >= <P_i,w_i>+sigma_i T>0.

Summing over all N original queries, terminal included, gives -N>0
because sum_i w_i=(0,N/R), a contradiction. Therefore the only
potentially legal geometric domain is

    0<R^(-2)<M.                                       (2.6)

Inside it, T<2M<2001/1000. The final bound follows from
M=1+zeta/N^2 and the declared range of N.

## 3. Mechanism I: exact terminal budget from positive original-row weights

Suppose the entire prescribed itinerary is legal. Set

    P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t v_t,
    A_i=||v_i||^2/2+<P_i,v_i>,
    H=sum_(i=0)^n 1/sigma_i,
    B_t=sum_(i=0)^t 1/sigma_i.

Here H is a positive scalar weight sum, not the physical level H_l.
The actual function formula gives <z_0,v_i>=f_i+A_i. Divide by
sigma_i and sum over ALL original vertices:

    sum_i (f_i+A_i)/sigma_i=-N.                        (3.1)

This uses the INITIAL vertical slice, not a false assumption that the
vertical coordinate stays fixed later. In fact it moves by
-h_i sigma_i/R at every physical step.

Let Delta_t=f_t-f_(t+1). The two adjacent original rows give exactly

    L_t=h_t<v_t,v_(t+1)>+||v_t-v_(t+1)||^2/2
          <=Delta_t
          <=h_t||v_t||^2-||v_t-v_(t+1)||^2/2=U_t.      (3.2)

Substitute f_i=f_n+sum_(t=i)^(n-1)Delta_t into (3.1). Since every
B_t is positive,

    f_n=[-N-sum_i A_i/sigma_i-sum_(t<n)B_t Delta_t]/H
        <=[-N-Z]/H=:Fmax,
    Z=sum_i A_i/sigma_i+sum_(t<n)B_t L_t.              (3.3)

The original terminal zero-vertex row also requires
f_n>=||v_n||^2/2>0. Consequently a proved Z>=0 excludes EVERY
initialization for the fixed itinerary. Choosing lower decrements
maximizes the formal terminal value under the weighted slice relation;
it does not by itself prove the existence of a function or trajectory.

## 4. Evaluation of the budget: a general explicit all-m criterion

The following bound evaluates Z, using all original steps and two
particular positive contributions, rather than leaving it as a sum.
Suppose known constants 0<q<=b bound every prescribed radius:

    q<=sigma_i<=b, 0<=i<=n.

Write v=R^(-2), gamma=M-v>=0. Every frame Gram is at least -gamma
by Cauchy-Schwarz on the horizontal coordinates. Expand the first
term of Z:

    sum_i A_i/sigma_i
       =(T/2)sum_i sigma_i
          +sum_(t<n)h_t sigma_t sum_(i>t)<w_t,w_i>
       >=-b gamma N S.                               (4.1)

For all t, the squared-distance term in L_t is nonnegative, so
L_t>=-h_t b^2 gamma. Also B_t<=N/q. Thus every omitted step in the
next lower bound costs at most gamma N b^2 h_t/q.

Now KEEP the TWO original level-(m-2) steps, whose time indices are
N/4-1 and 3N/4-1. Direct substitution into E_(m-2) gives the exact
identity

    T-2E_(m-2)
      =v+1/(N-1)+zeta(m-3)/(N^2 Kmix)>v.               (4.2)

Each of these two L_t is at least H_(m-2) q^2 v, and
B_t>=(t+1)/b. Their combined positive contribution is therefore at
least N H_(m-2) q^2 v/b. Charging every other contribution, even
overcharging by using the whole S for the negative part, yields

    sum_(t<n)B_t L_t
       >=N H_(m-2) q^2 v/b - gamma N S b^2/q.          (4.3)

To compare the bridge with the FULL word mass, exact level counting
gives

    S=[a/(c-2)]c^m+[1-a/(c-2)]N-1<c^m.

Here 0<a/(c-2)<1 because c>1+sqrt(2) and
(1+sqrt(2))((1+sqrt(2))-2)=1. Also
H_(m-2)>c^(m-2)/3, hence H_(m-2)>S/20 from c<5/2.
Combining (4.1)-(4.3) proves the EVALUATED bound

    Z >= N S [q^2 v/(20b) - gamma(b+b^2/q)].           (4.4)

In particular define the explicit positive constant

    kappa(q,b)=q^3/[20b^2(q+b)].

For any radii with the stated bounds, the whole prescribed trajectory
is impossible whenever

    M/(1+kappa(q,b)) <= R^(-2) < M.                   (4.5)

Indeed (4.5) makes the bracket in (4.4) nonnegative, so
Fmax<=-N/H<=-q<0, contradicting (2.5). Equality at the lower boundary
is also excluded. This is a nonempty band of the previously surviving
nonacute region. It is not an upper bound on the full W_n(h).

All time indices, the terminal term in (3.1), and every child mass
are original. No transition has been chronologically reordered or
deleted. Equations (4.1) and (4.3) lower-bound, rather than ignore,
every contribution not selected as a positive one.

## 5. Explicit radius bounds and resulting rejected parameter regions

We now supply q,b analytically for the actual law (1.1).
For a short step H_l=1+d, its root identity is

    (1-rho_l)(rho_l+d)=2(1-d)E_l rho_l/T.

Counting (1.1)'s weights gives E_0<2/N and E_1<4/N, hence
1-rho_0<=4/N and 1-rho_1<=8eps/N. The elementary inequality

    -x/(1-x)<=log(1-x)<=-x, 0<=x<1,

follows by integrating 1/(1-u). It gives
log rho_0>=-5/N and log rho_1>=-9eps/N. The exact full-word counts
at levels 0 and 1 are N/2 and N/4. Thus their undamped-root product
is greater than exp(-3). The EXTRA damping factor is exactly

    (1-delta_0/N)^(N/2)(1-delta_1/N)^(N/4)
      >=exp[-(delta_0/2+delta_1/4)/(1-16/N)]>exp(-13).

Every higher ratio is at least 1, and no prefix has more short-level
counts than the whole word. Therefore

    sigma_i>exp(-16) for EVERY original i.             (5.1)

This jointly derives, rather than assumes, the previous radius bound.
It also shows why O(N) occurrences of O(1/N) damping are not an
exponential-loss argument in N or m.

For a high step H_l>2, substitution of H_l-1 into its root polynomial
gives (H_l-1)(H_l-2)(1+t_l)>0. Hence rho_l<H_l-1, so
log r_l<=theta(rho_l-1)<theta H_l. Dropping negative short logs and
counting every high occurrence gives

    log sigma_i<=theta S.

Thus the explicit all-m restriction

    0<=theta<=1/(16S)                                 (5.2)

implies sigma_i<exp(1/16)<=16/15<9/8. Taking

    q=exp(-16), b=9/8,
    kappa_*=exp(-48)/[20(9/8)^2(exp(-16)+9/8)]

in (4.5) proves a nonempty, completely explicit excluded band for
ALL 0<delta_0,delta_1<=16:

    theta<=1/(16S),
    M/(1+kappa_*)<=R^(-2)<M.                          (5.3)

For this entire all-m region, Fmax<=-exp(-16). The band is narrow,
but its width is an explicit positive constant fraction of M and is
independent of m. No claim that it covers general theta=O(1/m) is made.

A wider and simpler example follows when both deltas are at most 1/8
and R^(-2)>=M/2. In this case T>=3/2. Since a>1/3,
2(1-a)/(1+a)<1 and the root identity improves to
1-rho_0<=E_0/T. More explicitly,

    E_0<=1/N+3/N^2<=17/(16N),
    log rho_0>=-3/(4N), log rho_1>=-6eps/N.

The first log bound follows from
(17/24)/(1-17/(24N))<=3/4, and the second from the short-root
identity and E_1<4/N. The total damping log is greater than -1/10,
as delta_0/2+delta_1/4<=3/32. Thus every prefix radius exceeds
exp(-3/8-(3/2)eps-1/10)>exp(-1/2)>1/2.
The last inequality follows, for example, from exp(x)<1/(1-x)
at x=1/2. Under (5.2) we may consequently use q=1/2,b=9/8 in (4.4):

    Z >= NS [R^(-2)/90 -(117/32)(M-R^(-2))].           (5.4)

This excludes

    0<delta_0,delta_1<=1/8, theta<=1/(16S),
    (5265/5281)M<=R^(-2)<M.                           (5.5)

For the explicit interior subband R^(-2)>=999M/1000, the bracket
in (5.4) exceeds M/200, since
999/90000-117/32000=1191/160000>1/200. As H<2N,

    Fmax< -1/2-SM/400.                                (5.6)

These are actual all-m sign and magnitude estimates for the original
terminal budget, not an unevaluated recurrence. Section 7 will exclude
the small-delta region even more broadly by a different mechanism.

## 6. Mechanism II: explicit lower-decrement/Walsh initialization

For the fixed word and vertices, set

    C_i=sum_(t<i)L_t,
    f_0=[-N-sum_i(A_i-C_i)/sigma_i]/H,
    f_i=f_0-C_i, F_i=f_i+A_i,
    q_i=F_i/sigma_i,
    x_(0,t)=(1/(N b_t))sum_i chi_t(Gamma(i))q_i
       for EVERY nonzero character coordinate t,
    z_0=(x_0,-R), z_i=z_0-P_i.                        (6.1)

All sums are explicit finite definitions from the prescribed word and
parameters. No optimization or numerical fitting is required to state
this candidate, and no such computation was executed. Since sum_i q_i=-N,
Walsh orthogonality gives

    <x_0,U_(Gamma(j))>=q_j+1,
    <z_0,v_j>=F_j,
    <z_j,v_j>-||v_j||^2/2=f_j.                        (6.2)

Thus the reconstruction of the formal values is exact in the required
initial slice. They are ACTUAL values of F_D only if ALL projection
normals hold. In particular negative formal values do not mean a
negative value of the nonnegative function F_D.

For this candidate every adjacent lower decrement is saturated,
Delta_t=L_t. Equivalently Q_(t+1,t)=0, the original residual at query
t being perpendicular to v_(t+1)-v_t. The opposite adjacent row is
nonpositive by the local firm inequality. No remote or zero row
follows automatically.

In the band (5.3), this explicit candidate has f_n=Fmax<=-exp(-16),
so its ORIGINAL terminal zero-vertex normal is

    <z_n-v_n,-v_n>=||v_n||^2/2-f_n
       >=||v_n||^2/2+exp(-16)>0.                      (6.3)

Under (5.6) it is larger than ||v_n||^2/2+1/2+SM/400.
These are precisely identified failed original rows. They cannot be
repaired by relabeling the formal f_i as legal function values.

We next derive a separate REMOTE row obstruction that applies well
outside this near-acute budget band and extends to every value choice.

## 7. A positive original cycle with strict damping

Consider ORIGINAL queries 1,2,3, whose two physical steps are
H_1=1+beta followed by H_0=1+a. Let

    s=sigma_1>0, u=r_1, v=r_0,
    e=E_1, f=E_0, J as in (2.1),
    p_0=v_1=sW_0, p_1=v_2=suW_1, p_2=v_3=suvW_2.

Their successive Gray flips are 1 and 0, with exact unscaled Gram
entries T-2e, T-2f and T-2e-2f+4J. The strict damping is retained.
Define the two nonnegative adjacent firm gaps

    k_0=-<(beta p_0+p_1),p_1-p_0>,
    k_1=-<(a p_1+p_2),p_2-p_1>.

Also define

    B=<a p_1+p_2,p_1-p_0>
      =s^2 u[(a+v)A+2vf(1-u)-4vJ],
    A=T(u-1)+2e.                                     (7.1)

The expansion in (7.1) uses the three full Euclidean Gram entries,
including the moving vertical coordinate; no root equality is used.

Add the three original ordered interpolation rows for the cycle
2 -> 1, 3 -> 2, 1 -> 3, in the Q convention (2.4). All function
values cancel. Direct substitution of the two physical steps yields

    Q_(2,1)+Q_(3,2)+Q_(1,3)=B-k_0-k_1=:C.             (7.2)

For an explicit check, write local d_0=beta, d_1=a. The right side
expands to

    -d_0||p_0||^2+(d_0-1-d_1)<p_0,p_1>
      +||p_1||^2+d_1<p_1,p_2>-<p_0,p_2>+||p_2||^2,

which is exactly the sum of the three rows after the two GD updates.
Every legal interpolation row must be nonpositive. Hence C>0 is a
value-independent obstruction to EVERY initial point. For the
specific candidate (6.1), its first two rows in (7.2) are zero, so
its ORIGINAL query-3 normal against vertex v_1 is exactly

    <z_3-v_3,v_1-v_3>=Q_(1,3)=C.                      (7.3)

This is not the old double-root argument: both k_0 and k_1 are
strictly positive and are paid explicitly.

## 8. Uniform evaluation of the cycle and the rejected damping wedge

By Section 2, the acute domain is already impossible for the full
itinerary. Work now in R^(-2)<M, so T<2001/1000. From the short-root
bounds and delta_l<=16,

    u>=1-17/N, v>=1-20/N,
    u,v>=999/1000,
    J<=1/N+(5/2)/N^2<=1001/(1000N).                   (8.1)

For clarity, Kmix>=N/2 in the present m range. Thus the mixed part
of J is at most 1/(2N^2), while
1/(N-1)<=1/N+2/N^2, proving its displayed bound. The bounds on u,v
use 1-rho_0<=4/N, 1-rho_1<=8eps/N and eps<1/1000.
N>=32768 is already enough for (8.1); the actual range is much larger.

Let u_bar=rho_1 before damping. Its exact root identity gives

    T(u_bar-1)+2e=2e beta(1+u_bar)/(u_bar+beta)>2e beta.

Since u=u_bar(1-delta_1/N), this implies

    A>2e beta-T delta_1/N
      >[5994/1000-(2001/1000)delta_1]/N.               (8.2)

Suppose

    401 delta_0+681 delta_1<437.                       (8.3)

Then delta_1<1, so the lower bound in (8.2) is positive. Also
a+v>1399/1000. Discard the positive term 2vf(1-u) in (7.1),
use v<=1, and then u>=999/1000. The bracket stays positive for
delta_1<1, giving the convenient rational lower bound

    B > (s^2/N)[437/100-(14/5)delta_1].                (8.4)

All constants in this simplification can be checked exactly: before
rounding outward, the constant coefficient is
4377224394/1000000000>437/100 and the magnitude of the delta_1
coefficient is 2796599601/1000000000<14/5. These rational inequalities
come from multiplying the displayed symbolic bounds; they are not a
numerical trajectory or an external arithmetic computation.

We must also PAY both firm gaps. Let P_d(r)=r^2+(d-1)t r-d for the
appropriate low-level cosine t>0 and root rho. Since
r=rho(1-delta/N),

    -P_d(r)=(rho-r)[rho+r+(d-1)t]<=2delta/N.

Both cosines are positive because E_1<4/N and T>1. Therefore

    k_0<=2s^2 T delta_1/N,
    k_1<=2s^2 u^2 T delta_0/N,
    k_0+k_1<(s^2/N)(401/100)(delta_0+delta_1).          (8.5)

Combining (8.4)-(8.5) proves the EVALUATED positive-cycle bound

    C>(s^2/(100N))[437-401delta_0-681delta_1]>0.         (8.6)

Thus (8.3) excludes the whole prescribed radial itinerary, for every
m>=1536, every zeta in (0,1], every theta in [0,1], and EVERY R>0:
Section 2 handles R^(-2)>=M, while (8.6) handles the rest. No
initialization, no choice of actual function values, and no adjustment
of any later large-step ratio repairs this three-query cycle.

In particular the nonempty rectangle

    0<delta_0,delta_1<=2/5                            (8.7)

is excluded uniformly. Indeed its worst right-side bracket is
437-(401+681)(2/5)=21/5, so

    C>21s^2/(500N)>s^2/(25N).                         (8.8)

For the explicit lower-decrement/Walsh candidate this is exactly the
strictly positive remote normal (7.3). For arbitrary values, (7.2)
is a sum of three original rows which would all have to be
nonpositive. The obstruction therefore strengthens a candidate-level
failure into a whole-itinerary failure on the stated parameter scope.
No claim is made at or beyond the boundary of (8.3) from this estimate.

## 9. True-radius accounting and precise remaining domain

For any hypothetical legal prescribed trajectory from z_0=(x_0,-R),
the actual vertical coordinate is exactly

    (z_i)_vertical=-R-R^(-1)sum_(t<i)h_t sigma_t.

Its true initial price is

    R_init=dist((x_0,-R),C^polar),

not automatically ||x_0||. The latter is only the distance to the
particular valid minimum z_star=(0,-R). Positive origin-normal cap
height <z_i-v_i,v_i> and slack about another polar minimum remain
distinct quantities. The score, only if all original normals hold,
would be

    [2<(x_0,-R),v_n>-2 sum_(t<n)h_t<v_t,v_n>-||v_n||^2]
       /R_init^2.

No paid score is attached to a candidate that fails (6.3) or (7.3).
In those regions the construction is invalid before any closest-radius
optimization. Outside those regions, no proof of all original normal
rows or a useful true-radius terminal estimate was obtained here.

Within the parameter family of the task, every as-yet-unexcluded
selection must satisfy at least

    R^(-2)<M,
    401delta_0+681delta_1>=437,

and must avoid the budget band (5.3) whenever theta<=1/(16S).
The previous report's additional small-theta restriction is retained
as UNVERIFIED awareness, but it is not needed as a premise for any
new exclusion here. In particular the present proof does NOT close

and does NOT establish a gain over the COMPLETE K/ray channel.

Even a future all-m witness on this one hierarchy would not by itself
resolve the separate original every-n/every-word minimax bridge.
Conversely none of these restricted impossibility results is a
full-function upper bound or a negative resolution of that goal.

## 10. Resource, provenance, and end-of-allocation record

- ONE DISTINCT fresh analytical invocation; TWO mechanisms: evaluated

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000544 -->
# An explicit growing-facet selector, and a whole-law obstruction

**UNVERIFIED. The original exact-profile minimax target remains OPEN.**

This is one bounded direct analytical invocation. It supplies one explicit
schedule-derived mechanism: a single maximum of coupled affine facets whose
gradients have a shared component and a different fresh coordinate at each
query. The zero-shared-component case is a subfamily of the same mechanism,
not a second candidate. No second mechanism is pursued.

The construction does not start from an unknown adverse trajectory, use a
feasible-interpolation oracle, or assume the direct representation draft.
All facet slopes, heights along the run, the initial point, the minimum set,
the true normalization, and a fixed-extra-coordinate realization are given
below. Every physical query and every remote support constraint is proved.

The result is negative for this law, rather than a solution of the original
problem. On the ENTIRE SAME-enclosing-m hierarchy H_(m,1/4), for every
m >= 1536, the supremum of its exact envelope payoffs, over every shared
component strength and every permitted terminal height, is precisely the
canonical Huber value. The prescribed finite-height polyhedral realization
can add at most U_n^ZJ/1000. Even after including the full canonical/cut
channel, its normalized original-benchmark ratio is strictly below 949/1000.
This obstruction includes the actual short final step and all tree levels.

## 1. Scope and accepted inputs

For h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) >= 0, the original performance is

    W_n(h) = sup 2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2),
    x_(t+1) = x_t - (h_t/L) grad f(x_t).

The supremum permits every finite real dimension, convex differentiable
function with L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, and chosen
minimizer x_star. We set L=1. Multiplying a displayed whole objective by L
preserves its physical iterates and normalized performance for general L.

The original all-integer-split benchmark is

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

The original missing assertion is a lower bound (1-epsilon)/B_n for every
sufficiently large ACTUAL n and every nonnegative physical word, with one
threshold independent of that word. A deficient explicit lower functional
does not refute that assertion or provide any upper bound on unrestricted W.

Accepted facts used as correctness premises:

* b98bb807d514e80d: its original benchmark definitions, its canonical/cut
  channel, and its bound B_n <= 1+4 n^p, p=log_2(3)<2, for n>=1.
* 5fd396eba75651e7: its exact SAME-enclosing-m definition and ONLY its
  Section 10 scalar inequality

      B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m) < 237/250, m>=1536, tau=1/4.

The interpolation fact 1252b8823bb5a290 was read as background, but no
completion, duality, or interpolation sufficiency statement from it is
needed here. Its elementary necessity inequality is rederived below.
The earlier direct draft and shared draft 25e480a41fc81415 were read for
the task interface and excluded mechanisms. Neither is a correctness
premise. In particular, none of the latter's new laws or scoped failures
is assumed in the proof below. No spiral-family optimality is used.

## 2. One explicit law on words with all h_i>1

This section includes n=0, with the condition on entries then vacuous.
Fix the whole word before constructing any function. Set

    S=sum_(i<n) h_i, A=S+1,
    r_n=1, T_n=1,
    r_i^2 = [product_(i<t<n) h_t/(h_t-1)]/(h_i-1), i<n,
    T_i = product_(i<=t<n) h_t/(h_t-1), 0<=i<=n,
    T_(n+1)=0.

All square roots are the positive real ones. These finite closed formulas
give

    T_i=r_i^2+T_(i+1),
    (h_i-1)r_i^2=T_(i+1), i<n.

Write w_i=h_i for i<n and w_n=1, and define

    J=sum_(i=0)^n 1/r_i^2,
    D=sum_(i=0)^n w_i^2 r_i^2.

In particular J>=1, D>=1, and w_i r_i^2=T_i for all i<=n.
These formulas are mathematical definitions, not executed recurrences.

For the whole-law statement allow any two real parameters z>=0 and b>=0.
They are fixed before the run. A completely deterministic selection from
the word, with no remaining choice, is

    z=D, b=S/J.                                             (2.1)

The larger two-parameter family is useful because its obstruction rules
out repairing this same law by changing either of these parameters.

Use R^(n+2) with its ordinary Euclidean metric and orthonormal basis
e_0,...,e_(n+1). Define, for 0<=i<=n,

    g_i=sqrt(z)e_0+r_i e_(i+1),
    v=sqrt(z)J e_0+sum_(i=0)^n e_(i+1)/r_i,
    p(y)=max{0, <g_0,y>, ..., <g_n,y>},
    x_0=sum_(i=0)^n w_i g_i+b v,
    f(x)=min_y {p(y)+||x-y||^2/2}.                          (2.2)

There are n+1 nonzero affine facets, all with intercept zero and weight
one. They belong to ONE coupled maximum, not a sum of independent
objectives. Their Gram matrix is explicitly

    <g_i,g_j>=z+1_(i=j)r_i^2.

This is an actual real Euclidean Gram matrix. In particular its positive
diagonal part is not a formal PSD variable to be chosen later.

The proposed original points and proximal heights are

    P_i=sum_(t<i) h_t, s_i=sum_(i<=t<n) h_t,
    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i) h_t g_t, y_i=x_i-g_i,
    delta_i=z s_i+T_(i+1)+b(1+zJ), 0<=i<=n.                (2.3)

Here s_n=0 and T_(n+1)=0. In particular

    x_n=g_n+b v,
    y_n=b v,
    delta_n=b(1+zJ).                                      (2.4)

The positive middle heights in (2.3) are consequences of the same fixed
planes and coordinates. They are not independent value assignments.

## 3. All original queries and all remote support guards

Fix any original index i, including i=n. The common coordinate of y_i
is sqrt(z)(s_i+bJ). For its other coordinates, subtracting the actual
prefix updates in (2.3) gives

    (y_i)_(j+1)=b/r_j                         if j<i,
    (y_i)_(i+1)=(w_i-1)r_i+b/r_i,
    (y_i)_(j+1)=w_j r_j+b/r_j                if j>i.

Consequently the current plane has value exactly delta_i. Every earlier
plane j<i has value

    z s_i+b(1+zJ)=delta_i-T_(i+1),

and every later plane j>i has value

    z s_i+b(1+zJ)+T_j=delta_i-T_(i+1)+T_j.

The tails T_j decrease with j, so T_j<=T_(i+1) whenever j>i. Also
delta_i>=0. Thus ALL old, current, future, and zero planes satisfy

    p(y_i)=delta_i, g_i in partial p(y_i).                  (3.1)

There is no nearest-neighbor restriction. Adjacent facets may tie;
that does not prevent the stated vector from being a subgradient.

For completeness, any finite convex p>=0 has a unique minimizer y(x)
in the defining expression for f in (2.2), by coercivity and strict
convexity. Its optimality condition is x-y(x) in partial p(y(x)).
The two subgradient inequalities at x and x' give, for
u(x)=x-y(x),

    <u(x)-u(x'),x-x'> >= ||u(x)-u(x')||^2.                 (3.2)

For d=x'-x, evaluation at the two minimizers gives

    f(x')-f(x)<=<u(x),d>+||d||^2/2,
    f(x')-f(x)>=<u(x'),d>-||d||^2/2.

Together with (3.2), these bound the remainder after subtracting
<u(x),d> in absolute value by ||d||^2/2. Thus grad f(x)=u(x).
Equation (3.2) gives a 1-Lipschitz gradient. Joint convexity of
p(y)+||x-y||^2/2 proves convexity of f. It is finite everywhere.

Equation (3.1) now proves that y_i is the UNIQUE minimizer defining f
at x_i, so

    grad f(x_i)=g_i,
    f(x_i)=delta_i+||g_i||^2/2.                            (3.3)

Induction on the ORIGINAL physical indices proves every proposed update
in (2.3). In particular the final physical step is included before the
value in (2.4) is evaluated. No additional terminal step is taken.

All ordered interpolation rows follow directly as well. For ANY i,j,
including the terminal, put

    Q_ij=f(x_i)-f(x_j)+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Exact cancellation gives

    Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i> <=0,                 (3.4)

by (3.1). This includes both orientations and every remote pair.
The full Gram of (x_0,g_0,...,g_n) is the Gram of the real coordinates
in (2.2), with no omitted vector or adjusted radius.

## 4. Complete minima, true radius, and exact terminal payoff

The complete minimum set is the polyhedral cone

    C={x:<g_i,x><=0 for every 0<=i<=n}, min f=0.            (4.1)

Indeed p>=0, and a zero of its envelope requires simultaneously y=x
and p(y)=0; conversely those conditions make the value zero.

Since v=sum_i r_i^(-2)g_i, the initial point is

    x_0=sum_i (w_i+b/r_i^2)g_i.

All coefficients are positive. For any x in C, <x_0,x><=0, and hence

    ||x_0-x||^2 >= ||x_0||^2,

with equality only at x=0. Thus zero is the UNIQUE closest minimizer.
The true squared radius is

    N=||x_0||^2
     =z(A+bJ)^2+D+2Ab+b^2J >0.                            (4.2)

This proof is for the displayed complete set, not an inherited radius
from a different function. At any minimum x, the two optimum rows in
(3.4) are explicitly Q_(i,star)=<g_i,x><=0 and
Q_(star,i)=-delta_i<=0.

The ACTUAL terminal performance of the exact envelope is therefore

    V_z,b(h)=[z+1+2b(1+zJ)]/
                    [z(A+bJ)^2+D+2Ab+b^2J].              (4.3)

This is a completely evaluated value on a static globally admissible
function, without an unspecified terminal maximization.

## 5. Why the innovation product is genuinely forced

This observation explains the mechanism's obstruction; it is not needed
to assume correctness of its construction. Consider zero common component
and an arbitrary proposed sequence of distinct orthogonal gradients
g_i=r_i e_(i+1), with arbitrary affine intercepts and proximal heights
delta_i. Suppose each advertised plane is really active at y_i=x_i-g_i,
all original GD updates use these gradients, and the zero plane is
retained. For i<j, the two actual plane comparisons necessarily give

    r_j^2 <= delta_i-delta_j <= (h_i-1)r_i^2.              (5.1)

The left inequality uses the later direction, which has never been
updated at the earlier query. The right uses the earlier direction,
which is updated exactly once before any later query. Summing the
adjacent left inequalities and using the right inequality with j=n
gives

    sum_(j>i) r_j^2 <= (h_i-1)r_i^2.                     (5.2)

Thus, for positive gradients, h_i>1 is necessary and the backward
innovation product in Section 2 is the equality case of a collection
of NECESSARY remote guards. Adding affine offsets does not remove
those guards. This is a constraint on distinct fresh directions, not
on arbitrary nonlinear adversaries.

For the shared-component law actually constructed, the same guards
remain after subtracting z times the intervening physical mass from
the height differences. Its exact payoff still depends on D and J.
It is not an independent orthogonal mixture whose normalized values
could simply be averaged.

## 6. One deterministic selector on every nonnegative word

On words with every h_i>1, use (2.1)-(2.3). On all other words use the
following explicit one-facet fallback, retaining all physical indices:

    S=sum h_i, d=1/(1+2S), p(y)=max(0,d y), x_0=1,
    f(x)=min_y {p(y)+(x-y)^2/2},
    x_i=1-d P_i, g_i=d,
    y_i=1-d(P_i+1), delta_i=d-d^2(P_i+1).                (6.1)

Because 0<=P_i<=S, y_i>=0. Therefore p(y_i)=delta_i and
g_i is a subgradient there, including all endpoints and zero steps.
The proof in Section 3 establishes every update and ordered row.
The minimum set is (-infinity,0], its true initial radius is one,
and its terminal payoff is exactly d. Empty or all-zero words present
no singularity. Arbitrarily large or unordered entries present none
in either branch. A single entry <=1 triggers this fallback; that
is a stated limitation of the selector, not a restriction of W.

Both branches now have explicit p, true initial squared radius N,
every envelope delta_i, and terminal gradient g_n. These are the
inputs to the fixed-coordinate realization next. This creates a
selector in the requested polyhedral class, rather than stopping at
an envelope representation.

## 7. A fixed extra coordinate with error paid against the original U_n

For either branch, and R>0, define the explicit polyhedron and objective

    K_R={(y,s): s>=p(y)/R},
    F_R(x)=dist((x,-R),K_R)^2/2-R^2/2.                    (7.1)

All plane slopes and R are fixed before the run. The extra coordinate
is ALWAYS -R. The algorithm updates only x. Its normalization is the
true original-coordinate distance N from Section 4 or (6.1); it is
never the ambient distance to (0,0).

Since p>=0, eliminating s gives the exact identity

    F_R(x)=min_y {||x-y||^2/2+p(y)+p(y)^2/(2R^2)}.        (7.2)

The function p+p^2/(2R^2) is finite and convex, since t+t^2/(2R^2)
is convex and increasing for t>=0. The proof in Section 3 therefore
proves that F_R is finite, convex, differentiable, and 1-smooth.
Equation (7.2) proves that its COMPLETE minimum set is still exactly
{p=0}, with value zero. Thus its true radius remains precisely N.

At every explicit envelope point x_i, strong convexity in the
minimized variable, and evaluation at y_i, give

    0<=F_R(x_i)-f(x_i)<=delta_i^2/(2R^2),
    ||grad F_R(x_i)-g_i||<=delta_i/R.                      (7.3)

For the second inequality, the original minimized expression exceeds
its minimum by at least ||y-y_i||^2/2, whereas the extra cost at y_i
is delta_i^2/(2R^2). The new minimizing proximal point is consequently
within delta_i/R of y_i.

For a convex 1-smooth function, I-h grad F is

    M(h)=max(1,|1-h|)

Lipschitz for every h>=0. Indeed, writing u=x-x' and
v=grad F(x)-grad F(x'), (3.2) gives <u,v>>=||v||^2 and
||v||<=||u||. Expanding ||u-hv||^2 gives the claimed bound separately
for 0<=h<=2 and h>=2. No upper bound on h is assumed.

Set the explicit finite expression

    E=sum_(t=0)^(n-1) h_t delta_t
                        product_(j=t+1)^(n-1) M(h_j).      (7.4)

It is zero for an empty word. Starting the F_R-run at the SAME x_0,
induction using (7.3) gives

    ||x_n^(R)-x_n||<=E/R.

Smoothness at x_n and (7.3) then give the complete terminal estimate

    |2F_R(x_n^(R))/N - V_z,b(h)|
       <= 2||g_n||E/(RN)+(delta_n+E)^2/(R^2N).             (7.5)

For the fallback replace V_z,b by its explicit payoff d. In particular
this estimate concerns the actual terminal after EVERY original step,
not the value at an unperturbed point alone.

The following completely explicit height rule pays the error relative
to the original all-split benchmark without executing that benchmark:

    eta_n=1/[1000(1+4n^2)],
    R=max{1, 4||g_n||E/(eta_n N),
                 sqrt(2)(delta_n+E)/sqrt(eta_n N)}.         (7.6)

All quantities on the right were selected from the given finite word.
They are finite even for huge steps. Equation (7.5) is at most eta_n.
The accepted crude bound B_n<=1+4n^p<=1+4n^2, with B_0=1, gives

    eta_n <= U_n^ZJ/1000.                                 (7.7)

Thus the approximation error is uniformly paid in the original units.
R itself need not be uniform in h, because the adversary is allowed
to depend on h. There is no hidden interchange of a schedule supremum
and a large-R limit.

### 7.1 Exact original queries of F_R, without a feasible-set oracle

The bound above already proves its actual trajectory is controlled.
For additional explicitness, every F_R query has this finite exact
evaluation rule on the KNOWN polyhedron (7.1).

For nonzero facet slopes a_i of p, form row normals in the ambient
space

    n_*=(0,-1), n_i=(a_i,-R).

At q=(x,-R), enumerate all linearly independent subsets I of these
known rows, including the empty subset. For nonempty I put

    lambda_I=(N_I N_I^T)^(-1) N_I q,
    u_I=q-N_I^T lambda_I.

For the empty subset put u_I=q. Retain I exactly when lambda_I>=0
and EVERY original row satisfies <n_j,u_I><=0. Take the first
retained subset in a fixed lexicographic order. Then

    grad F_R(x)=x-(u_I)_horizontal,
    F_R(x)=(||q-u_I||^2-R^2)/2.                            (7.8)

This is finite enumeration over explicitly known row subsets, not
optimization of unknown interpolation data. No such enumeration or
trajectory has been executed in this invocation.

Here is why the rule exists and checks every support. Projection onto
the nonempty closed cone K_R has a unique minimizer u. Its residual
q-u lies in the cone of active row normals. To see this directly, a
separating direction if it did not would have nonpositive product
with every active normal and positive product with q-u. A sufficiently
small step in that direction retains every inactive strict inequality
as well and decreases the squared distance, a contradiction. The
finitely generated cone is closed: remove linear dependences from
nonnegative representations until the positive generators are
independent, and then use one of finitely many independent subsets
to take limits. The same elimination supplies an independent active
subset representing q-u with nonnegative coefficients. Its equations
are exactly (7.8). Conversely every retained candidate is feasible,
and its displayed nonnegative normal representation proves projection
optimality by expanding squared distances to any feasible point.

Starting from the specified x_0 and using (7.8) at each ORIGINAL t,
the literal update is x_(t+1)^(R)=x_t^(R)-h_t grad F_R(x_t^(R)).
Its last coordinate never changes. Ties, dependent rows and zero steps
are handled by the rule. For every pair of its actual samples, including
all minima and its terminal, the full smooth-convex interpolation
inequality also follows directly. For clarity, for any convex 1-smooth
F, the function F(x)-<grad F(x_i),x> is minimized at x_i. Applying
the descent inequality at x_j to this function yields

    F(x_i)-F(x_j)+<grad F(x_i),x_j-x_i>
               +||grad F(x_i)-grad F(x_j)||^2/2<=0.

Thus no remote row is assumed from a partial list, for the exact
envelope or for its fixed-coordinate realization.

## 8. The entire SAME-enclosing-m hierarchy

Fix m>=1536 ONCE and put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/(4m),
    c=lambda(1+1/(4m)), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a c^l,H_l), 0<=l<m,
    n_m=2^m-1, S_l=sum H_l, X_l=S_l+1.

Every child uses this SAME enclosing m. All steps are strictly greater
than one, and the actual first and last steps are 1+a. Since
sqrt(2)<17/12 and m>=8,

    2<c<(29/12)(33/32)=319/128<5/2,
    0<a<1/2,
    c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta.

Writing v_h=a/(c-2), these imply 0<v_h<1, and direct substitution in
X_(l+1)=2X_l+a c^l gives

    X_l=v_h c^l+(1-v_h)2^l<c^l, l>=1.                    (8.1)

There are exactly 2^(m-1) level-zero entries 1+a. Each contributes
(1+a)/a>3 to the innovation product; all other factors h/(h-1)
are greater than one. Excluding the first entry, the formula for r_0
therefore gives

    D>=h_0^2 r_0^2 > 3^(2^(m-1)-1).                      (8.2)

For every m>=5, 2^(m-1)-1>=2m, as is proved at m=5 and then by
induction. Hence, at every present m,

    D>3^(2m)>(5/2)^(2m)>c^(2m)>(S_m+1)^2.              (8.3)

This bound uses the full physical multiplicity of the smallest level;
it does not delete any other level, select a prefix, or replace the
enclosing-m hierarchy by an unmodified silver word.

## 9. Exact whole-law obstruction, including terminal heights

Return to any word with all h_i>1 and let T=2S+1. Subtracting T times
the numerator of (4.3) from its denominator gives the EXACT identity

    N-T[z+1+2b(1+zJ)]
      =J(1+zJ)(b-S/J)^2+D-T-S^2/J.                       (9.1)

For the hierarchy, J>=1 and (8.3) imply

    D-T-S^2/J >= D-(S+1)^2 >0.

Consequently, for EVERY finite z>=0 and EVERY b>=0,

    V_z,b(H_m)<1/(2S_m+1).                               (9.2)

The supremum is exactly that value, not merely bounded by it. Indeed
fix b=S_m/J and send z to infinity in the explicit scalar expression
(4.3). Its leading numerator is z(2S_m+1) and its leading denominator
is z(2S_m+1)^2. Therefore

    sup_(z>=0,b>=0) V_z,b(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).                (9.3)

This proves that changing the common component or paying any terminal
height available in THIS law cannot improve its canonical channel.
It does not bound an arbitrary common-plus-fresh-direction transcript
with a different slope/height law, signed nonlocal memory, or any other
static function family.

The zero-common-component subfamily has a stronger quantitative failure.
Let Q=T_0=product_(i<n) h_i/(h_i-1). Since its true radius is at least

    sum_(i<n) r_i^2+(r_n+b/r_n)^2=Q-1+(1+b)^2,

and its terminal numerator is 1+2b, elementary one-variable algebra
gives

    V_0,b <= 2/[1+sqrt(4Q-3)], for every b>=0.             (9.4)

For example, maximizing (2t-1)/(Q-1+t^2), t=1+b>=1,
sets t=(1+sqrt(4Q-3))/2. On H_m, Q>3^(2^(m-1)), so

    sup_b V_0,b < 2*3^(-2^(m-2)).                         (9.5)

Thus this is a terminal-value obstruction, not just a remark about
growing dimension. Adding the common component can recover the Huber
scale but, by (9.3), cannot exceed it on the diagnostic hierarchy.

## 10. The full canonical/cut channel does not repair this law

For completeness the accepted channel is

    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S),
      max_j (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)]},

where here T_j denotes the PHYSICAL suffix mass, not an innovation tail.
We establish its whole-hierarchy value afresh to avoid importing the
unverified shared draft's comparison.

At any physical level-l occurrence, both adjacent complete children
are H_l. Put d_l=a c^l, p=P_j+1, t=T_j+1. Then p,t>=X_l and
the cut-to-Huber ratio is exactly

    (d_l/p)^2 [1+2(p+d_l)/(2t-1)].                         (10.1)

For fixed t,d_l the expression decreases in p: expanding it gives
positive multiples of 1/p and 1/p^2. For fixed p it decreases in t.
From (8.1), d_l<=(c-2)X_l; set d_h=c-2<1/2. At p=t=X_l,
using X_l>=1, (10.1) is at most

    d_h^2 [1+2(1+d_h)X_l/(2X_l-1)]
       <=d_h^2(3+2d_h)<1.                                (10.2)

This checks EVERY physical cut, not just a selected pair. Thus

    K_(n_m)(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).                              (10.3)

Let V_R(h;z,b) be the actual normalized terminal value of the
fixed-coordinate F_R with the prescribed R in (7.6). Equations
(7.5), (9.2), and (10.3) imply uniformly in its parameters

    max{K_(n_m)(H_m), sup_(z,b>=0) V_R(H_m;z,b)}
       <=1/(2S_m+1)+eta_(n_m).                            (10.4)

The exact accepted SAME-m benchmark comparison in Section 1 and
(7.7) therefore give, for EVERY m>=1536,

    B_(n_m) max{K_(n_m)(H_m), sup_(z,b>=0) V_R(H_m;z,b)}
       <237/250+1/1000=949/1000.                           (10.5)

In particular this applies to the entirely deterministic selector
(2.1), not just to a restricted choice of its parameters. The actual
last step 1+a<3/2 was present in the trajectory proof, the radius,
the mass S_m, and the perturbation expression (7.4). It was never
replaced by a large checkpoint or removed for terminal evaluation.

Choosing epsilon=1/25 gives 1-epsilon=24/25>949/1000. For any proposed
word-independent threshold there is m>=1536 with n_m beyond it.
Equation (10.5) disproves the desired lower comparison for THIS explicit
selector and its entire displayed parameter family, even with every
canonical single-cut witness included. It says nothing about an upper
bound on W_(n_m)(H_m).

## 11. What has and has not been proved

Proved together in this unverified draft:

* A direct formula from the complete physical word to a growing,
  noncollinear coupled facet dictionary and a legal initialized GD run.
* Every current, old, future, zero and optimum support/interpolation
  constraint, including the unqueried actual terminal.
* The complete minimum set and exact true initial distance.
* An explicit fixed-extra-coordinate polyhedral realization, a finite
  exact query rule, and a word-uniform error budget <=U_n^ZJ/1000.
* A whole-law terminal obstruction on every SAME-m hierarchy with
  m>=1536, uniformly in the common strength and terminal height.

Not proved: any improvement over the full canonical/cut channel on that
hierarchy, any all-word asymptotic lower bound for unrestricted W, or any
full-class upper certificate for an improving schedule. In particular no
missing selection/comparison theorem is renamed a routine interpolation
lemma. The explicit selector fails its required comparison by (10.5).

The failure has a precise mechanism: forward activation of a new
orthogonal innovation while retaining all earlier planes forces the
backward product (5.2). A single shared gradient direction can dilute
that cost enough to reach the Huber scale in a limit, but the exact
radius/terminal identity (9.1) prevents it from doing more on these
physical words. This is a scoped obstruction to the displayed law,
not a general impossibility theorem for polyhedral fixed-coordinate
adversaries.

Resource scope: ONE direct analytical invocation, ONE substantive
candidate mechanism, ZERO mathematical programs, ZERO numerical or
arithmetic replays, ZERO executed trajectories/recurrences, ZERO
optimizers/solvers/benchmark-DP executions, ZERO formal calls or fact
submissions, ZERO installations/paid consults/additional agents, and
ZERO public searches. Only public-file text retrieval and writing this
draft/handoff were performed. Accepted facts, the original direct
draft, services, workers and truth stores remain unchanged.

# Direct polyhedral selector — bounded handoff

**UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN. No verifier submission is allocated.**

Artifact: `DRAFT_UNVERIFIED.md` in this directory.

One mechanism was examined: a single coupled maximum of n+1 explicitly
selected slopes `g_i=sqrt(z)e_0+r_i e_(i+1)`, with backward innovation
sizes fixed from the whole word by

    r_n=1,
    r_i^2=product_(i<t<n)[h_t/(h_t-1)]/(h_i-1).

The new selector is completely explicit on words with h_i>1, with an
explicit canonical one-facet fallback on all other nonnegative words.
The deterministic common strength and terminal-height parameters are
`z=D`, `b=S/J`; the draft also proves a uniform obstruction over EVERY
`z,b>=0` in this same displayed law. The pure fresh-coordinate case is
a subfamily, not a second mechanism. No second candidate was pursued.

Complete proofs supplied together: every original GD query and every
remote affine support; all directed and optimum interpolation rows;
global convexity and 1-smoothness; complete minimum set and unique
closest minimizer; exact radius and actual terminal value; and a
fixed-extra-coordinate polyhedron with explicit height R and normalized
error at most `U_n^ZJ/1000`. A finite exact query rule for that KNOWN
polyhedron is given without executing it. The extra coordinate remains
fixed and the radius is always measured in the original variables.

The decisive identity is

    N-(2S+1)[z+1+2b(1+zJ)]
      =J(1+zJ)(b-S/J)^2+D-(2S+1)-S^2/J.

On the ENTIRE SAME-enclosing-m H_(m,1/4), m>=1536, the original level
multiplicities force `D>(S_m+1)^2`. Consequently the exact envelope
family's payoff supremum is precisely `1/(2S_m+1)`; every finite
parameter pair is strictly below it. The zero-common-component
subfamily is bounded by `2*3^(-2^(m-2))`. Both statements include all
levels and the actual last step `1+a`.

Including every canonical/single-cut witness and the prescribed
fixed-coordinate approximation gives the WHOLE-LAW obstruction

    B_(2^m-1) * portfolio(H_(m,1/4)) < 949/1000,
    for EVERY m>=1536.

Thus this law does not improve the supplied canonical/cut channel on
the discriminator and cannot prove the original uniform lower bound.
This is no upper bound on unrestricted W and no improving-schedule
certificate. No remaining theorem-level assertion is disguised as a
routine lemma: the displayed law fails, and the original all-word
selection/comparison remains unresolved.

Accepted dependencies, exact scope: b98bb807d514e80d for original
benchmark definitions, K_n, and the crude B_n upper bound;
5fd396eba75651e7 ONLY for the SAME-m hierarchy definition and Section
10's scalar `B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250`. The interpolation and earlier
direct/shared drafts were read but are not correctness premises.

Resource counts: one direct analytical invocation; one candidate
mechanism; zero mathematical programs, numerical/arithmetic replays,
trajectory/recurrence executions, optimizers/solvers/benchmark-DP
executions, formal calls/fact submissions, installations, paid consults,
additional agents, or public searches. Only public text retrieval and
these two new files were used. No accepted fact, worker, service, truth
store, original direct draft, or Goal status was changed.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000546 -->
# Independent adversarial audit: finite-polytope epigraph lift

**Status: `no_gap_found_at_declared_scope`; independent analytical audit only, UNVERIFIED.**

This is the complete result of ONE bounded direct Codex audit allocation for
`gd-exact-profile-20260926`. It is not a verifier acceptance, a new project fact,
or a resolution of the Zhang--Jiang minimax target. The original target remains
OPEN. The allocation ends with these audit artifacts and does not renew itself.

The frozen source audited was `DRAFT_UNVERIFIED.md` in this directory, 11407
bytes, with actual SHA256
`092bbe1d0717124238040e2c4e76ba0d3bce022595f774d2c58cb1c945837275`.
Its contents were read completely; the source was not edited.

## 1. Verdict and precise scope

I found no fatal mathematical gap in the stated finite-run approximation or
the resulting equality of suprema. In particular, the claimed perturbation
inequality has the correct sign, the vertical coordinate really follows GD,
the chosen nearby point is a genuine minimizer, and the denominator comparison
uses the lifted function's own minimum set.

The conclusion is the following normalized statement. Fix a finite integer
`n >= 0`, a finite nonnegative word `h`, and an actual run of a globally convex
differentiable 1-smooth function with a chosen minimizer at zero. Each positive
normalized terminal score can be approached from below by scores from functions

    F_D(z) = max_{v in D} { <z,v> - ||v||^2/2 },

where `D` is a finite compact convex polytope containing zero, in one extra
dimension, and the same unmodified GD word is applied. A different `D` and
starting point may be used for each requested accuracy. The maximization class
must permit the whole minimum cone and arbitrary selected minimizers in it.
Consequently the unrestricted and this polytope-restricted worst-case
SUPREMA agree for each fixed word.

This conclusion does not assert finite-parameter exact interpolation by
`F_D`, attainment within the polytope class, uniform approximation over words,
or a method to select a hard original run from the word. It gives no bound
against the Zhang--Jiang profile. The full derivation below is independent of
the source's favorable conclusions.

## 2. Interpolation anchors: signs and exact identities

Use the original samples `i=0,...,n`, including the terminal sample, and the
optimum sample `*` with `(x_*,g_*,f_*)=(0,0,0)`. The only accepted fact used is
the necessity of the ordered interpolation inequalities in local fact
`1252b8823bb5a290`:

    Q_ij = f_i-f_j + <g_i,x_j-x_i> + ||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0.

The local fact file was read, and its exact verification record has
`verdict="correct"`, `write_error=null`, record ID `5480845b8a751e16`.
No other part of that large fact, including its SDP or attainment statements,
is needed here.

For completeness, the required necessity has a direct short proof. For fixed
`i`, the function `k(x)=f(x)-<g_i,x>` is convex and 1-smooth and has a minimum
at `x_i`. The segment-integral smoothness estimate at `x_j`, with step
`-(g_j-g_i)`, gives

    k(x_i) <= k(x_j-(g_j-g_i))
           <= k(x_j) - ||g_j-g_i||^2/2.

Rearranging gives exactly the stated orientation of `Q_ij`.

Put

    delta_i = f_i-||g_i||^2/2,
    ell_i(g) = <x_i-g_i,g> - delta_i,
    p(g) = max(0,ell_0(g),...,ell_n(g)),
    K = conv{0,g_0,...,g_n}.

The two optimum rows have different roles:

    Q_*i <= 0  gives  delta_i >= 0,
    Q_i* <= 0  gives  ell_i(g_i) >= 0.

Thus `p(0)=0`, while direct expansion, with no change of pair orientation,
gives

    ell_j(g_i)-ell_i(g_i)
      = f_i-f_j + <g_i,x_j-x_i> + ||g_i-g_j||^2/2
      = Q_ij <= 0.

It follows that `p(g_i)=ell_i(g_i)` for every sample, including `i=n`.
The nonnegative maximum with zero does not spoil this equality precisely
because the outgoing optimum row gives `ell_i(g_i)>=0`.

For any `g in K`, the active-anchor inequality `p(g)>=ell_i(g)` gives

    <x_i,g> - ||g||^2/2 - p(g)
      <= f_i - ||g-g_i||^2/2.                         (2.1)

At `g=g_i` equality holds. Therefore the finite dual interpolant

    phi(x) = max_{g in K} { <x,g>-||g||^2/2-p(g) }

has value `f_i` and unique maximizer `g_i` at `x_i`. It will follow below that
this maximizer is its gradient. Since `0 in K` and `p(0)=0`, `phi>=0`;
at zero all the maximized expressions are nonpositive, so `phi(0)=0`.

Every element of `K` is a convex combination of its listed generators. With

    M = max_i ||g_i||,
    Z = max(0,p(g_0),...,p(g_n)),

convexity of `p` gives `0<=p(g)<=Z` on `K`, and the triangle inequality gives
`||g||<=M`. This uses finite known values, not a hidden unbounded maximization.
Maximizing the difference of the affine expressions also gives

    |phi(x)-phi(y)| <= M ||x-y||.                    (2.2)

## 3. Smoothness with a compact, possibly degenerate dual domain

Here is an elementary argument that avoids assumptions of differentiability
of `p`, full dimension of `K`, or interior optimality. Write

    A_x(g)=<x,g>-||g||^2/2-p(g),
    a(x)=argmax_{g in K} A_x(g).

Continuity and compactness give existence, and strict concavity gives
uniqueness. Strong concavity on a line segment from the maximizer to any
`b in K`, followed by division by the segment parameter and its limit to zero,
gives

    A_x(a(x))-A_x(b) >= ||a(x)-b||^2/2.              (3.1)

This argument works at a boundary or in a lower dimensional affine hull.
Use (3.1) for `(x,a(y))` and `(y,a(x))`, and add. The result is

    <x-y,a(x)-a(y)> >= ||a(x)-a(y)||^2.              (3.2)

Thus `a` is firmly nonexpansive and in particular 1-Lipschitz. Evaluating
each maximum at the other point's maximizer gives

    0 <= phi(y)-phi(x)-<a(x),y-x>
      <= <a(y)-a(x),y-x> <= ||y-x||^2.

Consequently `phi` is differentiable with `grad phi=a`, and is globally
1-smooth. Its convexity follows directly from the maximum of affine functions
in `x`. Equation (2.1) now proves both exact values and exact gradients at
ALL the original samples. Induction in the ordinary GD recurrence therefore
reproduces the original finite run for `phi`.

The same elementary argument, with `p=0` and any compact convex domain `D`,
proves differentiability and 1-smoothness of `F_D`. Completing the square in
its defining maximum identifies its unique maximizing vector as `P_D(z)`:

    F_D(z)=||z||^2/2-dist(z,D)^2/2,
    grad F_D(z)=P_D(z).

Convexity here comes from the affine maximum, not from an unjustified claim
that a difference of two convex functions is convex.

## 4. Finite polytope, complete minimum set, and nearby minimizer

For every `R>0`, set

    D_R = { (g,u): g in K, p(g)/R <= u <= Z/R }.

The lower and upper bounds are compatible because `p<=Z` on `K`.
The set is nonempty, closed, bounded, convex, and contains `(0,0)`.
For an explicit finite-polytope justification, start from the finite convex
hull `K x [0,Z/R]`, and intersect successively with the finitely many
halfspaces

    R u >= ell_i(g),  i=0,...,n.

The condition `u>=0` is already imposed by the product. Cutting a finite
convex hull by a halfspace leaves a finite convex hull: retain its feasible
generators and add intersections with the cutting hyperplane of segments
joining feasible and infeasible generators. One can see this by pairing the
positive and negative signed contributions to the hyperplane inequality in
a convex combination. Repeating the finite operation proves the claim
without a full-dimensionality hypothesis. When `Z=0`, the result is exactly
`K x {0}` because `p=0` throughout `K`. If `K={0}`, it is simply `{(0,0)}`.

For any compact convex `D` containing zero, the entire minimum set of `F_D`
is the cone

    C_D = { z : <z,v> <= 0 for all v in D }.          (4.1)

This is the zero-threshold polar CONE, not the body polar with threshold one.
Indeed, `F_D>=0`, and membership in this cone makes all the candidate values
nonpositive. Conversely, if `<z,v> > 0` for some `v in D`, then `v!=0` and
`tau v in D` for `0<=tau<=1`. For sufficiently small positive `tau`,

    <z,tau v>-||tau v||^2/2
      = tau <z,v> - tau^2 ||v||^2/2 > 0.

Hence no such `z` can be a minimizer. This proves (4.1) in both directions.

All elements of `D_R` have `u>=0`, so the fixed point

    z_star=(0,-R)

belongs to `C_{D_R}`. For `z_0=(x_0,-R)`, the exact initial distance to this
actual minimizer is `||z_0-z_star||=||x_0||`. A large coordinate has not been
charged to the denominator. The proof does not replace the actual minimum
set by the singleton `{0}`.

## 5. Actual GD dynamics and the moving added coordinate

At a point `(X,-Rt)` with `t>=1`, for fixed `g` the derivative of the objective
with respect to `u>=0` is `-Rt-u<0`. The optimizing `u` is therefore exactly
`p(g)/R`. The maximization becomes

    F_R(X,-Rt)
      = max_{g in K} { A_X(g) - d_{R,t}(g) },
    d_{R,t}(g) = (t-1)p(g) + p(g)^2/(2R^2).

Let its maximizing horizontal coordinate be `b_R(X,t)`. Uniqueness also
follows from uniqueness of the full projection onto `D_R`. Its full gradient
is

    ( b_R(X,t), p(b_R(X,t))/R ).

Starting with `X_0=x_0`, `t_0=1`, the SAME ordinary GD word gives

    X_{i+1}=X_i-h_i b_R(X_i,t_i),
    t_{i+1}=t_i+h_i p(b_R(X_i,t_i))/R^2.              (5.1)

The plus sign in the second equation is required: the vertical position
becomes more negative. If `S=sum_{i<n} h_i`, induction using `h_i>=0` gives

    1 <= t_i <= 1+SZ/R^2,  0<=i<=n.                 (5.2)

This proves at the same time that the region `t>=1` used to eliminate `u`
contains every actual iterate; there is no circular fixed-slice assumption.
The absolute displacement of the added coordinate from its INITIAL value is
at most `SZ/R`, not zero. It vanishes as `R` grows for this fixed run.

No projection step is substituted for GD: projection is the gradient of the
single static function `F_R`, and the algorithm still subtracts `h_i` times
that full gradient. Neither the word nor the body changes during the run.

## 6. Independent whole-run error derivation

On every visited `t_i`, (5.2) and `0<=p<=Z` give the uniform bound

    0 <= d_{R,t_i}(g) <= Delta_R := (S+1/2)Z^2/R^2,
    for every g in K.                               (6.1)

Fix such a visited pair `(X,t)`, put `a=grad phi(X)` and `b=b_R(X,t)`.
Optimality in the PERTURBED maximum gives

    A_X(b)-d(b) >= A_X(a)-d(a).

Combining this with (3.1) yields, with the indicated sign,

    ||a-b||^2/2 <= A_X(a)-A_X(b)
                <= d(a)-d(b) <= Delta_R.            (6.2)

In particular, the source does not need the looser bound `2 Delta_R` on the
right: the nonnegative perturbation lies in `[0,Delta_R]`, whose oscillation
is at most `Delta_R`. With `A=Z sqrt(2S+1)`, (6.2) gives

    ||b_R(X_i,t_i)-grad phi(X_i)|| <= A/R.

Let `E_i=||X_i-x_i||` and `Pi_i=product_{j<i}(1+h_j)`. Using (5.1), the original
GD update, interpolation of `g_i`, and 1-Lipschitz continuity of `grad phi`,

    E_{i+1} <= (1+h_i)E_i + h_i A/R,
    E_0=0.

Induction, including `h_i=0`, gives exactly

    E_i <= (A/R)(Pi_i-1).                           (6.3)

In fact the full iterates, after subtracting the nearby minimizer, satisfy

    ||(z_i-z_star)-(x_i,0)|| <= (A/R)(Pi_i-1)+SZ/R.

Thus the moving extra coordinate also converges to zero in the translated
trajectory; there is no hidden order-one displacement in that direction.

Taking maxima in (6.1) gives

    phi(X_i)-Delta_R <= F_R(z_i) <= phi(X_i).

Use (2.2), (6.3), and `phi(x_i)=f_i` to obtain

    |F_R(z_i)-f_i|
       <= M A(Pi_i-1)/R + Delta_R.

At the actual terminal iterate this is exactly the source's estimate

    e_R = M Z sqrt(2S+1)(Pi_n-1)/R
          + (S+1/2)Z^2/R^2.                        (6.4)

All constants are finite for each fixed finite actual run and each finite
word, however large its entries. Given `epsilon>0`, write

    C=M Z sqrt(2S+1)(Pi_n-1),  E=(S+1/2)Z^2.

Then `R>=max(1,2C/epsilon,sqrt(2E/epsilon))` makes each term of (6.4) at most
`epsilon/2`. This also works when either or both constants vanish. It proves
convergence without invoking an asymptotic interchange of unbounded words.

## 7. Own closest radius and equality of suprema

Set `r=||x_0||>0`. If `f_n>0`, choose `R` with `e_R<f_n`, so `F_R(z_n)>0`.
If `z_0` were a minimizer, its gradient would be zero and every GD step would
leave it fixed, for ANY finite prescribed steps. That would give terminal
value zero, a contradiction. Thus `z_0` is outside the closed cone `C_{D_R}`.

The distance

    r_R=dist(z_0,C_{D_R})

is positive. A closest point exists: zero belongs to the closed set, so a
minimizing sequence can be confined to a bounded ball, and finite-dimensional
compactness gives a minimizing limit. Because `z_star` is another member,

    0 < r_R <= r.

The lower numerator `f_n-e_R` is strictly positive. This sign matters when
comparing denominators, and it gives

    2 F_R(z_n)/r_R^2
      >= 2(f_n-e_R)/r_R^2
      >= 2(f_n-e_R)/r^2.                            (7.1)

Choosing `e_R<min(f_n,eta r^2/2)` makes (7.1) exceed
`2f_n/r^2-eta`. No claim that `r_R` equals or tends to `r` is needed.
Indeed, the normalized scores themselves need not converge to the original
score; the one-sided comparison is the correct assertion. One could already
obtain the required comparison using the selected minimizer `z_star`; the
own-closest comparison is a valid strengthening.

For `f_n=0`, the original score is zero, and the restricted supremum is
nonnegative. No ratio with `r_R=0` is necessary. In the original definition,
a start that is itself a minimizer but differs from the selected minimizer
also has score zero and causes no problem.

For precision, at smoothness one define the restricted supremum over every
finite dimension, every compact convex finite polytope `D` containing zero,
every `z_* in C_D`, and every `z_0!=z_*`. Every such `F_D` is a legal original
instance, so `W_n^poly(h)<=W_n(h)`. Conversely, (7.1) applies to each original
positive-score instance and each `eta>0`; its lifted instance is among the
restricted choices. Taking the two suprema yields

    W_n^poly(h)=W_n(h).

The quantifier order used is

    for every finite n and finite h>=0,
    for every original actual instance and every eta>0,
    there exists a finite R and one admissible D_R.

It is NOT a single choice of `R` for all instances or all words. Equality is
pointwise in `h`; no interchange of `inf_h` and an approximation limit is
used. Taking `inf_h` AFTER this equality gives equality of the corresponding
minimax formulations, but supplies no numerical or asymptotic bound for them.

There is also no hidden infinite-score issue. For any original instance,
Lipschitz continuity of the gradient at a chosen minimizer gives
`||x_i-x_*||<=Pi_i||x_0-x_*||`; smoothness at that minimizer then gives
`2(f_n-f_*)<=||x_n-x_*||^2`. Thus `W_n(h)<=Pi_n^2<infinity` at smoothness one.
The same bound applies to each lifted function with ITS closest minimizer.

## 8. Normalization and boundary cases

For an original `L>0`, chosen minimizer `x_*`, and minimum value `f_*`, apply
the preceding argument to

    tilde f(x)=(f(x_*+x)-f_*)/L.

Its gradient is `grad f(x_*+x)/L`, so its step `h_i` is exactly the original
step `h_i/L` after translation. Restore scale by using `L F_D` in the
original problem: its gradient is `L P_D`, and the update `h_i/L` again
gives the same lifted run. Its score is

    2[L F_D(z_n)]/[L ||z_0-z_*||^2]
      =2F_D(z_n)/||z_0-z_*||^2.

Accordingly, for general `L` the restricted functions are `L F_D`. A bare
`F_D` has gradient `P_D` and belongs to the normalized `L=1` statement.
The draft's final scaling paragraph supplies this qualification; it should
remain explicit in any standalone theorem statement.

The following cases require no additional hypothesis:

- `n=0`: `S=0`, `Pi_0=1`, horizontal error is zero and the value error is at
  most `Z^2/(2R^2)`. The single sampled point is also the terminal point.
- All steps zero: positions and `t_i` stay fixed; repeated sample anchors are
  harmless. The same estimate applies.
- Some zero steps: (5.1) and (6.3) simply leave the corresponding errors
  unchanged; no strict-Slater or distinct-point condition appears.
- Arbitrarily huge but finite steps: `S` and `Pi_n` may be very large, but
  remain finite, and the explicit choice of `R` remains valid.
- `Z=0`: convexity and nonnegativity force `p=0` throughout `K`; the lifted
  body is `K x {0}`, every perturbation is zero, and the run is exact.
- `M=0`: all sample gradients vanish. Each actual sampled point is a
  minimizer, all sample values are zero, and `D_R={0}`. The zero-score clause
  handles the closest-radius degeneracy.
- Lower dimensional `K` or `D_R`: all uniqueness and concavity arguments
  are in the ambient Euclidean norm restricted to a convex set, so no
  affine-span interior assumption is used.

Negative steps and an infinite horizon are outside the declared scope.
The present proof specifically uses nonnegativity to maintain `t_i>=1`.

## 9. Positive slack: analytic structural attacks

For `v=P_D(z)`, the defining maximum gives

    F_D(z)=<z,v>-||v||^2/2.

At any minimizer `a in C_D`, the outgoing optimum slack is therefore

    <z-a,v>-F_D(z)-||v||^2/2 = -<a,v> >= 0.          (9.1)

It is zero at `a=0`, but need not be zero at another minimizer. At the
specific nearby `z_star=(0,-R)`, use the exact second gradient component
from Section 5 to get

    -<z_star,grad F_R(z_i)>=p(b_R(X_i,t_i)).          (9.2)

The positive slack is thus retained exactly as stated. Moreover, `p` is
Lipschitz with constant `B=max_i||x_i-g_i||`, since it is a finite maximum
of affine functions with those slopes and the zero function. From (6.3),

    ||b_R(X_i,t_i)-g_i|| <= A Pi_i/R,
    |p(b_R(X_i,t_i))-p(g_i)| <= B A Pi_i/R.

Whenever the original outgoing optimum slack `p(g_i)` is positive, it stays
positive at this nearby minimizer for all sufficiently large `R`.

Here is a fully analytic attack on the possibility that choosing the OWN
closest minimizer might secretly force slack to zero. Take `n=0`,

    f(x)=x^2/4,  x_0=1,  g_0=1/2,  f_0=1/4.

Then

    K=[0,1/2],
    p(g)=max(0,g/2-1/8),
    Z=1/8.

The body lies in the nonnegative quadrant and contains nonzero horizontal
and vertical axis segments: `(g,0)` for `0<=g<=1/4` and `(0,u)` for
`0<=u<=1/(8R)`. Consequently its full polar cone is exactly the negative
quadrant. For `z_0=(1,-R)`, its unique closest minimizer is `(0,-R)`, at
distance one.

On the active segment `1/4<g<1/2`, the reduced objective derivative is

    1/2-g-(g/2-1/8)/(2R^2).

It is positive at the left endpoint and negative at the right endpoint.
Solving its linear zero gives the actual horizontal projection

    g_R=(8R^2+1)/[4(4R^2+1)],
    p(g_R)=R^2/[2(4R^2+1)] > 0.

Both inequalities `1/4<g_R<1/2` hold for every `R>0`, so this branch check
is exact. Direct substitution, using (2.1) with equality on that segment,
gives

    F_R(1,-R)=1/4-1/[32(4R^2+1)].

Thus the own-closest normalized score tends to `1/2`, while the outgoing
optimum slack at that OWN closest minimizer tends to `1/8`, not to zero.
This example supports the declared distinction; it is not a counterexample
to the reduction. All formulas in this example were derived analytically,
with no arithmetic program or solver.

A second structural test is `f(x)=||x||^2/2` under ANY finite word, including
overshooting words. Here `x_i=g_i`, every `delta_i=0`, every anchor is zero,
and `Z=0`. The resulting body `K x {0}` reproduces the entire finite run
exactly because it contains every `g_i=x_i`. This confirms that the
zero-height degeneration is compatible with large-step trajectories.

Finally, if `0` is in the relative interior of `D`, every vector in its polar
cone is orthogonal to `span(D)`: small positive and negative multiples of
every direction in that span occur in `D`, forcing both polar inequalities.
Equation (9.1) is then zero for EVERY minimizer. The positive-slack example
shows why the present construction cannot simply be declared a reduction to
that restricted representation. It does not disprove some separate equality
of suprema for a different restricted family; it establishes that such a
claim is absent from, and unsupported by, this proof.

## 10. Limitations, editorial qualifications, and resources

No repair is required for the stated theorem at its declared scope. To avoid
future scope inflation, a standalone version should retain these details:

1. Use `L F_D` at general smoothness `L`; reserve `grad F_D=P_D` for the
   normalized statement.
2. Define the polar by the explicit `<=0` inequalities and allow all its
   minimizers in the normalization.
3. State approximation and equality of suprema, not exact finite-`R`
   representation or attainment in the restricted class.
4. Preserve instance-dependent `R` and the factor `Pi_n`; there is no uniform
   word-independent error constant.
5. Keep positive outgoing-optimum slack about the nearby minimizer. The
   construction depends on the original adverse run and does not select one
   from `h` or establish any target-profile lower bound.

Resource accounting for this allocation:

- One direct native audit invocation; allocation requests `gpt-6-astra` with
  `ultra` reasoning. Actual serving-backend metadata is not exposed within
  this audit; the parent dispatch owns that execution receipt.
- Zero subagents, paid consults, mathematical programs, arithmetic replays,
  solver calls, formal verification calls, or `fact_submit` calls.
- Only read-only file inspection, source hash/length inspection, and writing
  `AUDIT_UNVERIFIED.md` plus `HANDOFF.md` were performed.
- Initialization marker, `OPERATOR.md`, `AGENTS.md`, project `PROBLEM.md`,
  `ALLOCATION.json`, the entire frozen draft, the accepted interpolation fact,
  and its exact shared verification metadata were read. A lightweight memory
  registry pass supplied only historical scope context; current authorization
  and target scope were checked in the live project files.
- The applicable `danus-research` skill and lifecycle reference were read.
  No external literature theorem was used and no web request was made.
- No worker-private memory was read; no worker, service, truth store, source
  draft, allocation limit, or finalization state was changed.

The mathematical assessment is `no_gap_found_at_declared_scope`, not
`verified` or `accepted`. The finite-run projection-polytope reduction is a
candidate for a separately authorized verifier submission. The original
exact-profile minimax theorem remains OPEN. This allocation is complete.
